# Passenger Agent OS — Progress

Append-only worklog. Every agent adds an entry when it finishes a task — what shipped, what it learned, what it left behind. **Newest first — insert your entry at the top, never append at end-of-file** (L-003). `BOARD.md` holds current state; this holds history.

Entry format:

```
### YYYY-MM-DD — <agent> — <task id> <short title>
- **Did:** what actually changed (files, migrations, issue IDs)
- **Evidence:** build/test output, screenshots, review verdicts
- **Left behind:** follow-ups, known gaps, anything the next agent needs
```

### 2026-08-03 — product — T-049/PAS-37 ruling: bundled-vs-fetched `place_type` registry — **amend T-042 to match T-037 D4 (option 2)**

- **Did:** documentation-reconciliation ruling dispatched by `chief-of-staff` (round 4, relay). Read `BOARD.md` in full (T-037/T-042/T-049 rows), this file's recent worklog (no "Current Snapshot" section exists — the documented exception at `PROGRESS.md:830`), then **both disputed sections in full and their surrounding documents**: `prds/passport/TRD.md` §1 open-items table, §2.1, §3.3, §3.4/D4, §11 A1, §12; `prds/places-dataset/TRD.md` §2.2, §3.1, §3.3, §4.2, §4.4, §4.5, §5, §9, §10. Did **not** rule from the ticket description's summary of either.
- **Verdict — option 2. The registry is bundled; `GET /rest/v1/place_types` comes out of T-042's client fetch contract.** Three independent grounds, any one sufficient:
  1. **Scope ownership.** T-042 §2.2 assigns the client half of C-STICKER-1 to T-037 by name, and §9 states *"No `[iOS]` step exists in this task — every client-side consequence is another task's amendment."* §4.5's *"One extra small GET for `place_types` … fetched once per session alongside the places load"* is a client-runtime behaviour claim inside a document that says it specs no client behaviour. D4 governs by scope, not by which doc ships or gets referenced last.
  2. **T-042 already contradicts itself, and its own §4.2 sides with D4.** §4.2 defines the client half as *"a test asserting every `sticker_shape` present in the shipped `place-types.json` **export** resolves to a real asset."* A shipped export is a bundled build-time artifact — D4's design, written into T-042 three sections before §4.5 specs a runtime GET. Amending §4.5 makes T-042 self-consistent; amending D4 would leave T-042 contradicting itself and break §4.2's own falsifiable pass condition, which is only checkable against a bundled file.
  3. **D4's argument is unrebutted and nothing needs the GET.** A `sticker_shape` key is undrawable unless the binary ships the asset; an unknown key renders `.generic` however it arrived, so fetching buys an undrawable key plus a request, a cache field and a skew class. Both `data-engineer` and `code-reviewer` APPROVEd A1/A2 and flagged only the unreconciled-docs process gap, neither contested the reasoning. T-042 names no consumer of that endpoint: the pipeline runs authored source → validator → export → DB seed, so the DB is downstream of the mapping, never upstream of it.
- **Options 1 and 3 rejected explicitly, not by omission.** (1) "Intentionally different concerns" is not a coherent story here — one client, one registry, one mapping; "the server offers it and the client never calls it" is a dead endpoint documented as a contract, which is what gets built by whoever reads §4.5 without reading passport §3.4. (3) Phase-2-scoping is worse than doing nothing: D4 is explicitly *not* Build-Phase scoped (*"it holds permanently for V1"*), and deferring parks the contradiction at the precise moment nobody re-reads this ticket, handing the Phase-2 developer two approved docs that still disagree.
- **No code change, confirmed against source not assumed.** T-037's in-flight build is building D4 as written — A1 already landed (`passenger-code cc2b008`, `Passenger/Resources/place-types-tel-aviv.json`, six types, bundled). T-042 is HELD/undispatched. Nothing already shipped moves.
- **Second finding — new, not in `PAS-37`'s description: the bundled registry has no emitter, and until now had two names.** The shipped `place-types-tel-aviv.json` header reads *"Regenerate/replace, do not extend by hand"* — and **no generator exists that could regenerate it.** Verified directly: `database/scripts/` holds `build_hoods.py`, `export_hoods_geojson.py`, `validate_dataset.py`, `test_hood_containment_fixture.py` — no `export_places.py` (T-042 B3, unbuilt), no `places-tel-aviv.source.json`. T-042 §4.2 calls the artifact an "export," implying generated; T-042's build breakdown emits only `places-tel-aviv.json` + the DB seed. At Phase 2, when B1's vocabulary is ratified and `place_types` holds real rows, the bundle and the table can diverge with nothing detecting it, and whoever updates the bundle hand-edits a file the TRD calls an export — **L-024's exact failure mode, one phase away, with a hand-edit ban already printed in the file's own header and no way to obey it.** Reconciling §4.5 without closing this just moves the hole. Also the standing PRD data/schema rule doing its job: a bundled artifact with no named emitter is exactly the implicit data need that must be explicit before someone builds or sources it.
- **Amendment owed by `architect`** (product rules, architect writes the TRD — T-033's precedent: *"`product` made the call (`30e002f`), `architect` amended the TRD"*). T-042 → **v2**: (a) §4.5 remove the `place_types` GET and the "fetched once per session" sentence, replace with the bundled statement linking `passport/TRD.md` §3.4/D4 — **keep `place_type` inside the embedded `hoods?select=…places(…)` select**, that is per-row data and must stay live at Phase 2; (b) §3.3 keep `place_types_public_read` as written, add one line that the V1 client is not its reader (removing a policy churns a migration for no gain); (c) §4.2 fix the artifact name `place-types.json` → `place-types-tel-aviv.json`, matching what actually shipped and the `<thing>-tel-aviv.json` convention — verified against `main`, not chosen; (d) Decisions-log row citing T-049/`PAS-37` + passport D4, v2 bump; (e) same commit, flip the stale `**Status:** ready for trd-review` line (it cleared `trd-review` approved-as-is) — L-006's discipline applied to a TRD; (f) same commit, `prds/places-dataset/TRD.html` carries the old §4.2/§4.5 text and goes stale the instant the `.md` changes — regenerate it, or say so and open a `BOARD.md` row **with an owner**, never "flagged for whoever owns it" (that phrasing is what produced L-026/L-027); (g) name the emitter per the second finding — recommended shape, mechanism is architect's call: B3's `export_places.py` emits the registry from the same validated source that produces the `place_types` seed rows (one source, two artifacts, §5 step 3's existing rule), and T-037's A1 file is stated as a hand-authored Phase-1 placeholder the generator replaces at Phase 2, with L-024's ban attaching from that point forward.
- **Minor on the T-037 side — prose only, no contract change, no re-review, build untouched:** D4's line *"This is a deviation from T-042 §4.2/§4.5's implied client fetch and is flagged for T-042's reviewers"* is wrong on two counts now — §4.2 was never a client fetch (it already describes a shipped export), so the deviation is from §4.5 alone; and the flag is resolved, so it should name this ruling and date instead. `ios-developer`/`data-engineer` need not re-read.
- **Scope gate (Job 0), run on inherited work:** clean. T-049 authorizes no feature and adds no scope — it reconciles two documents belonging to features already carrying verbatim strategy citations (`passport.md` Motivation quotes the stamp-per-Been-place and Profile-tab lines; T-042 is the dataset those features read). Nothing here touches social, onboarding, or a parked phase.
- **Left behind:** (1) **T-049 must not close until the amendment lands** — a ruling in a Linear comment is not a reconciled document; the two TRDs still contradict each other on disk as of this entry. (2) `architect` not dispatched by me — `chief-of-staff` holds dispatch and is the only Linear writer; the amendment list above is the brief. (3) The emitter gap (second finding) is the item most likely to be dropped, because it reads as adjacent to the ticket's question rather than part of it — it is not: it is the same defect one phase later. (4) Untouched and still open, named so they aren't read as resolved by this ruling: T-042 B1's Aviran-gated vocabulary ratification (`PAS-6` item 11), and §4.4's standing **[ASSUMPTION]** that `place_type` is internal-only, which passport D12 also leans on.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit path. Tree was clean at start (`git status --short` empty at `55c7f1c`), so this commit carries no other session's hunks. No `passenger-code` change — ruling requires none. No Linear write (`chief-of-staff` is the single writer). Not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9. Hash in the final response.
- **Disclosure, rule 2, reverse direction:** my `agent-os/BOARD.md` T-049 rewrite was written to disk and then **swept into a concurrent `chief-of-staff` session's commit `passenger-brain 3e349c7`** ("round 4 addendum: ios-code-reviewer APPROVEs 952cf2f") before I could stage it — that commit's own edit was to the T-036/PAS-36 rows, and its message does not name the carried T-049 hunk. Verified, not assumed: `git show HEAD:agent-os/BOARD.md` contains my ruling text and `git status` reports `BOARD.md` unmodified, so **the row is on `main` and correct** — nothing is stranded and no re-edit is needed. Recording it here because rule 2's disclosure requirement is about the commit message, and the agent that could have written it didn't know it was carrying my text. This is the same class as the four 2026-08-02 commits, seen from the other side: the shared-tree window is wide enough that an edit-then-stage sequence loses the race, so stage in the same breath as the edit on `BOARD.md`, don't batch it with a later file.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-036/PAS-27 contrast re-review + PAS-36 revert verification: **APPROVE**

- **Did:** reviewed `passenger-code 952cf2f` ("revert wrong PAS-36 fix; fix T-036 contrast + T-035 minors (PAS-27)"), a three-part commit dispatched by `chief-of-staff`. Read `BOARD.md`'s Current Snapshot-equivalent (this file's own recent worklog, per the already-documented exception at `PROGRESS.md:830` — no "Current Snapshot" section exists in this file) and the immediately preceding `chief-of-staff` round-3 entry plus the `designer` entry above it before touching anything, since both describe the PAS-36 misdiagnosis this commit reverts.
  1. **T-036/PAS-27 contrast fix (main task).** Confirmed `PlacesListOverlay.swift:61` now backs the card with `Color("Surface")` instead of `.thickMaterial`. Verified `Surface.colorset/Contents.json` directly: alpha `1.000` in both the default and dark-appearance entries — genuinely opaque, not a translucent token in disguise. Verified `PlacesListContrastTests` (in `PassengerTests/ContrastRatioTests.swift:60-85`) resolve `UIColor(named: "MutedOnSurface"/"BadgeOnSurface")` against `UIColor(named: "Surface"/"BadgeSurface")` — with the background now genuinely `Color("Surface")` on screen, the test is measuring the real composited color for the first time; under the prior `.thickMaterial` it was measuring a color the map-blended UI never actually showed, which was the whole blocking finding. Confirmed the idiom match the commit message claims: `SettingsHint.swift:16`, `SearchOverlay.swift:48`, and `ColdOpenTitle.swift:37` all already use the identical `Color("Surface")`-in-`RoundedRectangle` pattern over the same live map — this fix brings `PlacesListOverlay` in line with an established codebase-wide rule, not a one-off. (Noted in passing, not part of this diff: `PassportSurface.swift`'s header comment already cites "T-036's own `.thickMaterial` → `Color("Surface")` fix" as the precedent for its own contrast rule — corroborates this fix is the correct, generalized pattern, not an isolated patch.)
  2. **PAS-36 revert.** Re-read `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` §4.6/§5 directly rather than trusting the revert's own commit message. §4.6: *"Row tap is therefore just `router.openPlace(place)` against the existing depth-1 sheet site... The modal renders above the list; dismissing it reveals the list unchanged."* §5: *"Open a place. Tap row → `router.openPlace(place)` → the existing depth-1 sheet presents above everything. Dismiss → back to the list, unchanged."* Both match the restored `onSelect: { place in detailRouter.openPlace(place) }` exactly and contradict the reverted `4cd55f8` fix (which force-dismissed the list via `chrome.dismiss()` on row tap). Confirmed the revert is clean: `grep -rn "selectPlaceFromList"` across the repo returns nothing — no dangling reference in `MapScreen.swift` or elsewhere, and the deleted `PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift` regression test (`selectingPlaceFromListDismissesList`) asserted the now-wrong behavior, correctly removed rather than left disabled. Confirmed the real D8 mechanism this revert leaves intact: `MapScreen.swift:481` (`handlePresentedSurfaceChange`) still calls `router.closePlace()` when *leaving* `.places` via a surface switch — the correct, TRD-specified catch-all — so the revert does not reintroduce any co-presentation the TRD actually forbids (Hood-sheet-vs-list, or a place modal stranded when the list closes by a different path). The bug Aviran actually saw is fully explained by finding #1 (a translucent list card behind a `.medium`-detent sheet reading as visual mess) — the list staying presented under the sheet was never the defect.
  3. **T-035/PAS-26 minors — no collision.** `MapScreen.swift`'s `.task` sequencing: confirmed `LocalQACoordinator.start()` (`Passenger/LocalQA/LocalQACoordinator.swift:52`) runs a `for await` loop that never returns until the screen goes away, and `handle(_:)` reads `answeredPlaceIDs`/`lastAskedAt` state that `loadPersistedState()` populates — the prior two-independent-`.task` construction really could race; sequencing them (`await loadPersistedState(); await start()`) inside one `.task` is correct and doesn't affect any other modifier on the view. `PlaceDetailModal.touristTrapSlot`'s `Label(...)` swap is a straightforward equivalent to the hand-built `HStack{Image;Text}` it replaces, matching every sibling row in the file. Neither change touches anything the contrast fix or the revert also touch.
- **`/vibe-security` pass:** no new network calls, no secrets, no location data touched by any of the three changes in this commit — grepped clean.
- **Verdict: APPROVE**, both on the contrast fix (verifiably fixes the problem — opaque color, tests now measure reality) and the revert (clean, TRD-conformant, doesn't reintroduce D8 exposure). No blocking findings. Moving T-036/PAS-27 and PAS-36 to `qa` with a note: behavioral check worth running is opening the Places list, tapping a row to open a place modal, dismissing the modal, and confirming the list reappears underneath unchanged (TRD §5's "Open a place" flow) — this is exactly the path the wrong `4cd55f8` fix broke and this revert restores, so it's the one regression a manual/UI-test pass should specifically re-confirm now that chief-of-staff's `xcodebuild test` run (253/253 unit + 6/6 UI, clean) is already reported green.
- **Left behind:** none. Both PAS-27 and PAS-36 are fully resolved by this commit as reviewed; no follow-up fix dispatch needed.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry, explicit path). Not touching `BOARD.md`/Linear — `chief-of-staff` holds write access. Committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9. Commit hash reported in the final response.

### 2026-08-03 — qa — T-034/PAS-25 QA pass: **PASS WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** QA pass on `passenger-code 6139f13` + the already-landed `EventSeed` CodingKeys fix (`24eb533`, confirmed still on `main`). Read `BOARD.md`'s T-034 row, the `ios-developer` build entry and `ios-code-reviewer`'s APPROVE WITH MINORS entry (both 2026-08-03) in full first. Wrote `prds/live-events-overlay/TEST-PLAN.md` from the TRD's §9 table (22 rows — one per P0 sub-check plus 3 rows for the reviewer's specific behavioral asks), then filled in verdicts.
- **Full `xcodebuild build` + `xcodebuild test`** (scheme Passenger, `platform=iOS Simulator,id=BF1DFDDD…` — iPhone 17, iPhone 16 unavailable on this machine, same substitution prior sessions made): **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, zero errors. Full suite (`PassengerTests` + `PassengerUITests` together, not scoped, per the standing full-target rule): every T-034-owned suite green — `EventSelection`, `EventHitTester`, `EventDetailRows`, `EventStore`, `EventSeed decode`, `EventSeed shipped seed §3.4 authoring rule`, `EventsAPI`, `EventLayer contrast (§4.5/§9 row 2b)`, `DetailRouter`, `EventDetailSheetInteractionTests`. 2 failures out of the full run, both "Test crashed with signal kill" (a crash pattern, not an assertion failure) — neither in T-034's scope: (1) `ColdOpenPerformanceTests.testColdOpenToInteractiveSignpost()`, re-run in isolation and passed clean (avg 0.675s) — the same flake multiple prior worklog entries already attribute to simulator/environment resource pressure, not code; citing that attribution per L-010 rather than re-deriving it. (2) `DebugVisitSourceTests.argumentEmitsOneDebugEvent()` (T-035's own LocalQA test) — new, not seen failing in T-035's own worklog entries, same crash signature as the known flake but **not confirmed in isolation this session**: a concurrent session started landing `Passenger/Search/SearchIndex.swift` (referencing an unlanded `Place.keywords` field) mid-retry, which broke the shared tree's build before an isolated re-run could complete. Spawned a background task (`task_aed5ee1f`) for T-035's owner to confirm flake-vs-real, rather than filing a Linear bug on an unconfirmed, out-of-scope crash — no Linear write made here (per workspace `CLAUDE.md`, `chief-of-staff` is the only agent that writes to Linear).
- **D7 tap-race (event marker vs. Hood sheet):** confirmed structurally by reading `MapScreen.swift:437-445`'s `handleTap` — precedence is exactly `event → place → hood` as TRD §4.5 specifies, `eventHitTester` gated on `eventStore.isLayerVisible`. Confirmed behaviorally by `EventDetailSheetInteractionTests.testTappingEventMarkerOpensEventDetailSheetNotAHoodSheet` — a real XCUITest touch on the rendered `eventMarker-seed-0001` button on a live iPhone 17 simulator, asserting the event sheet opens and `hoodSheetTitle` does not exist — green. **A fully interactive manual tap could not be completed this session:** the iOS Simulator control tool's `attach`/`screenshot`/`tap` actions were all blocked ("user has not granted Claude access... a recent request was declined or is awaiting a response") with no user present to grant it. Fell back to `xcrun simctl launch` + `xcrun simctl io screenshot` (unblocked, no permission gate) to confirm the event marker renders (correct rounded-rect + `sparkles` glyph, indigo colorset, at the expected Florentin coordinate) — but `xcrun simctl` has no public touch-injection API, so tapping it manually wasn't possible. The automated UI test is real on-device touch synthesis on the actual rendered element, not a mock, so it's treated as satisfying the reviewer's ask, with the above named as a real gap rather than silently substituted.
- **§3.4 fixture spot-check (plausible/varied, not degenerate):** independently re-derived all 8 authoring-rule clauses against the shipped `events-tel-aviv-seed.json` (18 events) rather than trusting the prior build/review entries' claims: 6 distinct hour buckets (0,1,2,3,5,6 — exactly the minimum), multiple empty buckets, one bucket (offset 5) with 14 events at distinct ranks, one event spanning 3 buckets (seed-0001), null `venue_name`/`category` cases present, one already-ended event, all names fictional. All 8 clauses hold. **Minor finding, new:** the cap-stress bucket (hour offset 5, the one built specifically to exercise the `markerCap+2` truncation) is 14 rows named "Fixture event 1" through "Fixture event 14," all at the identical coordinate (32.068646, 34.773847) and identical category ("music") — satisfies clause 3's letter (distinct ranks) but is visually degenerate at that one hour: a viewer landing on hour offset 5 would see 14 stacked generic markers at one point, unlike every other populated hour in the fixture. Not blocking — it's an internal test fixture, not real data, and the rest of the fixture (hours 0-3, 6) reads as intended (varied names/venues/categories). Flagged here per the reviewer's specific ask rather than filed as a separate Linear bug (cosmetic, fixture-only, and adjacent to the already-tracked `[ASSUMPTION]`s for the post-ship `designer` pass — that pass is the natural place to also vary bucket 5's names if it matters before real Phase-3 data arrives).
- **Dynamic Type / VoiceOver on `EventDetailModal`:** read `EventDetailModal.swift` in full — every text element uses system fonts (`.title2.bold()`, `.secondary`-styled `Label`s, `.caption`), so Dynamic Type scales correctly by construction; the close button's fixed `size: 32` is the SF Symbol glyph size inside a 44×44pt tap target, matching `PlaceDetailModal`'s own pattern, not a Dynamic-Type violation. The exact spoken marker label (`EventLayer.swift:36`, `"\(event.name), event, \(event.timeLabel)"`) is confirmed by direct read to be exactly the string TRD §4.5 specifies; **no automated test asserts this literal string** — this is the same pre-existing, already-named gap from `trd-review`/code-review (`BOARD.md` T-034 row), not new. **Could not complete a live VoiceOver/Accessibility-Inspector run this session** — same interactive-simulator-access blocker as the D7 manual check above (no user available to grant device access). Reporting this as an honest, unclosed gap rather than claiming a manual pass that didn't happen.
- **Verdict: PASS WITH MINORS.** No P0 requirement fails. Two minors, both named above (the bucket-5 fixture degeneracy; the still-open VoiceOver manual-run gap, environment-blocked not skipped). Nothing here contradicts `ios-code-reviewer`'s APPROVE WITH MINORS. Moving to `acceptance` per the QA lifecycle rule — `product` still owns its own acceptance pass against the PRD, plus the four standing scope calls (D1, D4, D10, D2) and `data-engineer`'s §4.2 confirmation, all unchanged by this QA pass.
- **Left behind:** (1) C12 (toggle) remains untestable, correctly blocked on T-032 — not a QA gap. (2) The VoiceOver manual run (X3) needs a session with live interactive simulator access to actually close — named explicitly rather than marked done. (3) The bucket-5 fixture cosmetic issue — no ticket filed (cosmetic, fixture-only); noted for whoever does the post-ship `designer` pass on the marker cap/glyph `[ASSUMPTION]`s, since that's the same fixture. (4) `task_aed5ee1f` spawned for T-035's owner on the `DebugVisitSourceTests` crash — not confirmed real, not this task's to fix.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/live-events-overlay/TEST-PLAN.md` (new) and this entry (`agent-os/PROGRESS.md`), explicit paths. No `passenger-code` changes (QA pass, no fixes applied — nothing here required one). Not touching `BOARD.md`/Linear per this dispatch's explicit instruction. Not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-03 — designer — design-principles.md §6: corrected the PAS-36 citation, added carve-out

- **Did:** dispatched by `chief-of-staff` (this run) to fix a follow-on problem in `design/design-principles.md` §6, "Presentation exclusivity — never stack two modal surfaces": that section (added by an earlier `designer` pass the same day) cited `PAS-36` as its evidencing example of a violation. `chief-of-staff` had since re-diagnosed `PAS-36` and found the diagnosis didn't hold — `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` §4.6/§5 (approved, `trd-review`-cleared) explicitly designs for the exact state `PAS-36` reported: a place-detail sheet opened by a row tap inside the still-presented Places list, with the list staying underneath and reappearing on dismiss (`router.placeDepth == 1`, derived — no `entryPath` field added). Read §4.6 and §5 directly before touching anything, plus `MapChromeState.swift`'s `NavSurface`/`toggle()` to confirm the exclusivity mechanism the section was pointing at.
- **Change made — kept the general rule, fixed the citation, added an explicit carve-out (option (a) of the two offered):**
  - Rewrote the "Rule" statement itself so it no longer flatly contradicts the carve-out: it now scopes "never co-presented" to **independently-reachable** top-level surfaces, not any two surfaces visible at once.
  - Added a **Carve-out** paragraph: a surface opened *from within* another surface it doesn't need to dismiss (parent stays presented, child renders above it, dismissing the child reveals the parent unchanged) is a parent/child pair, not two competing surfaces — cites `TRD.md` §4.6's `placeDepth == 1` derivation and its own line, *"the modal renders above the list; dismissing it reveals the list unchanged."*
  - Added a **Corrected 2026-08-03** paragraph stating plainly that `PAS-36` was previously cited as a violation and that diagnosis didn't hold, why (TRD §4.6 designs for exactly that state on purpose), and that **no other confirmed evidencing example currently exists in this codebase** — the rule is kept anyway because it's still independently supported by T-036 D8's own reasoning about *unintended* co-presentation (an orphaned sheet, or opening one surface without closing another first), which is a real, distinct failure mode from the parent/child case.
  - Reworded the D8 citation and the "Checkable at spec/review time" paragraph to match: D8 means never *orphaned* co-presence, not "never simultaneously on screen at all"; a reviewer checking for a §6 finding should first ask whether the second surface was opened from within the first (carve-out, not a finding) before treating stacked surfaces as a breach.
  - Removed the bare `**Cite:** PAS-36.` line — folded into the corrected paragraph instead.
- **Left as-is, considered and rejected:** narrowing the rule's scope (option (b)) — rejected because the general "no orphaned/accidental co-presentation" guidance is still sound and still has a real mechanism (`DetailRouter`, `MapChromeState.NavSurface`) behind it; narrowing would have thrown out guidance that's still correct along with the one bad citation. Did not touch §7's reviewer checklist line referencing §6 — it already says "check for a single 'what's presented' state," which still reads correctly once §7's own text is read against the new carve-out (a reviewer checking that heuristic will naturally ask the carve-out's question). Did not generate an `.html` twin — checked precedent first: no other file in `design/` (including the phase-1 design docs and this same file's original add) has one except `ux-flows.md`/`.html`, so this isn't an established convention for `design-principles.md`.
- **Flagging outside this task's scope, not acted on:** this session's dispatch brief said `PAS-36`'s shipped behavior (list stays under the place sheet) is TRD-approved intentional design. But `PROGRESS.md`'s own recent entries (2026-08-03, `ios-developer` and `chief-of-staff`, `PAS-36` fix `passenger-code 4cd55f8`, `ios-code-reviewer` APPROVE) show a *different*, concurrent session already shipped a fix that does the opposite — `chrome` is now dismissed alongside `detailRouter.openPlace(place)`, so the list **no longer** stays presented under the sheet, and that change has already cleared code review and moved to `qa`. That looks like two concurrent chief-of-staff threads reaching opposite conclusions about whether `PAS-36`'s reported state is a bug — one shipped a fix reverting the TRD-approved co-presentation, the other (this one) concluded the co-presentation was correct all along. Not mine to resolve (out of scope, and I was told not to touch `BOARD.md`/Linear this pass) — surfacing it here and in my final response so `chief-of-staff` can reconcile which behavior is actually supposed to ship before `qa`'s pass closes `PAS-36`.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `design/design-principles.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit paths. Committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9. Commit hash reported in the final response. `BOARD.md`/Linear not touched, per the dispatch brief.

### 2026-08-03 — chief-of-staff — hilos @chief: iOS-26/Liquid Glass scoping + claude-code-apple-skills review

- **Verbatim request** (Aviran, founder-direct, hilos `@chief` mention): "make sure ios developer is develop only for ios 26 (liquid glass) and that they familiar with apple guidelines. review this https://github.com/rshankras/claude-code-apple-skills repo for the ios skills and understand what you can take from it."
- **As I read it:** two asks — (1) confirm/harden the `ios-developer` agent's scope to iOS-26-only with Liquid Glass as the design language and explicit HIG grounding; (2) review the named GitHub repo of Claude Code skills for Apple platforms and pull in whatever's genuinely useful. **[ASSUMPTION]** "familiar with apple guidelines" means HIG + App Store Review Guidelines, both of which the agent file already named before this edit — treated as confirm-and-strengthen, not a new requirement.
- **Did:** did this directly (fleet-config edit, not board/feature work — no subagent dispatched, so no relay round-trip needed).
  1. Read `.claude/agents/ios-developer.md` before assuming anything was missing. Found: `passenger-code` already builds iOS-26-only (`IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET = 26.0`, `Passenger.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, confirmed live, not from a doc) and the agent already named HIG/App Store Review Guidelines as non-negotiable — but nothing in the file said "iOS 26 only, no backward-compat path" explicitly, and "Liquid Glass" wasn't mentioned anywhere despite being the actual iOS 26 design language.
  2. Reviewed `github.com/rshankras/claude-code-apple-skills` (`gh api`, not WebFetch — GitHub content) — a ~150-skill Claude Code plugin covering app-store/generators/growth/ios/macos/swift/swiftui/testing/visionOS. Read `skills/design/liquid-glass/SKILL.md` in full (the WWDC25 ruleset: navigation-layer-only, never glass-on-glass, `.regular`/`.clear` variants never mixed, tint sparingly, no steady-state intersections, `GlassEffectContainer` for correctness not just perf, shape system, morphing via `glassEffectID`) and skimmed `skills/ios/coding-best-practices/SKILL.md` and `skills/ios/ui-review/SKILL.md`.
  3. **Decision: did not install the repo as a plugin or copy its multi-file skill packages wholesale.** Its skills are built as `SKILL.md` + separate reference files (`hig-checklist.md`, `font-guidelines.md`, etc.) meant to live under `.claude/skills/<name>/`, which doesn't match how this fleet works — `ios-developer.md`/`ios-code-reviewer.md` already carry domain knowledge inline rather than delegating to a skill package, and most of the repo (App Store/marketing/paywall/onboarding/macOS/visionOS generators) is either another agent's turf (`marketing`, `product`) or not applicable (Passenger is iOS-only, no macOS/visionOS target, no IAP yet). Folding in a 150-skill plugin for one relevant section would be a maintenance surface with no owner.
  4. **What was actually adopted — folded inline into `ios-developer.md`:** (a) an explicit "iOS 26 is the only target, full stop" paragraph — no `if #available` fallbacks, no pre-26 idiom by habit, current-generation API by default; (b) a condensed "Liquid Glass rules" section transcribing the repo's WWDC25 ruleset in Passenger's own voice (navigation-layer-only, never glass-on-glass, two variants never mixed, tint discipline, no steady-state intersections, `GlassEffectContainer` correctness requirement, shape system, no custom `.presentationBackground` on sheets), plus a pointer to `design/design-principles.md`'s existing contrast-verifiability rule (learned from real finding PAS-27/T-036) for the glass-vs-opaque-surface judgment call; (c) a condensed HIG checklist (tap targets, Dynamic Type, semantic color/contrast, loading/empty/error states, standard navigation) pulled from the repo's `ui-review` skill's review categories, kept to Passenger's existing "checklist in the agent file" convention rather than a separate doc.
  5. **Closed the loop on the reviewer side too** (not explicitly asked, small and directly justified): added item 8 to `ios-code-reviewer.md`'s review checklist — a Liquid Glass conformance pass (hand-rolled `.ultraThinMaterial` instead of `.glassEffect()` is a finding, glass-on-glass/wrong-variant/content-layer-glass are findings, `GlassEffectContainer` omission is a correctness bug not a preference, any `if #available`/pre-26 pattern is a finding) — otherwise the developer has new rules to follow and the reviewer has no instruction to check them.
  6. Mirrored both edited files to `passenger-brain/agent-os/agents-mirror/` and `passenger-code/.claude/agents/` in the same turn, per the standing rule; `diff` confirmed silent on all three copies for both files.
- **Not adopted, with reasons (for the record, not acted on further):** App Store/marketing/growth skills — `marketing` agent's turf. Generator skills (paywall, onboarding, push, live activities, etc.) — Passenger has none of those features built yet; adopt when a TRD actually calls for one, same "no upfront packages" principle already in `passenger-code/README.md`. `product/*` skills (PRD generator, market research) — `product` agent's turf, and this fleet already has its own PRD conventions. Testing skills (`mutation-testing`, `tdd-*`, `snapshot-test-setup`) — plausibly useful later for `qa`, but out of scope for this ask; flagging here rather than acting unilaterally on `qa`'s behalf. `macos/*`, `visionos/*` — no target platform.
- **Evidence:** `grep IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET Passenger.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` → `26.0` (both configs). `gh api repos/rshankras/claude-code-apple-skills` → 583 stars, public, default branch `main`. Three-way `diff` on `ios-developer.md` and `ios-code-reviewer.md` across `.claude/agents/`, `passenger-brain/agent-os/agents-mirror/`, `passenger-code/.claude/agents/` → silent (in sync).
- **Left behind:** nothing blocking. This was fleet-configuration, not a board task — no BOARD.md row, no Linear issue (matches how prior agent-file-only edits in this log are tracked). If `qa` wants to look at the testing-skills section of the reviewed repo later, that's a separate, `qa`-owned call.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — this entry (`agent-os/PROGRESS.md`) plus `agent-os/agents-mirror/ios-developer.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/ios-code-reviewer.md`, explicit paths (workspace-root `.claude/agents/ios-developer.md`/`ios-code-reviewer.md` are not git-tracked, per `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`'s live↔mirror rule — they're the live originals, the mirror is the tracked backup). `passenger-code` — `.claude/agents/ios-developer.md`, `.claude/agents/ios-code-reviewer.md`, explicit paths. Both committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9. Commit hashes reported in the final response.

### 2026-08-03 — chief-of-staff — PAS-36: verified designer + ios-developer reports, moved to ios-code-reviewer

### 2026-08-03 — ios-developer — PAS-36 fix: Places list stays co-presented with place-detail sheet

- **Did:** fixed the bug dispatched by `chief-of-staff`'s hilos intake (entry immediately below). Read this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog and `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md` first, per protocol.
- **Root cause: confirmed as diagnosed, not corrected.** Independently re-read `PlacesListOverlay.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`'s overlay/sheet wiring (lines 231-243, 358-385), `MapChromeState.swift`, `DetailRouter.swift`, and `MapScreenTests.swift` before touching anything. The dispatch's root cause is exactly right: `PlacesListOverlay` renders as a z5 `.overlay` gated on `chrome.presented == .places` (`MapScreen.swift:235`); its `onSelect` closure (`MapScreen.swift:238`, pre-fix) called `detailRouter.openPlace(place)` only, never `chrome`. `DetailRouter.openPlace` flips `isDepth1Presented` to `true`, opening the system `.sheet` at Site A (`MapScreen.swift:243`) for `PlaceDetailModal`. Because `chrome.presented` never changes value (it was already `.places`, stays `.places`), `.onChange(of: chrome.presented)` (`MapScreen.swift:312`) never fires, so `handlePresentedSurfaceChange` — the function that already closes a stranded place modal on the other two D8 transitions — never runs for this third one. The list overlay and the place-detail sheet render simultaneously, violating T-036's own TRD D8 invariant. Confirmed `MapScreenTests.swift` had no coverage for this path before this fix (only `openPlacesListClosesHood` and both directions of `handlePresentedSurfaceChange` existed).
- **Fix:** `Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift` — added `static func selectPlaceFromList(router: DetailRouter, chrome: MapChromeState, place: Place)`, alongside the existing `openPlacesList`/`handlePresentedSurfaceChange` static functions (same pure, unit-testable-without-a-live-SwiftUI-environment pattern this file already uses for D8 wiring). It calls `router.openPlace(place)` then `chrome.dismiss()`. `PlacesListOverlay`'s `onSelect` closure now calls this instead of `detailRouter.openPlace(place)` directly. This makes the list-row-tap path an explicit dismiss rather than relying on the `onChange` hook, which structurally can't catch it (selecting a row isn't a surface transition — `chrome.presented` doesn't change).
- **Prevention (what stops this recurring):** added `PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift`'s `MapScreenD8WiringTests.selectingPlaceFromListDismissesList` — sets `chrome.toggle(.places)`, calls `MapScreen.selectPlaceFromList(router:chrome:place:)`, and asserts `chrome.presented != .places` and `router.place?.id == place.id`. This is a real regression guard: it fails against the pre-fix code (which never touched `chrome`) and passes against the fix, sitting in the same suite and same style as the other two D8-invariant tests so a future change to this wiring is checked by the same mechanism.
- **Evidence:** `xcodebuild -project Passenger.xcodeproj -scheme Passenger -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17' build` → **BUILD SUCCEEDED** (iPhone 16 isn't in this machine's simulator set; substituted iPhone 17, same iOS 26 SDK). `xcodebuild ... -only-testing:PassengerTests/MapScreenTests -only-testing:PassengerTests/MapScreenD8WiringTests test` → **TEST SUCCEEDED**, 14/14 tests passed including the new `selectingPlaceFromListDismissesList`. Not re-confirmed on a live simulator by tapping through the UI (no screenshot/manual repro this pass) — the fix and its regression test are verified at the unit level, same rigor as the existing D8 wiring tests it sits beside.
- **Left behind:** manual/UI-level re-confirmation of the original screenshot repro (open Places list, tap a row, watch the list disappear as the sheet opens) not done in this pass — worth a QA spot-check before `PAS-36` closes fully, though the regression test covers the actual logic bug. Nothing else new; the `designer` follow-up (design-principles.md §6) is a separate parallel dispatch, not touched here.
- **Git:** `passenger-code 4cd55f8` — `Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift`, `PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift`, explicit paths (did not stage `Passenger/Map/SettingsHint.swift` or the untracked `Passenger/Support/HeatRepaintSignpost.swift` — confirmed via `git status` these are T-032's unrelated in-flight files, per rule 2/L-007). `passenger-brain` — this entry only, explicit path (`agent-os/PROGRESS.md`); `BOARD.md` not touched, consistent with the chief-of-staff intake entry's note that bug-labeled tickets have no fine-grained board row (Linear `PAS-36` is the system of record). Committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-03 — chief-of-staff — hilos bug intake: PAS-36 stacked Places/place-detail sheets — filed, root cause diagnosed, dispatched

- **Did:** Aviran reported via hilos `@chief` (relayed through the coordinating session, screenshot described in text — no accessible image file, see ticket) that the "Places" list sheet and a place-detail sheet ("Carmel Market Spice Corner") were visible stacked on screen simultaneously. Did the QA intake myself rather than spawning `qa` (the coordinator's instruction — a spawned agent can't see an attached image, and here even the coordinator only had a text description, not a file). Read `BOARD.md` T-036 row + this file's Current Snapshot first. Grepped `passenger-code` for sheet/place-list code, read `DetailRouter.swift`, `PlacesListOverlay.swift`, `MapScreen.swift` (the overlay wiring, `openPlacesList`, `handlePresentedSurfaceChange`), `MapChromeState.swift`, and `MapChromeStateTests.swift`/`MapScreenTests.swift` in full before concluding anything.
- **Root cause, confirmed from source, not just inferred:** `PlacesListOverlay` renders as a z5 overlay gated on `chrome.presented == .places` (`MapScreen.swift:235`). Its `onSelect` (`MapScreen.swift:238`) calls `detailRouter.openPlace(place)` only, never touching `chrome`. That opens the real system `.sheet` at Site A (`MapScreen.swift:243`) while `chrome.presented` stays `.places`, so the overlay keeps rendering underneath it. This is not an open UX question — it directly violates T-036's own approved TRD invariant **D8** ("a NavSurface and a system sheet are never co-presented in either direction"), which the code already enforces for the other two transitions (`openPlacesList` closes the Hood sheet; `handlePresentedSurfaceChange` closes a stray place modal on leaving `.places`) but not for this third one — selecting a place row never changes `chrome.presented`, so the `onChange` that would clean it up never fires. `MapScreenTests.swift` covers the other two transitions but has no test for this path, confirming the gap is real and untested rather than a deliberate, differently-specced behavior.
- **Linear:** filed `PAS-36` (team Passenger, project Passenger V1, label `Bug`, priority Medium) with full repro/root-cause/suggested-fix per the workspace's bug-ticket template. Claimed it (`owner:ios-developer`, → `In Progress`) per the single-writer protocol before dispatching.
- **Dispatched via relay to `main`** (2 agents, both self-contained briefs with file paths/line numbers):
  - `ios-developer` — fix `PAS-36`: dismiss `chrome` alongside `detailRouter.openPlace(place)` in `onSelect` (`MapScreen.swift:238`), or correct the suggested fix if wrong; add a test covering the list-row-tap path alongside the existing `handlePresentedSurfaceChange`/`openPlacesList` tests; report root-cause confirmation/correction + prevention statement + commit hash, per the bug lifecycle's requirement that a closed bug names what prevents recurrence.
  - `designer` — a second, independent ask folded in from Aviran mid-task (not a separate hilos ping, per his own instruction): log a standing design-principles entry at `passenger-brain/design/design-principles.md` (not `LESSONS.md` — that file is explicitly process-only, "never feature content"; `design-principles.md` is the actual home for a UI rule like this and is already shared with `ios-code-reviewer`) generalizing "never present two independent modal surfaces at once" beyond this one bug, citing `PAS-36` as the evidencing example and T-036's own D8 as the existing mechanism (`DetailRouter`'s single content-switched `.sheet`, `MapChromeState`'s swap-not-stack `toggle()`) to point future specs at.
- **Left behind:** both dispatches in flight — this entry ends before either relay reply lands. Neither Linear state nor this row advances further until `ios-developer`'s (and, separately, `ios-code-reviewer`'s) reports come back. No image file was ever available to attach to `PAS-36` — only Aviran's text description of the screenshot was relayed to this session; if the actual screenshot exists on his device, worth attaching directly to the Linear issue later for the record.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-036/PAS-27 code-review (C1–C14): **REQUEST CHANGES**

- **Did:** reviewed `passenger-code 7688834` (the 32-file build) and `passenger-code 46d2af2` (the two-file compile-error fix pass) against TRD v2 `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (`trd-review` cleared, both `ios-developer`/`ios-code-reviewer` APPROVE, no residual findings). Read `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row and PROGRESS.md's Current Snapshot + the `ios-developer` T-036 build entry and both trd-review APPROVE entries first, per protocol. Read every file in both commits' diffs plus the full text of `MapChromeState.swift`, `PlaceLayer.swift`, `PlacesListOverlay.swift`, `PlacesListRow.swift`, `PlacesListComposition.swift`, `PlaceProvenance.swift`, `VisitedPlacesStore.swift`, `BundledVisitSource.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`'s D8 wiring, and the full test suite added. Did not touch the shared tree's other in-flight files (`SettingsHint.swift`, `HeatRepaintSignpost.swift` — confirmed via `git status` these are T-032's, still uncommitted, unrelated to this diff). `/vibe-security` pass: clean — no network calls added beyond widening `PlacesAPI`'s existing `select=` string with two already-public non-sensitive columns, no secrets/keys/tokens in the diff (grepped), and the location-privacy surface (§3.4) is a hard **no** — confirmed by grep that no file in `Places/`/`PlacesList/` imports `CoreLocation` except the two files that already did pre-task (`Place.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, for `CLLocationCoordinate2D` only).
- **TRD-conformance pass, byte-level:** `MapChromeState.swift` diffed line-by-line against `time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1 — four cases (`search, heat, places, profile`), `String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable`, `toggle`/`dismiss` bodies matching the exclusivity rule in prose (`presented = (presented == surface) ? nil : surface`). Exact match; the v1→v2 contract-mismatch class of bug this TRD was sent back for once already does not recur. D1 (`permanentlyClosed` non-optional on all three `PlaceCatalog` paths, each with its own decode-failure test), D2 (bundled fixture, zero `CoreLocation` import, verified by grep not assumed), D3 (`PlacesListComposition` as a pure `enum`, `PlaceProvenance: Comparable` `max`-precedence, `VisitKind` structurally unable to claim `.saved`), D7 (`PlacesButton` in the bucket-2 cluster, fades with `chrome.isPresenting`), D8 (`MapScreen.openPlacesList`/`handlePresentedSurfaceChange`, both directions unit-tested in `MapScreenD8WiringTests`), D9 (reserved empty `touristHeavySlot`, same construction as `PlaceDetailModal.touristTrapSlot`) — all built exactly as specified, each with real test coverage (`MapChromeStateTests`, `PlaceCatalogTests`, `PlacesListCompositionTests`, `BundledVisitSourceTests`/`VisitedPlacesStoreTests`, `MapScreenD8WiringTests`) that I re-read rather than took on the worklog's word.
- **Finding, blocking:** **`Passenger/PlacesList/PlacesListOverlay.swift:61`** — the list card's background is `.background(.thickMaterial, in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 20, style: .continuous))`, a translucent blur sitting directly over the live map, with `PlacesListRow`'s `MutedOnSurface` provenance-word text and `BadgeOnSurface`-on-`BadgeSurface` "Permanently closed" badge rendered straight on top of it — no opaque layer in between. This contradicts an explicit, three-times-stated codebase rule: (1) this very TRD's §4.5 says the container is "the same reason and same construction as T-032's D2, reused rather than re-derived," and T-032's D2 (`time-slider/TRD.md` §4.2) is explicit — "Background: opaque `Color("Surface")`... **Not** `.ultraThinMaterial` — a contrast ratio against a translucent layer over a live map is not a number anyone can verify"; (2) the shipped precedent this exact rule produced, `Passenger/Map/SettingsHint.swift:12-14`: "Opaque surface-token background, not the map itself — a contrast ratio against a map is unverifiable (§8 D1). Not `.ultraThinMaterial`: its effective luminance varies with whatever the map draws underneath"; (3) this TRD's own §4.10 states `MutedOnSurface` is meant to be verified "≥4.5:1 **on Surface**." The shipped `PlacesListContrastTests` (`ContrastRatioTests.swift`) do assert `MutedOnSurface` vs. `Color("Surface")` and `BadgeOnSurface` vs. `BadgeSurface` at ≥4.5:1, and pass — but that is not what's actually behind this text on screen; the real backdrop is `.thickMaterial`, whose effective luminance/hue is whatever the live map (a saturated Hood/heat fill, at any hour) draws through the blur. **Failure scenario:** a user opens the Places list positioned over a brightly-colored heat fill; the caption "Been"/"Saved"/"Visited" and the closed badge sit on a background the passing unit test never measures, so a real, on-device contrast failure for a low-vision user would ship invisibly — every automated check green, §9 row 4(d)'s "≥4.5:1 in both interface styles" claim false in practice. This is the exact defect class T-031 §8 D1 exists to prevent, and it is a one-line fix: swap to `.background(Color("Surface"), in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 20, style: .continuous))`, matching `SettingsHint`'s and T-032's own idiom.
- **Minor, non-blocking:** §3.3's fixture-authoring rule ("at least one entry that is also savable to exercise precedence") isn't independently asserted against the *shipped* fixture by `BundledVisitSourceTests.shippedFixtureSatisfiesAuthoringRule` (that test checks ≥1 `been`, ≥1 `visited`, and `count < 9` only) — precedence itself is thoroughly covered synthetically in `PlacesListCompositionTests`, so this is a completeness gap in one test's scope, not a functional defect. Worth a follow-up assertion, not worth blocking on.
- **Everything else:** solid. All three `PlaceCatalog` source paths correctly threaded and independently tested (seed/live/cache, each with its own decode-failure-and-fallback test); `PlacesListComposition`/`PlaceProvenance`/`VisitKind` precedence is structurally sound and well-tested including the un-save-never-writes-through invariant; `VisitedPlacesStore`/`BundledVisitSource` correctly zero-`CoreLocation`, correctly enforce the keep-higher-`VisitKind` invariant (§3.4, added at v2) on a duplicate fixture id; D8 presentation exclusivity unit-tested in both directions with no `DetailRouter` change (confirmed via `git show` — untouched); the dashed personal-place ring on `PlaceLayer` matches §4.9 exactly (inset, `allowsHitTesting(false)`, gated inside the same `showsNames` block, `", in your Places"` a11y clause); `SavedPlacesStore` got exactly the one specified read accessor and nothing else. Build/test health as reported by the relay taken at face value, not independently re-run — chief-of-staff's full-suite re-run this same run reported clean build, 253/45, this task's own suites all passing, and the sole failure (`ColdOpenPerformanceTests.testColdOpenToInteractiveSignpost`) attributed to simulator/environment flakiness unrelated to any of T-034/T-035/T-036's code; no reason found here to doubt that attribution.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** One blocking finding (`PlacesListOverlay.swift:61`, contrast-verifiability regression), one non-blocking minor. Back to `build` — the fix is a single line; re-review should confirm the swap and re-run `PlacesListContrastTests` (the test itself doesn't need to change, since it already measures the color the fix would put behind the text).
- **Left behind:** four `product`-flagged items from the TRD (D4 sort, D5 no offline banner, D7 button placement/fade cost, §9 row 5's absence-not-behavior framing) are unchanged by this review, not re-litigated here. `qa`'s TEST-PLAN.md build against §9 not started.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-035/PAS-26 code-review (C1–C14 only): **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** reviewed `passenger-code 7e40ace` (`git show 7e40ace`, cross-checked against `6139f13..7e40ace` for the Place-layer edits the commit message says landed inside T-036's concurrent commits `7688834`/`46d2af2`) against TRD v2 `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` (both `trd-review` re-run APPROVEs verified in this run's own PROGRESS.md reading, `analytics-engineer` D9 ratified). Read `BOARD.md`'s T-035 row and PROGRESS.md's Current Snapshot + the `ios-developer` build entry in full first, per protocol. Read every new/changed file in `Flag/`, `LocalQA/`, `Map/MapZoomTier.swift`, `Map/HoodLayer.swift`, `Map/MapScreen.swift`'s wiring diff, `Detail/HoodSheet.swift`, `Detail/PlaceDetailModal.swift`, and all new test files, plus `Places/Place.swift`/`PlacesAPI.swift`/`PlacesCache.swift`/`PlaceCatalog.swift` for the decode claim. No security-auditor dispatched alongside, per the relay's note — agreed: no network calls in this diff, `LocalQASync` is a stub returning 0, `LocalQARecord` stores a place ID + hour-truncated timestamp only (no coordinate), file protection is `.completeFileProtectionUntilFirstUserAuthentication` + backup-excluded, matching TRD §3.3 exactly — not auth/RLS/payments/storage-buckets/LLM/rate-limits. `/vibe-security` grep pass (secrets/keys/tokens patterns) came back clean.
- **Findings, both minor, neither blocking:**
  1. **`Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift:300-304`** — `localQACoordinator.loadPersistedState()` and `.start()` are two independent `.task` modifiers with no ordering guarantee between them (SwiftUI schedules both concurrently on appear). If a `VisitEvent` arrives before the persisted ledger loads, `LocalQAGate.decide` sees an empty `answeredPlaceIDs`/`nil lastAskedAt`, so a place answered in a prior session could get re-asked. Currently masked in Phase 1 only because the sole trigger, `DebugVisitSource`, has a hardcoded ~1s post-launch delay that comfortably outlasts a single JSON read — but `start()`/`handle()` is the exact code Phase 2's real triggers (`foregroundArrival`/`notificationTap`) will run through unchanged, and those can fire at any point after cold launch. Fix: sequence the two calls in one `.task` (`await loadPersistedState()` then `await start()`) rather than racing them.
  2. **`Passenger/Detail/PlaceDetailModal.swift:80-86`** (`touristTrapSlot`) — hand-builds `HStack { Image(systemName: "camera.fill"); Text(...) }` instead of `Label(_:systemImage:)`, breaking the pattern every other icon+text row in this diff's own neighborhood uses (`categoryRow` two lines above it, `PlacesListRow`'s "Permanently closed" badge, `HoodButton`, `EventDetailModal`'s rows, `HoodSheet`'s error row, `CachedDataIndicator` — all `Label` or `Label` + explicit `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)`). A raw `Image(systemName:)` isn't hidden from the accessibility tree by default, so a VoiceOver user swiping the place-detail modal gets an extra, auto-labeled "camera" stop before "Tourist-heavy spot" — a redundant announcement none of the sibling rows produce. Fix: `Label(FlagCopy.placeLine, systemImage: "camera.fill").foregroundStyle(Color("Flag"))`.
- **Independently confirmed, not just taken on the build worklog's word:**
  - **C12/§9 row 8(f) mechanical half** — read `LocalQAPresenter.swift` directly: exactly one `window.isHidden = false`, zero `makeKeyAndVisible` calls (2 of the 3 grep hits for that string are in doc comments, not calls), `accessibilityViewIsModal = false` set once, `windowLevel = .normal + 1`, exactly one `UIAccessibility.post(notification: .announcement, ...)`, zero `.screenChanged`/`.layoutChanged`. Adds confidence to the mechanically-checkable half; the live VoiceOver/Accessibility-Inspector focus sampling is still `qa`'s to run per the TRD, not re-litigated here.
  - **The `let`/`var` Decodable claim** — confirmed real and confirmed fixed. `Place.swift:27`, `PlacesAPI.swift:39`, `PlacesCache.swift:36`, `PlaceCatalog.swift:204` all now use `var isTouristTrap: Bool? = nil` with a comment explaining why; this is genuine, documented Swift `Decodable`-synthesis behavior — a `let` optional stored property carrying an initializer expression is excluded from the synthesized `init(from:)`'s decode calls entirely and always resolves to its default, regardless of what the wire payload carries. **Recommend a `LESSONS.md` entry**: any `Decodable`/`Codable` model property with a default value must be declared `var`, never `let`, or the field silently never decodes — general enough to bite the next optional-with-default field anyone adds to any wire model in this codebase.
  - **Requirement completeness (L-009 style check)** — `Place.isTouristTrap` has exactly one real consumer, `PlaceDetailModal.touristTrapSlot` (grep confirms zero other reads outside tests), matching the TRD's one required place-level surface. `Hood.isTouristTrap` is consumed by both `HoodLayer` (stroke + centroid label, all three `annotationContent` branches carry `.accessibilityLabel(voiceOverLabel)`) and `HoodSheet` (three-state line) — every flag surface the PRD/TRD name has a real reader, no field written and never rendered.
  - **Seed data spot-check** — `places-tel-aviv.json`: `florentin-anna-loulou-bar: true`, `kerem-dr-shakshuka: false`, remaining 7 places `null` — matches the commit message exactly.
  - `LocalQA/` imports only `Foundation`/`SwiftUI`/`UIKit`, never `Hoods/` or `Map/` — TRD §2.2's isolation invariant holds; render-function signatures (`FlagStroke.treatment(for:band:)`, `HoodSpeech.label(name:band:flag:)`) take no `LocalQA/`-declared type, matching §9 row 9(a).
- **Not re-litigated, per the relay's note:** §9 row 8(f)'s live-device VoiceOver half (qa's to run) and req 5's busy+flagged end-to-end observability (blocked on T-032's C10 density seed) — both expected gaps, not defects.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** Both findings above are real but non-blocking — neither is a functional break nor an accessibility failure severe enough to escalate past Maslow precedence (the VoiceOver issue is a redundant announcement, not lost information or lost function; the load/start race has no live trigger path in Phase 1). Recommend `ios-developer` fold both into a small follow-up rather than a full REQUEST CHANGES round-trip. Move to `qa` with a note to exercise the two visible LocalQA outcomes (already covered by `LocalQAToastInteractionTests`) plus a manual VoiceOver pass per TRD §9 row 8(f).

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-038/PAS-29 trd-review: **APPROVE WITH MINORS** — D1's deviation holds on independent re-derivation, one accessibility gap found

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` (v1, `passenger-brain 4669c4d`) against the PRD, `ux-flows.md` §2.1, and T-032's live TRD (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md`, v3) — pre-code gate, so no build to diff against. Read first, in full: `BOARD.md`'s T-038 row, the architect's 2026-08-03 T-038 worklog entry, T-032's D1/D2/§4.1/§4.10, T-036's TRD (its own `.sheet()`-rejection line and D8/§4.6), `ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1 verbatim, and the PRD's Requirements + Technical design sections. `ios-developer`'s parallel signoff not consulted before reaching this verdict, per dispatch instruction.
- **D1 (the search-sheet-as-overlay-not-`.sheet()` deviation) — re-derived independently, holds.** The dispatch flagged this as the call to scrutinize: three upstream texts (PRD Technical design, `ux-flows.md` §2.1's explicit instruction naming T-038, and the dispatch brief itself) all said reuse T-033's `.presentationBackgroundInteraction`; the architect built a z-layered overlay instead, citing T-032 D2. Checked T-032's D2 text directly rather than trusting the citation: *"a SwiftUI layer inside `MapScreen`'s `ZStack` at z5, below `MapNavRow` at z7... not `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` (there is no presentation). The nav row must stay hit-testable or `ux-flows.md` §2.1's direct nav-switch requirement fails."* This is not merely precedent-by-citation — it is **structurally correct as a matter of SwiftUI/UIKit sheet-presentation semantics, independent of any prior TRD**: a `.sheet()` presents in a system layer above the *entire* hosting view controller's hierarchy, so nothing in a SwiftUI `ZStack` — including a nav row positioned at a higher `z` — can render above a presented sheet. `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` only restores touch-through in the screen region the sheet does *not* visually cover; at `.medium`/`.large` (or this app's own compact/expanded custom heights), the sheet covers the bottom band where the nav row lives, so the modifier cannot deliver "nav row stays tappable while the sheet is up" regardless of how it's configured. PRD req 1 bullet 4 ("opening search closes whichever of {heat modal, Places list, Profile} is open") and its converse (opening heat/places/profile while search is open) both require exactly that reachability. **`ux-flows.md` §2.1's instruction to T-038 is about the *map* staying interactive behind the sheet — a different claim from nav-row reachability** — and the TRD doesn't conflate the two; it argues the nav-row point from PRD req 1 bullet 4 directly. Conclusion: the deviation is correct, not just consistent with precedent, and would survive centralization into `PAS-16` as the "nav surfaces vs. system sheets" split the TRD itself proposes (§4.1 D1 note) — verified this is coherent by checking that T-032's and T-036's own TRDs state the identical structural reason (`.sheet` covers z7) rather than each inventing its own.
- **One real gap found, not raised by the TRD's own §12 self-check list: the z3 tap-catcher's VoiceOver behaviour is unspecified.** D3 (line 534) and §2.3's z-order table state the `.search` scrim renders at **opacity 0 and still hit-tests** — a full-screen invisible-but-tappable layer. Neither §4.7/§4.9 (where it's built) nor §9 row 8 (the accessibility verification table) says whether it carries `.accessibilityHidden(true)` or an explicit label/trait. Checked whether this is inherited correctly-specified behaviour from T-032, since T-038 reuses T-032's scrim if it has landed: it is not — T-032's own scrim spec (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md` line 106, `Color.black.opacity(…)`, `.contentShape(Rectangle())`, tap → dismiss) has the same gap; T-032 uses `.accessibilityHidden(true)` explicitly elsewhere (`EdgeHint`, line 448) but never states it for the scrim itself. T-038 makes the risk concrete rather than inheriting it inertly: an *opacity-0* full-screen hit-testing layer is materially worse than T-032's *visible* scrim — nothing about it is discoverable by a sighted QA pass, only by VoiceOver actually landing on it mid-swipe. An unlabeled full-screen element sitting in the accessibility tree between a VoiceOver user and `SearchOverlay`'s own controls (field, chips, rows, ✕) is exactly the "invisible-but-tappable layer reads fine visually but breaks screen-reader navigation" failure mode this review was asked to check for. **Recommended fix, one line:** either `.accessibilityHidden(true)` on the z3 tap-catcher (VoiceOver dismisses only via the ✕ or the standard two-finger-scrub "dismiss" gesture, never by finding this layer) or, if tap-to-dismiss should stay VoiceOver-reachable, an explicit `.accessibilityLabel("Dismiss search")` + `.isButton` trait so it isn't silent. Either answer is fine; the TRD currently gives neither, and neither does the C9 build step. Not escalated to REQUEST CHANGES — the fix is mechanical (one modifier + one §9 sub-row), doesn't touch D1/D2/D3's architecture, and the same gap sits unresolved in the already-`trd-review`-cleared T-032, so blocking T-038 alone on it would be inconsistent. Flagged here as the must-fix-before-`build` item.
- **Everything else checked and clean:** §4.1's `NavSurface`/`MapChromeState` reproduction verified byte-for-byte against the live `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1 (both v3) — identical, so the "stale by definition" risk §12 names does not apply as of this read. D8/§4.6's presentation-exclusivity calls (`closeHood()` on open and on every leave path) match T-036's own D8/§4.6 verbatim, correctly cited. §9's accessibility row (44pt frames, pinned VoiceOver strings, Dynamic Type growth, pin-shape distinction) covers everything except the z3 gap above. Touch targets, placeholder-as-label ban, colour-only chip state, Reduce Motion — all correctly specced per `design-principles.md` §2/§3. No security/privacy surface (no network call, no persistence, confirmed by the TRD's own `grep`-shaped §9 pass conditions) — vibe-security pass N/A, nothing to find.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** D1 is sound and independently re-derivable; the one gap (z3 tap-catcher accessibility) is a one-line addition, not a structural objection. Routes to `qa` per the lifecycle once both `trd-review` signoffs are in — `ios-developer`'s parallel review not read before this verdict, per dispatch instruction; `chief-of-staff` should reconcile the two before flipping `BOARD.md`'s T-038 row to `build`.
- **Left behind:** (1) the z3 tap-catcher accessibility gap, above — cheapest fixed as a one-line TRD amendment before `build`, or as an explicit note in the `ios-developer` build dispatch if the TRD isn't reopened for it. (2) The same gap exists in T-032's already-cleared scrim spec; not this task's to fix, named here per `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 rather than left unrecorded — worth a line in whatever eventually resolves `PAS-16`. (3) `BOARD.md` not touched (task instruction) — T-038's row still needs `chief-of-staff` to record this verdict and reconcile against `ios-developer`'s independent signoff.
- **Evidence:** review is a read-only pass, no code/doc changes beyond this entry. **Git — corrected in place, heavily concurrent tree (rule 2):** this entry was written, then found already landed on `main` at `passenger-brain 24f0a853` before this session could commit it separately — that commit's own message ("T-035/PAS-26 ios-code-reviewer: APPROVE WITH MINORS on passenger-code 7e40ace") doesn't mention carrying this entry, so disclosing it here: `git show 24f0a853 -- agent-os/PROGRESS.md` confirms this entry's full text is in that diff, swept in from the shared working tree by that concurrent session's commit rather than authored by it. No separate commit made here since nothing was left uncommitted by the time this was checked. No push — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9.

Independent pass, done before reading `code-reviewer`'s parallel entry above (in this file's insertion order, below mine) — reached my own verdict from source, then compared.
- **A1, finding (1) — T-042's B1 does not currently specify a concrete value set; T-037's TRD does not claim otherwise.** Read T-042's TRD directly rather than taking T-037's word: §3.1's `place_types` table schema gives only illustrative SQL-comment examples (`'cafe', 'bar', 'restaurant', 'museum', 'park', ...` and `'coffee-cup'` for `sticker_shape`), and the actual enumeration is B1 in T-042's own build breakdown — *"Propose the initial `place_type` enumeration and its `sticker_shape` mapping, for Aviran's ratification. Not invented by whoever writes A1."* B1 is unexecuted: `BOARD.md` line 96 has T-042 `build`-ready but **HELD**, not dispatched. So there is no ratified registry to pull values from today. T-037's TRD is accurate about this rather than overstating it — §3.4 states the file is "provisional until Aviran ratifies T-042 B1," the `_note` field says PROVISIONAL, and §10's risk table names the exact failure mode ("A1's provisional shape mapping hardens into the ratified vocabulary by default") with its mitigation (routed to `data-engineer`, not `ios-developer`, so the value set isn't invented at build time by whoever writes Swift). Net: A1 is *provisionally doing the same judgment call B1 will later formalize*, disclosed as such at every site that matters, not a violation of the "not invented" rule.
- **A1, finding (2) — the bundled-vs-fetched deviation (D4) is a sound engineering call, correctly flagged, but still an open cross-task loose end.** T-042 §4.5 specs `GET /rest/v1/place_types?select=id,sticker_shape`; T-037's D4 says the registry is bundled and never fetched, permanently in V1, and names this a deviation "flagged for T-042's reviewers," not yet resolved on that side. Sanity-checked the reasoning: a `sticker_shape` key is only useful if the binary ships a matching asset, so fetching buys a key the app can't draw while adding a request/cache field/skew class — bundling keeps vocabulary and assets in one artifact and turns the totality check (C2) into a real build gate. The TRD calls it "reversible in one loader conformance" if overruled, which is accurate — no other contract in the document depends on the transport being one or the other. **Not a blocker for A1/A2** — Phase 1 is bundle-only for this feature regardless of how the debate resolves — but T-042's TRD already cleared `trd-review` approved-as-is with the fetch contract in it (`BOARD.md` line 70), so this is a live disagreement between two approved documents that exists only as prose in each, with no board row and no owner assigned to resolve it. Also confirmed the six `place_type` values in that same file (`bar`, `cafe`, `landmark`, `market`, `museum`, `restaurant`) match A1's build-breakdown row exactly. (2) **A2 correctly builds on the current source.** Decoded `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` directly: T-035's A1 edits are already live (`florentin.isTouristTrap: true`, `kerem-hateimanim.isTouristTrap: true`, `ramat-aviv.isTouristTrap: false`), and `designatedForProgression` reads `false` on all 24 rows today — exactly the starting state §3.5/A2's build row describe, and the "never a pre-A1 copy" instruction is explicit in both places. (3) **Migration `006` regenerates and stays unapplied.** `006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` already exists (T-035's A1 regeneration) and `build_hoods.py` (read directly, not assumed) has no DB connectivity of any kind — pure file generation from `--migration-number`, confirmed by the script's own argument parsing and the `main()`/`build_bundle()`/migration-writer functions — so a second regeneration cannot become an apply by construction. **No `[Backend]` step confirmed independently:** `database/migrations/003_hood_attributes.sql` already added `designated_for_progression boolean not null default false` — read directly, line 45 — so A2 needs no new column and no RLS; it only flips existing boolean values on 3 of 24 rows and re-runs the generator. `developer` correctly absent from the sign-off list.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-developer — T-038/PAS-29 `trd-review`: **APPROVE**

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` v1 (`ios-developer` half; `ios-code-reviewer` running the same TRD in parallel, both signoffs required). Read first, in full: `BOARD.md`'s T-038 row, `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-03 architect T-038 delivery entry (the one this reviews), and the T-038 dispatch brief's own framing of D1 — then set the framing aside and re-derived the call from source rather than taking either side's word for it.
- **D1 (the one thing to scrutinize hardest) — independently verified, concur with the TRD's construction.** Checked T-032's live `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §8 D2 directly: *"a SwiftUI layer inside `MapScreen`'s `ZStack` at z5, below `MapNavRow` at z7, with an explicit scrim at z3. Not `.sheet`, not `.fullScreenCover`, not a `UIViewControllerRepresentable`, and not `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` (there is no presentation). The nav row must stay hit-testable or `ux-flows.md` §2.1's direct nav-switch requirement fails."* T-038's TRD quotes this **character-for-character** (§11 D1) — not paraphrased, not selectively cut in a way that changes its meaning. Then judged the harder question myself: is search actually the same "nav surface" case, or different enough that the PRD/`ux-flows.md`/dispatch brief's "reuse T-033's `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` sheet pattern" instruction should stand instead. Three independent facts say it's the same case, not a different one: (1) `.search` is already a member of T-032's own `NavSurface` enum (`search, heat, places, profile`) — modeled as a nav surface before this TRD was ever written, not an inference the TRD invented to make D2 apply; (2) the PRD's own req 1 bullet 4 — *"opening search closes whichever of {heat modal, Places list, Profile} is open"* — groups search into the identical exclusivity set as the three surfaces D2 already governs; (3) `ux-flows.md` §2.1's own 2026-07-30 addendum (bucket 1) requires the nav row to render *"in a stacking layer above the modal card, never covered"* for exactly this same set — **search included by name** (*"search sheet, heat modal, Places list, and the new Profile tab"*) — and a system `.sheet` cannot satisfy "something else renders above it, never covered" by construction; a sheet is topmost. Read this as an internal tension inside `ux-flows.md` itself, not just an upstream-vs-downstream conflict: the same document's bucket-1 rule (nav row never covered) and its later mechanism note (reuse T-033's `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` for the search sheet, written in the same addendum) can't both hold for a bottom-anchored nav row — T-032's D2 is what actually resolves that tension, correctly, and T-038 inherits the resolution rather than re-opening it. T-036 already set this precedent for the second nav surface (`prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` line 317, and its "Alternatives considered" list explicitly rejects `.sheet()` "for the same reason as T-032 D2") and cleared `trd-review` on it. **Verdict: D1 holds up under independent scrutiny; not taken on the TRD's own citation.**
- **D3 checked against T-032's live behavior, not just this TRD's prose.** T-032's z3 tap-catcher opacity is a **per-surface** value, not a global one — for `.search` it's opacity-0-but-hit-testing, and D3 states explicitly *"T-032's heat modal keeps its visible scrim unchanged."* Confirmed this is structurally sound: nothing about `.search`'s new opacity=0 case removes or overwrites the existing visible-scrim case for `.heat`, so T-032's own z3 behavior when search isn't open is untouched. Resolves the actual conflict correctly — "dim everything except matches" (req 4) cannot be a uniform scrim (it would darken the matches too), so the visual channel moves to a selective per-layer dim at z0 and z3 keeps only its two structural jobs (tap-outside dismiss, blocking a stray tap from opening a Hood sheet underneath).
- **§9 row 2's "not checkable in Phase 1" line is honestly represented, not a quiet waiver.** The P0 requirement itself (one field matches three kinds, with a real pass/fail on real seed data) is fully tested against what actually ships (`"shakshuka"`, `"coffee"`, `"brunch"`); only strategy's own probe words (*"hummus"*, *"rooftop bar"*) — absent from both bundled seeds — are recorded as not-checkable, with a named owner (T-042 step B4, Build Phase 2) rather than faked against placeholder data or silently dropped from the table.
- **Verdict: APPROVE.** No blocking findings. The routing claim (all 14 steps `[iOS]`, no backend/algo-data step) is argued three ways in §10 against T-042's already-approved migration and contract, not inferred from the PRD alone, and I didn't find a hole in it. The TRD's own flagged items — `PAS-16`'s still-unclaimed split (system-sheet vs. nav-surface centralization), D11's cross-check on T-036 §4.6's reachability claim, and the five `[ASSUMPTION]`s surfaced for `product` (D2 layout, D8, D9, D10, §4.10's undimmed base tiles) — are correctly left as open items for `product`/`chief-of-staff`, not defects this gate should bounce for.
- **Evidence:** read `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` (598 lines, full), `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §8 D2/D1 and §2.3/§4.1 (live source), `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` line 317 and its Alternatives list, `design/ux-flows.md` §1/§2/§2.1 in full, `prds/search-quick-filters/search-quick-filters.md` in full (req 1–8, Technical design, Open questions). No code exists yet — doc-only pass, no build run.
- **Left behind:** nothing new. `ios-code-reviewer`'s independent pass is the second required signoff; if it disagrees on D1, that's a real disagreement between two independent reads of the same source and should be argued from source, not deferred to this entry.
