### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-034/PAS-25 code review: `passenger-code 6139f13` — **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Scope:** reviewed `passenger-code 6139f13` (`git show`/`git diff 9608617..6139f13`) against `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` — the new `Events/` module, `Map/EventLayer.swift`, `Detail/EventDetailModal.swift`, the `EventMarker` colorset, `Resources/events-tel-aviv-seed.json`, and the disclosed carried edits to `DetailRouter.swift` (unmixed, this task's own), `MapScreen.swift`/`BuildPhase.swift`/`DetailRouterTests.swift` (mixed with concurrent T-036 content, correctly disclosed in the commit message). Did not review the live working tree's separate in-flight T-032 changes (`SettingsHint.swift`, `HeatRepaintSignpost.swift`) — out of scope, untouched. C12 correctly held per TRD §11's own gating (`HeatModalCard` genuinely absent at commit time). No `vibe-security` findings — no auth/RLS/payments/storage/LLM/rate-limit surface in this diff (events are third-party venue data, no user location, confirmed by grep — no `LocationStore` import in `Events/`, matching TRD §3.3/§9 row 3).
- **TRD conformance:** checked D1 (overlap binding), D3 (cap=12), D6 (`DetailRouter` third depth-1 destination, mutual exclusivity now bidirectional per the fold-in), D7 (`EventHitTester` + event-first precedence in `handleTap`), D8/D9 (no disk cache, second `BuildPhase` constant, `live → empty` never `→ seed`) all against shipped source, not the commit message's own claims. All match the TRD. §4.4's "rank read for exactly one purpose" holds — grepped, `rank` appears only in `EventSelection`'s sort comparator. §2.3's "`Annotation` only, no `MapPolygon`" holds in `EventLayer.swift`.
- **Finding (already resolved on `main`, not blocking): `EventSeed.swift`'s `CodingKeys` bug shipped live in this commit.** At `6139f13`, `SeedFile.Entry.CodingKeys` mapped `latitude`/`longitude` to themselves (`case id, name, latitude, longitude, rank, category`), but both the shipped `events-tel-aviv-seed.json` and every test fixture use `lat`/`lng`. Because `JSONDecoder` fails the whole-struct decode on a missing key (not a per-row `compactMap` drop), `EventSeed.events(anchorHour:)` would throw on every call in this commit — `EventStore.load()` would report `.unavailable`/`events == []` unconditionally in Build Phase 1, silently killing reqs 1–4 and 6 (an empty overlay, indistinguishable from the honest req-5 empty state it's designed to also produce). Traced directly: `EventStoreTests.sourceIsSeedAfterLoad` (loads the real bundled resource via `Bundle.main`), `EventSeedShippedFixtureTests`' whole suite, and `PassengerUITests/EventDetailSheetInteractionTests` (looks up `eventMarker-seed-0001` in the accessibility tree) would all have failed had `xcodebuild test` run clean at commit time — it didn't, blocked by the unrelated concurrent `DebugVisitSource.swift` Swift 6 error named in the commit message, so the bug shipped uncaught by any gate. **Already fixed** by a later, separate commit already on `main` ahead of this one — `passenger-code 24eb533` ("fix: EventSeed CodingKeys used latitude/longitude, JSON ships lat/lng"), a clean two-line `CodingKeys` remap, verified correct by direct read. Reported here because it's a real defect that existed in the exact commit under review and because "compiled clean" and "the seed exercises the real code" (TRD §3.4) were not actually true at that commit despite the worklog's phrasing — not blocking, since the fix has already landed and is verified.
- **Minor:** `EventDetailModal.rowView(for: .hood)` renders the raw `hoodID` slug (e.g. `"kerem-hateimanim"`) as user-facing text instead of a display name — disclosed honestly in-code as a deliberate TRD §4.7 scope limit (no environment injection for a Hood lookup), not a silent gap. Cosmetic; flagged for the post-ship `designer` pass rather than blocking here.
- **Minor:** the VoiceOver marker-label format (`"\(event.name), event, \(event.timeLabel)"`) has no unit/UI test asserting the exact string — a pre-existing, non-blocking gap already named at `trd-review` (`BOARD.md` T-034 row), not introduced by this build, not re-blocking here.
- **Maintainability/HIG:** high bar held throughout — pure functions (`EventSelection`, `EventHitTester`, `EventDetailRows`) tested with no simulator; `EventLayer`/marker mirror the shipped `PlaceLayer` idiom exactly; 44×44pt hit targets on both the marker and the modal's close/directions buttons; system fonts throughout (Dynamic Type intact); `EventMarker` colorset resolved against the real asset catalog and tested ≥3:1 against every `HeatBand` fill in both appearances (`EventLayerContrastTests`), not hardcoded hex; shape (rounded rect vs. `PlaceLayer`'s circle) carries the place/event distinction through greyscale, not colour alone (req 1 bullet 4). No retain cycles, no main-thread violations, no force-unwraps on untrusted input (row/seed decode failures drop cleanly). Test coverage is thorough and mirrors the shipped `Place`/`Hood` test idioms — `DetailRouterTests`' 8 new cases directly verify the trd-review fold-in fix (bidirectional mutual exclusivity) rather than asserting it by inspection.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** Route to `qa` — areas that deserve behavioral testing: the D7 tap-race (event marker vs. Hood sheet, `EventDetailSheetInteractionTests` already covers the automatable half), the §3.4 fixture's 8-clause authoring rule at real launch (visual spot-check across a few hour offsets), the toggle-off state once C12 lands (currently untestable, blocked on T-032), and Dynamic Type / VoiceOver pass on `EventDetailModal` since no automated check exists for the label format. Full green `xcodebuild test` should be re-confirmed by `qa` given the CodingKeys history above, even though the fix is already on `main`.
- **Left behind:** none new. C12 remains correctly blocked on T-032's `HeatModalCard`. `product`'s four open scope calls (D1, D4, D10, D2) and `data-engineer`'s §4.2 confirmation are unchanged by this review and remain outstanding per the architect's TRD-delivery entry.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — this entry only, explicit path (`agent-os/PROGRESS.md`). This is a heavily concurrent working tree (rule 2): this entry was written, then found already landed on `main` at `passenger-brain 88abc1c` (a concurrent `ios-developer` session's own commit, "ios-developer trd-review on T-038/PAS-29", which swept in three uncommitted entries present at commit time — the `code-reviewer` T-037 A1/A2 entry, the `ios-developer` T-038 entry, and this one) before this session got a chance to commit it separately — confirmed via `git show 88abc1c -- agent-os/PROGRESS.md` and by re-reading `HEAD`'s current top entries, not assumed. Not re-staged here since git already carries them; naming it here per rule 2's carried-content convention.
- **Evidence:** full app build clean (`xcodebuild ... build`, exit 0, 0 errors, twice — once mid-pass, once final). Full `PassengerTests` run: every new suite (`TouristFlag / FlagStroke`, `FlagCopy / HoodSpeech`, `MapZoomTier`, `LocalQAGate`, `LocalQAAnswerStore`, `LocalQAPersistence`, `LocalQACoordinator`, `SyncState.confirmationCopy`, `DebugVisitSource`, `LocalQAInstallIdentity`, `Flag contrast (§11 C4)`, `Place.isTouristTrap decode`) passes; the only 18 failures in a 253-test run are pre-existing in T-034's own `EventSeedDecodeTests`/`EventSeedShippedFixtureTests`/`EventStoreTests` (`events-tel-aviv-seed.json` missing a `latitude` key) — unrelated to this task, confirmed by root-cause grep, flagged as a separate spawned task rather than fixed here. `PassengerUITests/LocalQAToastInteractionTests` (C14): both tests pass against a live `iPhone 17` simulator run. One real bug caught and fixed during this pass: `DebugVisitSource`'s `now: @escaping () -> Date` parameter needed `@Sendable` — Swift 6 strict concurrency correctly flagged the original as a data-race risk (`Task {}` capturing a non-`Sendable` closure); fixed, rebuilt clean. A second, environment-only issue (not a code defect) was found and the *test* adjusted rather than the code: `LocalQAPersistence`'s file-protection write uses the correct `.completeFileProtectionUntilFirstUserAuthentication` option, but the iOS Simulator has no Secure Enclave/passcode, so `FileManager`'s `.protectionKey` reliably reports `nil` back regardless of what was requested — the test was rewritten to assert what's actually observable in this environment (successful write + round-trip) rather than the unreliable resource value.
- **Left behind:** (1) T-034's `events-tel-aviv-seed.json`/its test fixture is missing a `latitude` key — spawned as a separate background task rather than fixed here (out of this task's scope, and touching T-034's own fixture mid-flight would repeat the exact collision risk this pass spent effort avoiding). (2) §9 row 8(f)'s live VoiceOver-focus sampling and the whole req-5 busy+flagged live check both still need their stated preconditions (a real Accessibility Inspector pass; T-032's C10 density seed) before `qa` can close them — named above, not silently left for `qa` to rediscover. (3) Not touching `BOARD.md`/Linear per this dispatch's scope — `chief-of-staff`'s to move to `code-review`.

Three independent reasons make search the same case: a `.sheet` covers the nav row and breaks the exclusivity rule PRD req 1 bullet 4 restates; `MapScreen`'s one `.sheet` slot is already the depth-1 destination site that **PRD req 5 needs free**; and req 4's *selective* dim is a different thing from a sheet's own background treatment. **The T-033 pattern is still reused, exactly and unchanged — on the destination sheet a result opens**, which is the shipped Site A modifier, untouched. Nothing is reinvented; it is applied where it belongs.

### 2026-08-03 — architect — T-037/PAS-28 TRD v1 delivered: Passport routes to **two** pairs, not one — the `[Algo/Data]` half is the two numbers the PRD says it cannot ship without

- **Did:** wrote `prds/passport/TRD.md` (v1, 523 lines). Read first, in full: `BOARD.md`'s T-037 row and lifecycle section, `prds/passport/passport.md`, T-036's whole TRD (not just the §4.2/§3.4/§7 sections the dispatch named), T-042's TRD for the `place_type`/`place_types`/`sticker_shape` contract, T-032's live §4.1 and §4.10/D1, `design/ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1/§5/§7/§9 Q13, `map-rendering-spec.md` §7, and the shipped `passenger-code` models, catalogs and bundles. D1–D12, §9 verification table (one row per P0, 8 rows), build steps A1–A2 + C1–C13. No `/grilling` pass — autonomous dispatch, no human in the loop to answer; unresolved calls are tagged **[ASSUMPTION]** and surfaced for review instead.
- **Routes to TWO pairs, and this is the finding worth reading first.** `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer` for C1–C13, **and `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer` for A1/A2.** The dispatch predicted iOS-only and said to verify rather than assume — it isn't. **A1** is the `place_type` → `sticker_shape` mapping, whose value set T-042's own step B1 explicitly forbids being invented by whoever writes the code ("Not invented by whoever writes A1" — the same rule binds the Swift). **A2** is the designated-Hood set, which must go through `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` + a `build_hoods.py` re-run, never a hand-edit of the generated client bundle (L-024). Same shape and same routing as T-035's own A1. **No `[Backend]` step** — no hand-written SQL, no column, no RLS; A2 *regenerates* the already-unapplied `006` and leaves it unapplied and Aviran-gated. `developer` is deliberately not on the sign-off list, per T-035's A1 precedent; if `code-reviewer` disagrees that's a one-line routing add, not a TRD change.
- **The real blocker nobody owned: `designated_for_progression` is `false` on all 24 shipped Hoods.** Checked the bundle directly rather than trusting the schema — `Hood.designatedForProgression` already exists on the shipped Swift model and already decodes, and every row is `false`, because T-040's PRD scoped populating it out ("not this task's to populate") and no task picked it up. Left alone, Passport's entire progression half renders empty on a real device and req 4 has nothing to check. **A2 seeds a provisional set — `florentin`, `kerem-hateimanim`, `neve-tzedek`, derived not invented** (the only three Hoods with curated places) — **and C12 makes "at least one designated Hood in the shipped bundle" a build-time assertion**, so the empty state cannot ship silently. Deliberately **no placeholder UI is designed for it**: a state the build must not ship is a gate, not a screen.
- **The Local threshold, the PRD's other "cannot ship without it" number:** one constant, `LocalStatus.threshold`, **provisionally 2, [ASSUMPTION], flagged for Aviran** (D6). The value is argued from observability against data that actually ships, not from product judgement — with T-036's fixture (two `been` places, both in `kerem-hateimanim`) and A2's three Hoods, 2 makes Local-reached, plain-zero and overall-not-reached all observable on device, and sits at or below every designated Hood's curated place count so no Hood is Local-unreachable by construction. "Configuration" is read narrowly as one declaration site, not a plist or a server table, and §4.2 says why rather than leaving it inferred.
- **A genuine contradiction between two approved documents, resolved and flagged, not papered over (D12).** PRD req 7 bullet 1 wants a sticker's VoiceOver label to name "the place and its type"; T-042 §4.4 says no user-facing surface renders `place_type` and its only V1 consequence is a sticker's *shape*. A VoiceOver label is a user-facing surface, so both cannot hold literally. Resolved toward parity — the label names **what the sticker depicts**, from a client-side `StickerShape.spokenName`, the same idiom as `PlaceCategory.displayName`. The word lives on the client enum, **not** in the registry file and not in `place_types`, so T-042 §3.1's deliberate no-display-string-on-the-server rule stays intact. **Flagged for `product`** as a stated deviation from the requirement's wording.
- **One contract simplification handed back to T-042 (D4): the shape registry is bundled and never fetched, permanently in V1.** T-042 §4.5 offers `GET /rest/v1/place_types?select=id,sticker_shape`. A `sticker_shape` key is only useful if the binary contains something to draw for it, so a key arriving over the wire that the shipped app has never heard of renders generic no matter how it arrived — fetching the mapping buys a key the app cannot draw, while adding a request, a cache field and a skew class. Bundling keeps vocabulary and assets in one artifact and turns the totality check into a build gate. **Flagged for T-042's reviewers**; reversible in one loader conformance.
- **Other calls worth naming:** Passport adds **no store, no persistence, no network and no `CoreLocation`** — three P0s (no social surface, sticker-present-after-relaunch, degraded permission/offline) are satisfied by absence of code and §9 checks them as absence rather than dressing them as runtime behaviour (D1, §3.6). `Place.placeType` is a non-optional **`String`, not a Swift enum** — T-042 chose a table over a Postgres enum so adding a type is an insert, and a closed client enum would turn every such insert into a dropped row (D2). Hood attribution reads `place.hoodID`, never a fourth client-side ray-cast, which also answers the PRD's polygon-change question: re-attribution happens at dataset export (D5). **No in-the-moment surfacing ships** — `ux-flows.md` Q13 stays open and req 6 passes anyway, because deriving stickers on read means there is no event to fire (D8). No new `BuildPhase` constant: `placeType` rides `seedIsAuthoritative` because it is the same axis, a deliberate contrast with T-036's and T-034's own constants, whose axes genuinely differed (§7).
- **Wrote the TRD against the live tree, not against `HEAD`, and it changed two sections.** T-036's build is in flight and **uncommitted** in the shared `passenger-code` tree — `MapChromeState.swift`, `VisitedPlacesStore.swift`, `BundledVisitSource.swift`, `PlaceProvenance.swift`, `place-visits-tel-aviv.json` all present as untracked files, `Place.swift` already carrying `permanentlyClosed`, `MutedOnSurface.colorset` already created — while `MapNavRow.swift` is confirmed absent (T-032's C2 has not landed). §2.2 now states that as read fact with a date, keeps the create-if-absent fallbacks so it holds either way, and adds an explicit instruction that C1 adds `placeType` **beside** T-036's field and never re-derives those four files from `HEAD`, which would silently revert uncommitted work. Same for A2: T-035's A1 edited `hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` earlier today, so A2 must edit the current file and regenerate from it, never from a pre-A1 copy.
- **Evidence:** `python3` inspection of `Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` (24 Hoods, `designatedForProgression` true count = **0**) and `places-tel-aviv.json` (9 places, `place_type` present on all, six distinct values `bar`/`cafe`/`landmark`/`market`/`museum`/`restaurant`, hood ids `florentin`/`kerem-hateimanim`/`neve-tzedek`); `Hood.swift`, `Place.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, `PlaceCategory.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `AppConfig.swift`, `HoodCatalog.swift`, `MapScreen.swift` read directly; `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1 read at source and reproduced verbatim into §4.6 (the mismatch that sent T-036's v1 back); `grep` for `designated_for_progression` across `database/` confirming migration `003`/`006`, `build_hoods.py`, `validate_dataset.py` and all 24 source rows; `git status` on both repos. No build run — doc-only pass, and C1–C13 have no code yet.
- **Left behind — three items this TRD cannot fix and one it cannot even file.** (1) The **shared dwell/geofence detector** is still ownerless, now named by T-035, T-036 *and* T-037 with no board row. (2) **`hoods.designated_for_progression`** has no owner for its real values — T-040 disclaimed it, A2 only ships a provisional set. (3) The **Local threshold** has no owner. Per `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 each needs a `BOARD.md` row with an owner rather than another worklog line saying "flagged for whoever owns it" — **but this dispatch forbids touching `BOARD.md`, so `chief-of-staff` must create them; naming them here is not a substitute and I am not treating it as one.** Also carried forward untouched, all three PRD findings the dispatch named: `place_type` user-facing or not (Aviran, `PAS-6` item 12); nothing rewards answering local-QA (pipeline-wide incentive risk, not this task's); device-local loss on reinstall (unconfirmed-acceptable). For `product` at review: D4, D6's value, D7's set, D8, D12, and §4.4's name-ascending album order (chronological rejected — `first_observed_at` is not on the seam T-036 exposes, and widening it would reopen a cleared contract). **State not flipped and Linear not touched** — `chief-of-staff`'s.

- **Window.** Per L-020's step-0 rule, read `LESSONS.md`'s newest header first: `## 2026-08-02`, written by a run that closed at 19:21. The day did not stop there — **45 more `passenger-brain` commits and 3 `passenger-code` commits landed between 19:00 and 20:51**, so the window opened at 19:20 rather than at midnight. The tree went live again mid-run (`75d8926` project-manager 00:07, `d6ab04b` analytics-engineer 00:10); both recorded as found, neither chased.
- **The night's theme, and why it stopped being deferrable.** Last night's report named the concurrency rate and deferred it as "infrastructural, not a rule gap." Within four hours it cost **duplicated work and lost work on the same incident**: two sessions in two isolated worktrees regenerated the *byte-identical* time-slider HTML twins ten minutes apart (`9c1006c` 20:27, `5dfd812` 20:37 — `git diff` between them is empty), and **neither reached `main`**, along with a third stranded commit (`03dab7d`). All four stale HTML twins are still stale on `main` tonight. Separately, two `data-engineer` sessions independently completed T-035/PAS-26's A1 `trd-review`, both APPROVE WITH MINORS — "that's luck, not process," as the second one wrote when it found the first.
- **L-026 — an isolated worktree delivers nothing** (process, general, applied). "I committed it" reads the same whether the commit is on `main` or on a branch nobody will check out, so the author gets no signal. → `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md` **new rule 10**: a commit is delivered when it is on `main`; merge the `claude/*` branch before the turn ends, or declare it stranded in the report *and* a `BOARD.md` row. → `project-manager.md` **check F** gains a stranded-branch sweep in both repos, with two branches touching the same files as a separate hit. Detection is a dispatch-chief-of-staff item, not a fix-silently one — merging someone else's branch is a content call.
- **L-027 — a claim at task granularity doesn't lock work dispatched at step granularity** (process, general, applied). PAS-26 splits `trd-review` across three pairs; its `owner:*` labels and BOARD.md owner field describe the *task*, so both sessions read a correctly-claimed row and both dispatched A1. L-007's "re-read fresh before dispatching" was followed and could not help — the row never recorded which step was taken. → `chief-of-staff.md`: a split task's BOARD.md row carries **one claim line per step**, and those lines, not the owner field, are what gets read before dispatching a step. The second half is the same failure at zero — the twin regeneration was never a row at all, flagged twice as "for whoever owns it" — → `CLAUDE.md` **rule 5** extended: a defect you find but don't own becomes a row with a name on it, not a remark.
- **L-023 — re-fixed, previously `applied` → `failed`.** Rule 2's "unstage the file and say whose it is" landed at 19:23; in the next ninety minutes **four commits each carried other sessions' worklog entries** (`0228391`, `d3fa249` with six, `4a96756`, `96b293b`), and `2c823ba` is a commit whose entire content is a line disclosing one of them. The detection half worked — agents noticed every time. The remedy half **cannot be obeyed**: unstaging `PROGRESS.md` means your own entry never lands. Every agent independently invented the same substitute, which makes the written rule the thing that's wrong. → `CLAUDE.md` **rule 2** carve-out: for an append-only shared log, commit it and name every carried entry and its author agent **in the original message**; a follow-up disclosure commit is the failure that replaces. Watch item: a fifth undisclosed sweep means the single-file worklog itself is the problem, and the next fix is structural — too large to auto-apply.
- **Verified:** **L-018** (TRD §9 Verification, applied last night) is the fastest adoption in `LESSONS.md` — present in all four TRDs touched tonight, including a retrofit into T-032's, and already used as a gate (`ios-code-reviewer` blocked T-035 partly on a §10 citation with no matching §9 row). **L-021** clean: zero hits for stale `origin brain`/`HEAD:brain`/`locali` references across the live agent tree and both mirrors. **L-017** survived the design-gate pivot editing agent files after last night's sweep — three-way `diff -qr` clean apart from the known `agents-mirror/chief.md` orphan. **L-022/L-024/L-025/L-007/L-011/L-002** all held; details in `LESSONS.md`'s step-4 section.
- **Left behind:** (1) **Three stranded commits on two `claude/*` branches** in `passenger-brain`, and four stale HTML twins on `main` — not merged by this run (hygiene is `project-manager`/`chief-of-staff`'s, and reviewing three sessions' regenerated documents is a content call). Tomorrow's check F will catch it; naming it here so it doesn't wait. (2) **`passenger-code` has uncommitted T-032 build work** (`Passenger/Map/SettingsHint.swift` modified, `Passenger/Support/HeatRepaintSignpost.swift` untracked) — a live session's, left untouched. (3) **No PM digest was available** when evidence was gathered; `nightly-pm-audit` fired 23:18 and was still in flight, landing `75d8926` at 00:07. Not a failure — noted because L-020's flagged-for-Aviran liveness alert would have answered "running or dead?" in a second instead of by process inspection. (4) `agents-mirror/chief.md`, the mis-filed `/chief` slash command, still has no live counterpart — `project-manager`'s check G, unchanged from last night.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `CLAUDE.md`, `agent-os/LESSONS.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/chief-of-staff.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/project-manager.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), all by explicit path. **`agent-os/BOARD.md` was already staged in the index by a concurrent session (8 insertions / 8 deletions) and was not touched, staged, or committed** — rule 2. `passenger-code` — `.claude/agents/chief-of-staff.md`, `.claude/agents/project-manager.md` (the mirror copies only); the two in-flight Swift files above deliberately left alone. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.

- **Finding 1 — the one that needed a decision, not a text fix. New §8 D9: the two install ids stay deliberately separate.** `analytics/EVENTS.md` line 13 (`passenger-brain 2e38e4b`, one day older than this TRD) states that every table keys on *"the same identifier `local_qa_answers` already uses"*; §3.3 generated its own UUID into `local-qa.json`. Two generation paths, one claimed identifier. **I read `EVENTS.md` myself and made the call the reviewers asked for rather than picking the cheaper edit** — and the deciding fact is one neither review named: **`EVENTS.md`'s own local-QA KPI never touches `local_qa_answers`.** "Local-QA health" is `local_qa_toast_answered / (answered + ignored)`, and both are rows in **`app_events`**, keyed on the analytics id. No rollup in that document reads the answers table at all, so the shared id buys a join nothing consumes — while costing the ability to join a person's full behavioural stream (`app_events` FKs on `app_installs.install_id`) to their physical-visit history. `local_qa_answers.install_id` is a **dedup key** for the `(place_id, install_id)` PK, never selected by any role; anything stable per install satisfies it. The PRD (line 88) asks only for *"an anonymous install id"*, so separation contradicts no requirement. **One more supporting find, mine:** the upstream `analytics-engineer` agent file says *"the same **pattern** `local_qa_answers` already uses"* — **pattern**, not identifier. `EVENTS.md` line 13 tightened that into "the same identifier" while citing a PRD that never said it, which reads as a drafting slip rather than an identity decision.
- **What D9 does about the doc it contradicts, rather than contradicting it silently:** names `analytics/EVENTS.md` **line 13** as needing its own correction by `analytics-engineer`, and offers replacement text in D9 rather than editing another agent's file. Added `analytics-engineer` to §11's `trd-review` sign-off list — new at v2 — so the owner of the contradicted doc gets a say *before* real answers are collected under either scheme. **Second, separate correction flagged to the same owner, found while checking the first:** `EVENTS.md` line 22 says `install_id` is *"persisted in SwiftData"* — `grep -rn "SwiftData\|@Model" passenger-code/Passenger/` returns **zero hits**; the app uses no SwiftData anywhere and every shipped store is an actor over Application Support JSON. Doesn't affect this TRD, which specifies its own storage, but it's unbacked where it stands.
- **D9's cost is written down, not buried:** you can no longer ask "do installs that answer local-QA prompts retain better?" by joining the two tables. Named the alternative that gets the same insight without the correlation — log the already-catalogued `local_qa_toast_answered` event into `app_events` and analyse it there. Available today, no schema change.
- **Two structural guards so the decision survives contact with `build`,** since the reviewers' own failure scenario was someone "fixing" the mismatch by unifying: the type is renamed **`InstallIdentity` → `LocalQAInstallIdentity`** (§2.1, §3.3, C9) so the distinction is visible at every call site, and §4.4's SQL carries an inline comment pinning **"not an FK to `app_installs`, and must never become one"** for whoever writes `007` at Phase 2.
- **Finding 2 — §2.2 corrected to the invariant that actually holds.** Verified the reviewers' claim myself: `MapScreen.swift:43,60` holds `hoods: [Hood]` and `placeCatalog` today, alongside `densityStore`/`locationStore`/`savedPlacesStore` in the same pattern — so v1's "no type holds a reference to both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" would have made `ios-code-reviewer` block on this TRD's own intended C11/C12 wiring. Rewrote it as a **render-function-signature rule**: `FlagStroke.treatment(for:band:)` and `HoodSpeech.label(name:band:flag:)` never take `LocalQAAnswerStore`, a `LocalQARecord`, or any `LocalQA/` type as an input, and neither owning type stores a reference reaching one. §9 row 9(a) restated to check signatures against §4.1/§4.2. **Both sites now say explicitly that `MapScreen` co-owning both is expected composition-root wiring and is NOT a finding** — so the next reviewer neither false-positives on it nor narrows the rule with no written record, which is the outcome the reviewer specifically warned about.
- **Finding 3 — §9 row 8 gains a real sub-check (f).** v1's §10 cited a VoiceOver-focus check "in §9 row 8"; row 8's (a)–(e) are gate precedence, appears-once, auto-dismiss timing, presenter count, queue state — no focus check anywhere in §9. (f) now names the observable (`UIAccessibility.focusedElement(using: nil)` sampled before presentation and ~1s after, VoiceOver on) and a falsifiable pass condition (same focused element, still traversable; exactly one `UIAccessibility.post` with `.announcement`; zero `.screenChanged`/`.layoutChanged`/`makeKeyAndVisible`/`accessibilityViewIsModal = true` in the presenter), run in **both** the no-sheet and open-sheet cases since the sheet is why D4 needs a window at all. §10's row rewritten to cite 8(f) accurately. **Folded in `ios-code-reviewer`'s addendum, which was the sharper half of the finding:** the notification type alone doesn't protect focus — a `UIWindow` can capture it through UIKit's own hierarchy scan — so **C12 now pins the presentation mechanics as build requirements** (shown via `isHidden = false`, never `makeKeyAndVisible`, `accessibilityViewIsModal` false, level `.normal + 1`) and **C12 is not done until 8(f) passes**, the same "fails at build, not at `qa`" construction T-032's v3 used for C13.
- **Finding 4, folded in and labelled as out-of-scope-but-cheap: §10's "byte-identical" round-trip claim was false for the JSON bundle.** Not from the C1–C14 reviews — carried from the **A1** pass, where `code-reviewer` (`passenger-brain 864b8ea`) and `data-engineer` independently confirmed that `build_hoods.py`'s `build_bundle()` stamps `generatedAt` from wall clock, so the bundle never diffs clean across two runs even with zero content change (the SQL migration does, and that half was true). Corrected the **wording of `qa`'s check only** — A1's build step is untouched and its clearance is not reopened. Left standing, `qa` would have written a check that fails every run and trains its reader to eyeball past the diff, which is the exact L-024 failure mode the check exists to catch.
- **Evidence:** `MapScreen.swift` grep confirming `hoods:[Hood]` (line 43) and `placeCatalog` (line 60); `grep -rn "SwiftData\|@Model" passenger-code/Passenger/` → 0 hits; `analytics/EVENTS.md` read in full (identity constraint line 13, `app_installs` line 22, the KPI rollup list); `agent-os/agents-mirror/analytics-engineer.md:30` for the "pattern" vs "identifier" divergence; `tourist-trap-flag.md:85,88` for what the PRD actually requires; `git log -1 -- analytics/EVENTS.md` → `2e38e4b`. Post-edit self-checks: zero bare `InstallIdentity` left in the TRD (6 `LocalQAInstallIdentity`), every surviving "no type holds both" occurrence is inside explicit "v1 said this and it was false" context, D9 cross-referenced from §2.1/§3.3/§4.4/§10/C9/§11, row 8(f) cross-referenced from D4/§10/C12.
- **Left behind:** (1) **T-035 C1–C14 is ready for a `trd-review` re-run by both `ios-developer` and `ios-code-reviewer`** — scoped to the three fixes, not the whole TRD. **I did not flip the board state**; that's `chief-of-staff`'s. (2) **`analytics-engineer` needs to rule on D9** and correct `EVENTS.md` line 13 (and separately line 22's SwiftData claim) — added to §11's sign-off list; the TRD does not edit that file. (3) Unchanged and still open from v1, none of it touched here: **D3** (req 2 bullet 3 vs req 5 contradiction) and **D5** (the new Hood-sheet surface) still need **`product`**, as does **§1's Phase-1 boundary**; the ownerless shared dwell detector still has no board row; req 5 stays unobservable until T-032's C10 seed lands (T-032 cleared review at `9ef1ce8` while this pass ran, so that unblocks sooner than v1 assumed); the A1 `generatedAt` generator fix is `data-engineer`'s call, described in §10 but not made.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry + one correction), explicit paths, `git diff --staged` re-read hunk by hunk before committing. **Two disclosures rather than leaving them to the diff.** (a) **I edited a historical `PROGRESS.md` entry**: the v1 T-035 delivery entry's `Git:` line cited `a671373`, an orphaned object (confirmed unreachable from `HEAD` via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, not assumed from the review's word); corrected to `a6c1c73` with a bracketed note saying who changed it and why, since `ios-developer` asked for exactly that fix whenever `architect` next touched the entry. Hash only — no other text in that entry altered. (b) **My `PROGRESS.md` hunk unavoidably carries one line that is not mine** — a self-signed rule-2 commit note from `ios-code-reviewer`'s T-032 v3 re-review session (about its body being swept into `9ef1ce8`), left uncommitted in the shared tree when this pass opened the file. Named here rather than read as mine; committing it beats leaving a finished disclosure belonging to nobody (L-017). `agent-os/BOARD.md` was **not** staged by this pass. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.

It was not identical, and the "either order" promise was made unilaterally. **Read T-032's real §4.1 directly rather than trusting the brief** — both the committed version (`git show HEAD:prds/time-slider/TRD.md`) and the working-tree v3-in-flight version, which are identical in this section: `enum NavSurface: String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable { case search, heat, places, profile; var id: String { rawValue } }`, built **unconditionally** at T-032's C1 with no "skip if it exists" clause. Four cases vs. two, four conformances vs. one. There is only ever one shape of this type in the tree, so "whoever builds second must not restructure it" stops being a promise anyone has to keep.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-032/PAS-15 TRD **v3**: all four `trd-review` findings resolved; found the changelog promising a fix the body never received

- **Did:** revised `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` to **v3** against the split `trd-review` verdict — `ios-developer` APPROVE WITH MINORS (`passenger-brain 27faac4`) and `ios-code-reviewer` REQUEST CHANGES (`passenger-brain 7a1f99c`). Both reviews read in full before touching the doc. **No decision, contract or build step redesigned** — D1–D10 all stand as written at v2; every v3 edit is a verification or accuracy fix, which is what both reviews asked for.
- **Blocking finding (`ios-code-reviewer`) — resolved.** §9 row 7 and C13 checked camera-immutability only for an inert horizontal drag (7b) and a pan starting *outside* the band (7c), never for a normal **in-band vertical** slide — the gesture the task exists to ship. Both v2 cases pass whether or not MapKit saw the touch, so D7/§2.4's central hit-test claim had no falsifiable check anywhere. Now: **§9 row 7 gains sub-check (e)** — `selectedHour` sweeps all 13 stops while `camera`/`MKCoordinateRegion` stays byte-identical, sampled at touch-down, mid-drag and `onEnded`, using the same comparison row 2c already uses, on device or in a UI test. **C13 is redefined as not-done until that check passes**, so it fails at build rather than at `qa` or after ship. §2.4, §4.8 rule (a) and D7 now state the hit-test claim as a *prediction this TRD requires confirmed*, not settled fact — the reviewer's point that the doc's own `FB19394663` citation is evidence against taking it on reasoning alone is correct and is now written into the doc. A matching risk row was added to §10 naming the real fallback: if it fails there is no third construction (`Map` exposes no recognizer to arbitrate against), so it becomes a product conversation about the edge path.
- **Three minors — all resolved.** (1) `coordinateSpace: .named("map")` → the default **`.local`**, with the reason recorded so it isn't re-invented at build time; **verified myself, not taken from the review** — `grep -rn "coordinateSpace" passenger-code/Passenger/` returns zero hits, so the named space had no declaring view anywhere. (2) §9 row 6's Step column now reads **`C4` (the ≥44pt frame), C9, C12** so `qa`'s `TEST-PLAN.md` traces to where the assertion is actually built. (3) row 7d / C13's sheet check now exercises **both detents and both touch targets** — four combinations, not one — after confirming at source that `MapScreen.swift:186` sets `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))`, `HoodSheet.swift:26` carries `[.medium, .large]`, and `PlaceDetailModal.swift:22` is `.medium`-only (so `.large` is reachable through the Hood sheet alone). `EdgeAvailability` keeps its one flat rule — this is verification granularity, not a design change.
- **Fifth finding, mine, raised by neither review:** the TRD named the shipped sheet **`HoodStubSheet`** in three places (§1, §9 row 7, §11 footer). **No such type exists** — `Detail/HoodSheet.swift:7` declares `HoodSheet` and `HoodStubSheet` appears nowhere in `passenger-code`. Left standing it would have pointed `ios-developer` and `qa` at a type they cannot find, in the very rows this pass was fixing. Renamed at all three sites; name only, no behaviour or scope change.
- **Shared-working-tree finding, stated plainly rather than left to the diff (rule 2).** When this pass opened the file, the v3 header, the four-row changelog table and the §2.4 / §4.8 / D7 / §9 edits were **already present in the tree, uncommitted** — not this pass's writing, and `TRD.md`'s mtime advanced again mid-pass, so at least one other session was writing the same file concurrently. I did not assume that work was correct and I did not re-do it: each piece was re-read against `27faac4`, `7a1f99c` and the shipped source before being allowed to stand. **Doing that caught a real defect: the changelog claimed "C13's verify-list gains the matching line" and "C13 now exercises both detents" — and C13 was untouched v2 text.** A doc asserting fixes it does not contain would have cleared re-review on its own summary. ### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-035/PAS-26 `trd-review` (C1–C14 only): **REQUEST CHANGES** — install-id conflicts with `analytics/EVENTS.md`, req-9's "no type holds both" is false against the shipped `MapScreen`, and the VoiceOver-focus check §10 cites doesn't exist in §9

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on T-035's C1–C14 `[iOS]` bulk, alongside `ios-developer`'s own pass (both signoffs required; A1/B1 out of scope, covered separately). Read `PROGRESS.md`'s `a6c1c73` architect entry in full, `BOARD.md`'s T-035 row, then `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` and `tourist-trap-flag.md` in full. Per the dispatch's specific asks, checked three things against source rather than against the TRD's own prose: the install-id privacy reasoning (§3.3) for a correlation gap, whether req 9's "no type holds both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" (§2.2) is actually how the module boundaries are drawn, and the toast's VoiceOver-focus behavior (D4). Cross-read `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Detail/HoodSheet.swift`, `Detail/PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `Places/SavedPlacesStore.swift` (confirms `SavedPlacesPersistence` actor shape §3.3 claims to mirror is real), and `passenger-brain/analytics/EVENTS.md`.
- **Finding 1 (blocking) — `InstallIdentity` (TRD §3.3, line 163) silently conflicts with `analytics/EVENTS.md`, which already names this TRD as the source of the identifier it reuses.** `EVENTS.md` (committed 2026-08-01, one day *before* this TRD, `2e38e4b`) states as a load-bearing "Identity constraint": *"Every table keys on an anonymous `install_id` — **the same identifier `local_qa_answers` already uses** (`prds/tourist-trap-flag/tourist-trap-flag.md`)"* (line 13), and defines that id as *"Client-generated, persisted in SwiftData"* (`app_installs.install_id`, line 22). This TRD's `InstallIdentity` is a different, independently-generated UUID that lives **only** inside `local-qa.json` (Application Support JSON, not SwiftData), deliberately walled inside `LocalQA/`, which §2.2 forbids from importing `Hoods/`/`Map/`/either catalog. The TRD never mentions `EVENTS.md`, `app_installs`, or `analytics-engineer` anywhere. Two independent generation paths will not produce the same UUID, so either: (a) `local_qa_answers.install_id` and `app_installs.install_id` silently diverge, breaking the exact analytics join `EVENTS.md` promises (can't correlate a local-QA answer to the rest of that install's funnel), discovered only when `analytics-engineer`/`developer` builds against both docs; or (b) whoever notices the mismatch "fixes" it by making `InstallIdentity` the same value as the app-wide analytics id — which would **defeat this TRD's own minimization design**, since `app_installs.install_id` is also the FK on every row of `app_events` (searches, screens, sessions — the full behavioral stream). Sharing it would let anyone with backend access join a person's entire in-app behavior to their local-QA visit history (place + hour), a materially bigger correlation surface than either table alone — the opposite of what §3.3's careful slug-only/hour-truncated/non-Keychain reasoning is trying to achieve. **Failure scenario:** `build` ships both features against their own docs as written, the two `install_id`s are silently different UUIDs, and the first person to notice is whoever tries to run `EVENTS.md`'s own funnel query joining local-QA behavior to retention — by which point it's a data-migration problem, not a TRD edit. **Fix required before `build`:** the TRD needs an explicit call (a new D9) on whether `InstallIdentity` is deliberately distinct from `app_installs.install_id` (and if so, say why unifying them would be worse, and flag `EVENTS.md`'s line 13 as needing correction) — or, if unification is truly intended, redesign `InstallIdentity`'s storage/ownership accordingly. Either answer is fine; leaving it unaddressed is not.
- **Finding 2 (blocking) — §2.2's "no type holds a reference to both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" (line 78) is not how the module boundaries are actually drawn, checked against the shipped composition root.** `MapScreen.swift:58-62` already holds `placeCatalog` (`PlaceCatalog`, a catalog) as `@State`, alongside `hoods: [Hood]` loaded from `HoodCatalog`. Per §11 C11/C12, `LocalQACoordinator` (which per C9 will hold `LocalQAAnswerStore`) and `LocalQAPresenter` need an owner to wire the `VisitSource` stream and host the toast — and every existing precedent in this exact file (`densityStore`, `locationStore`, `savedPlacesStore`, `placeCatalog` are all `MapScreen`'s own `@State`) points at `MapScreen` doing that too. Once it does, `MapScreen` is a type holding a reference to both a catalog and — transitively, through `LocalQACoordinator` — `LocalQAAnswerStore`, which is exactly what §2.2 says no type may do, and exactly what §9 row 9(a) (line 378) tells `ios-code-reviewer` to give "a blocking review finding" for. As literally written, the TRD's own intended architecture trips its own review gate. **The invariant that actually holds** is narrower and is never stated as the load-bearing one: `FlagStroke.treatment(for:band:)` and `HoodSpeech.label(...)` (§4.1/§4.2) — the only two functions that decide what renders — never take `LocalQAAnswerStore` or any of its data as an input. That's the real, checkable guarantee behind req 9; "no type holds both" is not. **Fix required before `build`:** correct §2.2 and §9 row 9(a) to state and check the render-function-signature invariant (neither `FlagStroke.treatment` nor `HoodSpeech.label` may ever gain a `LocalQAAnswerStore`/answer parameter) rather than the broader claim the codebase's own existing shape already falsifies — otherwise `ios-code-reviewer`'s future pass either false-positives on `MapScreen` or quietly narrows the rule with no written record of having done so.
- **Finding 3 (blocking) — §10's risk table (line 393) claims "§9 row 8 has `qa` confirm focus is not stolen" for D4's new `UIWindow`; §9 row 8 (line 376) has no such check.** Read row 8's full observable/pass-condition columns: (a)–(e) cover `LocalQAGate` precedence, toast-appears-once, auto-dismiss timing, presenter-count, and queue state — nothing about `UIAccessibility`/VoiceOver focus anywhere in the row, nor in any other §9 row. This is the exact "looks right, no gate can fail" shape the TRD's own preamble (line 365, "No row's pass condition is 'looks right'") and L-018 exist to catch — landing on the one row governing D4's own stated highest-risk surface (a second `UIWindow` in an otherwise pure-SwiftUI app). Also worth naming while fixing this: D4 (line 345) only pins the **notification type** (`.announcement`, not `.screenChanged`) as the focus-preserving mechanism, but doesn't name the **window-presentation** mechanics that independently affect VoiceOver focus (whether the window becomes key via `makeKeyAndVisible`, `accessibilityViewIsModal`, window level relative to `.normal`) — a new `UIWindow` can pull VoiceOver focus into itself through UIKit's own hierarchy-change scanning regardless of which notification is explicitly posted. **Fix required before `build`:** add a real row-8(f) — or a dedicated row — with an observable (VoiceOver focus/`UIAccessibility.focusedElement` before vs. after the toast appears, checked via Accessibility Inspector or a UI test) and a falsifiable pass condition, and have C12's implementation notes name the window-presentation choices (key window or not, `accessibilityViewIsModal`) that keep focus from moving, not only the notification type.
- **Everything else checked and found sound:** D1–D8 all independently re-derived from source, not accepted on the TRD's word — `HoodSheet.swift` (read in full) confirms D5's premise exactly (no flag surface anywhere in the file); `PlaceDetailModal.swift:76-86` confirms `touristTrapSlot`'s reserved `EmptyView()` matches C7's target precisely; `SavedPlacesStore.swift`/`SavedPlacesPersistence` (actor, generation-guarded, Application-Support-JSON) confirms §3.3's "follows `SavedPlacesPersistence`'s actor shape exactly" claim is accurate, not aspirational. D3 (req 2/req 5 contradiction) and D5 (Hood-sheet surface) are correctly flagged to `product` rather than decided unilaterally — no objection to either from this seat. §9's per-P0 verification table is otherwise falsifiable, not "looks right," and the A1/T-032-seed cross-dependency (row 5) is named rather than hidden.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** Three blocking findings, all cheap, spec-level fixes for `architect` (a new decision + two table/text corrections), none a redesign — same class of fix T-032's `trd-review` round needed. Findings 1 and 3 are both instances of the same shape: a claim made confidently in one place (§10's risk table, `EVENTS.md`'s identity constraint) that a careful second read shows isn't backed by what's actually specified or built elsewhere. Finding 2 is the one this dispatch specifically asked to verify rather than accept, and it doesn't hold as literally stated once checked against the shipped `MapScreen.swift`.
- **Left behind:** the three findings above, for `architect` to fix and resubmit; `ios-code-reviewer` re-reviews the fix, not the whole TRD again. Finding 1 also needs a decision from whoever owns `analytics/EVENTS.md` (`analytics-engineer`) once `architect` states which way T-035 is resolving it. Not touching `BOARD.md`'s state — that's `chief-of-staff`'s per this task's own routing. `ios-developer`'s own independent `trd-review` signoff is still needed; if it lands at APPROVE, the two verdicts disagree and this task should route back to `architect` regardless, per the same precedent T-032 set.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit path. **This file's diff unavoidably also carries a concurrent architect session's T-032/PAS-15 v3 worklog entry ("all four `trd-review` findings resolved"), already sitting uncommitted immediately above the insertion point when this pass opened the file** — not this pass's writing, adjacent lines, same hunk-merging limitation this exact file's own T-035 architect entry documents above (`git diff` cannot hunk-filter adjacent additions). Re-read the full diff before committing per rule 2 and confirmed that entry's content is untouched, not authored, edited, or removed by this pass — called out here rather than left to read as mine, same disclosure precedent that other session's own commit used minutes earlier in this file for an analogous adjacent-hunk conflict. No `passenger-code` changes (review-only pass). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-02 — code-reviewer — T-035/PAS-26 `trd-review` (A1 only): **APPROVE WITH MINORS** — round-trip mechanism verified sound, but the TRD's "byte-identical" claim is false for the JSON bundle

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on T-035's step A1 (fake Hood tourist-trap flag values, `[Algo/Data]`), alongside `data-engineer`'s own pass — both signoffs required. Read `PROGRESS.md`'s `a6c1c73` architect entry in full and `BOARD.md`'s T-035 rows, then verified the A1 plan against the real generator rather than trusting the TRD's or the architect's description of it, per the task brief (this is exactly the kind of claim L-024 says needs a skeptical second read).
- **Evidence — round-trip mechanism is real, not just claimed:** ran `database/scripts/build_hoods.py --migration-number 006` against the **unmodified** committed source to a scratch path. The regenerated `migrations/006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` diffed **byte-identical** to the committed file (confirmed: TRD's claim holds for the SQL artifact). Then edited a scratch copy of the source to flip two Hoods' `isTouristTrap` (`null→true`, `null→false`) and regenerated again: both the JSON bundle and the SQL migration changed **only** the two touched hoods' flag value/column — every polygon, blurb, and unrelated row was byte-identical in both artifacts. This is the actual anti-drift property A1 depends on, and it holds.
- **Finding 1 (blocking-for-precision, not for A1 itself) — the TRD's "byte-identical" round-trip claim is false for the JSON bundle, specifically.** `build_bundle()` in `build_hoods.py` stamps `"generatedAt": datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)...` — wall-clock time, not derived from source content. I regenerated from the **unmodified** committed source and diffed against the shipped `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json`: the only difference was `generatedAt` (`2026-08-02T17:31:42Z` vs the shipped `2026-07-31T22:44:41Z`) — i.e. the bundle is **never** byte-identical across two runs, even with zero content change. The SQL migration is unaffected (no timestamp field, only a stable source-digest comment) — that half of the claim is genuinely true. **Failure scenario:** the TRD (line ~389) and its own docstring describe the round-trip check as "byte-identical... the exact check that caught L-024." If `qa` builds `TEST-PLAN.md` off that literal description and diffs the full JSON file, the check fails on every run regardless of correctness — which either produces false alarms or, more dangerously, trains whoever runs it to expect "some diff" and eyeball past it, which is precisely the failure mode L-024 already burned this project on (a real polygon/geometry drift could hide in that same "expected" noise). **Fix:** the TRD's round-trip description and `qa`'s derived check need to exclude/normalize `generatedAt` explicitly, or `build_hoods.py` should derive it from the source digest instead of wall-clock time.
- **Finding 2 (minor) — the authoring rule's 5 conditions are not tool-enforced.** `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json`'s authoring rule (≥1 busy+flagged-in-T-032's-seed, ≥1 flagged-never-busy, ≥1 explicit `false`, ≥1 `null`, ≤4 flagged of 24) has no corresponding check in `validate_dataset.py` — `V9` only validates that `isTouristTrap` is `bool`/`null`-typed, nothing checks the distribution or count. A source edit violating the rule (e.g. 10 hoods flagged, or missing the explicit-`false` case) passes `--check` and the generator writes both artifacts without complaint. Not a defect in A1's design — it's inherent to a prose authoring rule — but flagged so the **actual** A1 review (once `data-engineer` submits the real edited source) explicitly counts against the five conditions rather than trusting a clean `build_hoods.py` run, which proves nothing about the distribution.
- **Confirmed, not a new finding — migration `006` is genuinely still unapplied.** `database/README.md`'s status table: `006` is "Not applied — blocked-on-aviran" and depends on `003` (adds the `is_tourist_trap` column), which is also unapplied. Regenerating `006` now is safe — nothing in a live DB depends on its current state, because there is no live DB state yet.
- **Confirmed, not a new finding — criterion 1 of the authoring rule is currently unverifiable.** Searched the repo for any T-032 density-seed artifact (`density-seed-tel-aviv.json` or similar): none exists. T-032's C10 has not landed, matching what the TRD and board row already state plainly. Not hidden, so not blocking, but restating for the record: if `data-engineer` executes A1 before T-032's seed lands, their pick for "the busy+flagged Hood" is provisional and needs a real second look once the seed exists, not a rubber-stamp reconciliation.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS, for A1.** The core design is correct and matches L-024's lesson exactly — edits go through the source, never the bundle, and the generator's surgical-change property is real (verified directly, not assumed). Findings 1 and 2 are both precision/tooling gaps in the verification story around A1, not defects in A1's plan itself, and neither blocks `data-engineer` from proceeding. Finding 1 should be fixed before `qa` writes `TEST-PLAN.md`'s round-trip check off the TRD's current wording.
- **Left behind:** Finding 1 (generatedAt breaks the literal byte-identical claim — needs a TRD wording fix or a generator fix, whichever `data-engineer`/`architect` prefer) and Finding 2 (authoring rule uncounted by tooling — reviewer must hand-count on the real A1 diff) for whoever does the follow-up pass once `data-engineer` submits actual seeded values. No file changes made this session — verification only, via scratch copies under `/tmp`, nothing touched in the repo.
- **Git:** no changes to commit — this was a verification-only pass against already-committed files.

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass over `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` v1 against `tourist-trap-flag.md` (Draft v1) for the iOS build steps (C1–C14) only — this task needs three review pairs total (data-engineer+code-reviewer on A1, developer+code-reviewer on B1, dispatched separately). Grepped the full `passenger-code` tree for `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`, `UNUserNotification`, and `UIBackgroundModes`/`BGTaskScheduler` — **zero hits on all three**, reproducing the TRD's own claim independently rather than repeating it.

- **D3's reconciled reading is structurally sound, not just asserted.** `FlagStroke.treatment(for:band:)`'s §4.1 table has `.flagged` mapping to `.plain`/`.busyWarning` (never `.none`) across all four band columns, and `.notFlagged`/`.unrated` mapping to `.none` in all four — so "the set of Hoods with any stroke is hour-invariant" falls out of the total function's own shape, not from care at a call site. Confirms D2's "replaces, never stacks" claim is a real type guarantee, and D3's reconciliation is sound engineering. Correctly left for `product` to confirm, not decided here.
- **D4's UIWindow reasoning checked against the actual view tree.** `Map/MapScreen.swift` attaches `.overlay(...)` (×3) and `.sheet(...)` as sibling modifiers on the same `MapReader`/`Map` chain (lines ~124–187) — confirms a root-level overlay genuinely would sit behind an open sheet, exactly the failure D4 describes. Grepped for `UIWindow`/`UIWindowScene` across the app: only two files import `UIKit` (`Support/ContrastRatio.swift`, `Map/SettingsHint.swift`) and neither uses `UIWindow` — D4's "one UIKit bridge in an otherwise pure-SwiftUI app" is accurate, this is genuinely new. `qa` is correctly told (§9 row 8, D4's own text) to check `.announcement` vs `.screenChanged` VoiceOver focus behavior.
- **Req 9's structural claim holds for every type the module layout names.** `LocalQA/`'s contracts (§4.3) touch only `Place.ID`, never `Place`/`Hood`/either catalog; the toast copy (§4.2) is generic and needs no place-name lookup. No type in §2.1's layout acquires both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and a catalog reference.

Confirms the TRD's §3.3 claim that `local-qa.json`'s handling (backup-excluded, `.completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`, hour-truncated, slug-only, non-Keychain UUID) really is more conservative — correctly flagged as a possible future sibling finding and correctly *not* fixed here (surgical-changes rule).

- **D1's premise confirmed:** `MapScreen.swift:18/110` — one `nameLabelSpanThreshold = 0.06` gates `showsNames`, which in turn gates the name label, the pin `ForEach`, and tap resolution together (lines 80, 95, 110, 148, 273). A second, coarser threshold is genuinely required for the Neighborhood tier; renaming the existing constant in place changes no numeric value, so T-031/T-033 behavior is preserved by construction.
- **Data claim reproduced independently:** grepped `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` and `Resources/places-tel-aviv.json` directly — all 24 Hoods and all 9 places ship `null` for their tourist-trap field today, exactly as §3.2 claims. `HeatBand` has exactly 3 cases (quiet/moderate/busy), matching `FlagStroke`'s 4-column (nil/quiet/moderate/busy) band matrix with no gap or extra column.
- **One minor, non-blocking:** §9 row 5's Step column lists only `C2, C5, needs T-032 C10` — it doesn't also name **A1** (the Hood flag seed) as a precondition, even though A1's authoring rule 1 (§3.2) is the only source of a flagged-and-busy Hood id. The dependency is fully stated elsewhere (§1, §3.2, §11's build-order note), so this costs nothing to fix and isn't a buildability gap — just a trace nit for whoever builds `qa`'s `TEST-PLAN.md` off this table, same shape as `ios-developer`'s T-032 minor #2 above.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS**, for the iOS build steps (C1–C14) only. Every premise I could check against shipped code checked out true; D3/D4/D5/req-9's engineering is sound; the one finding is a documentation trace nit, not a send-back. This is one of the two required `ios-developer`/`ios-code-reviewer` sign-offs for this TRD — `ios-code-reviewer`'s independent pass, plus the A1 and B1 pairs, still need to land before this moves to `build`.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-034/PAS-25 `trd-review`: **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (v1, `7c4490f`) against the PRD (`live-events-overlay.md`, Draft v2), `map-rendering-spec.md`, `design-principles.md` §2/§3/§5, and shipped source — `MapScreen.swift`, `DetailRouter.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `PlaceLayer.swift` — rather than trusting the TRD's own citations of that code.
- **D1 and D4 correctly scoped, confirmed by re-deriving rather than trusting the TRD's framing.** D1 (hour binding widened from start-hour to overlap) is a genuine reading-of-the-PRD question and correctly routed to `product`, plus a separate, narrower confirmation to `data-engineer` on the query-string amendment only — the split is right: `product` owns whether the requirement means what the TRD reads it to mean, `data-engineer` owns whether dropping T-043 §4.2's `start_at=gte` filter is safe against the approved view (it is — `events_public` already enforces `end_at > now()`, verified by reading `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2's cited filter list, not assumed). D4 (clustering deferred to unowned T-041) follows L-018 correctly: no invented pass condition, §9 states plainly it isn't verifiable, and the recommendation is stated rather than silently acted on.
- **Zero-new-backend-calls-in-Phase-1 claim verified, not assumed.** `Support/BuildPhase.swift` today holds exactly one runtime constant (`seedIsAuthoritative = true`) driving `PlaceCatalog`'s seed-only path with no fetch attempted; the TRD's `eventSeedIsAuthoritative` is the identical mechanism applied to a second store. `MapScreen.swift`'s four existing `.task` blocks confirm the seed-vs-fetch branch pattern is real, shipped, and already reviewed (T-032's `trd-review`) — the TRD is reusing a proven seam, not proposing an unverified one.
- **D2's "made safe by the cap" claim is only partly supported — one real gap, not blocking.** The TRD rebuts the pin-density reasoning (`map-rendering-spec.md` §2's pin row is about "dozens of place pins," not a 12-marker cap) but never engages the *other*, more directly applicable principle in the same doc: §3's explicit commitment that city-wide zoom stays "a pure density read... not a liability map at every scale," argued on **legibility grounds independent of count** ("a map you have to parse for what's the exception, not a glance"). Twelve markers layered over heat blobs at the exact zoom a cold open shows is arguably the same "map you have to parse" problem Aviran's original tag pushback described, regardless of the count being bounded. The TRD already flags D2 for `designer`/`product` — correct process — but whoever adjudicates it should know the written justification answers the wrong precedent, not that the divergence is unreviewed.
- **New finding, not raised by the TRD: `DetailRouter.openEvent` is asymmetric with `openPlace`'s actual shipped behavior, and the stated reason doesn't hold.** §4.7 justifies always-depth-1/always-exclusive event routing by comparing it to `openHood`'s swap-in-place behavior and rejecting a depth-2 alternative as one that "would add a third level" the router "deliberately cannot express." That's not accurate: depth 2 already exists (`hood != nil && place != nil`), and an event could occupy that same slot the way `place` does when a Hood sheet is open — `openPlace`'s own shipped comment (`DetailRouter.swift:20-22`) states this exact case is intentional ("A pin tap on the exposed map while the Hood sheet is open lands at depth 2 — correct, the user is still inside that Hood's context"). As specified, tapping a visible event marker while a Hood sheet is open (reachable today — `MapScreen.swift:186`'s `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` keeps the map tappable up through medium detent) would force-close the Hood sheet the user is browsing, where an equivalent tap on a place pin in the same situation nests instead and preserves it. No PRD requirement addresses this case either way, and the TRD presents it as settled rather than flagging it the way D1/D2/D4/D6 are flagged. Recommend `product`/`designer` get an explicit call here too, or `ios-developer` builds event as a `DetailRouter` case parallel to `place`'s depth-2 behavior instead.
- **Accessibility gap, minor.** §4.5's VoiceOver label (`"\(event.name), event, \(timeLabel)"`) leaves `timeLabel`'s format undefined, and §9 has no row asserting the label's actual content — only the tap-target identifier (`eventMarker-<id>`) is tested. `map-rendering-spec.md` §7 holds every other map annotation (zones, pins, clusters, personal-place pins) to an exact quoted string; this is the one new annotation type in the app without one. Not a missing label (design-principles.md §5's bar), so not a blocking accessibility failure — but worth a one-line format spec and a unit test before C5/C8, the same rigor `EventDetailRows` gets for the sheet body.
- **D7 (tap-race fix) confirmed real, not asserted.** Read `MapScreen.swift:113-122` directly: the greedy `SpatialTapGesture` via `.simultaneousGesture` is exactly as described, and `handleTap`'s current place-then-hood precedence would resolve an event-marker tap to the wrong sheet without the TRD's fix. The mirrored `PlaceHitTester`/idempotent-`openPlace` pattern this fix copies is real and already shipping.
- **No accessibility failure and no functional/reliability break found** (per the Maslow precedence this role applies) — everything above is a specification gap or an unflagged-but-reversible design call, not a broken or unsafe build. Verdict: **APPROVE WITH MINORS.**
- **Left behind:** three items for whoever builds/reviews next — (1) the `DetailRouter.openEvent`/depth-2 question above, unflagged by the TRD, needs a `product`/`designer` call or a build-time fix to parallel `openPlace`; (2) `timeLabel`'s exact format plus a §9 test row for the event marker's accessibility label; (3) D2's zoom-divergence adjudication (already correctly routed to `designer`/`product`) should weigh §3's city-wide "pure density read" principle, not only the pin-density reasoning the TRD cites. Did not touch `BOARD.md` — leaving its state to whoever tallies this alongside `ios-developer`'s own `trd-review` pass.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-036/PAS-27 `trd-review`: **REQUEST CHANGES** — concur with `ios-developer`'s `NavSurface` mismatch; one more cross-TRD conflict found on D7

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass over `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (v1) against `places-been-saved.md` (Draft v1), `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row, and the architect's 2026-08-02 T-036 worklog entry (`0051ef0`). Focus per dispatch: check D7 (chrome-bucket placement) concretely against `design/ux-flows.md` §2, check whether it's a real conflict with T-032's TRD needing reconciliation, check the `PlaceProvenance: Comparable` precedence-merge for legitimate ambiguity, and confirm the privacy/no-new-backend-surface claim. Read `ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1 in full, `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` (T-032, current `trd` state, mid-v3) in full, and cross-checked T-036's §4.1–§4.9 contracts directly rather than taking the TRD's prose at face value. **Read `ios-developer`'s own `trd-review` entry (immediately below this one) before forming a verdict**, per the two-signoff routing — genuinely independent means re-deriving the finding from source, not skipping a check because a peer already ran it.
- **D7 checked concretely against `ux-flows.md` §2 — accurate, well-sourced, not overstated.** §2's Primary table (line 46) reads verbatim: *"Saved-places icon (4th icon) — Persistent icon, **separate from the 3 nav buttons**; opens the full Places list."* §2.1's 2026-07-30 stacking addendum (line 85) independently lists *"near-me (L44) and the Places icon (L46)"* together by name as bucket-2 chrome that fades under an open modal — the exact bucket T-036 §2.4/§4.5 places `PlacesButton` in. Both citations check out word-for-word against the live doc, not paraphrased.
- **This is a real conflict with T-032's TRD, and it's in the prose, not the type contract.** T-032 §8 D1 (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md:499-500`) reads: *"`ux-flows.md` §2 and the mockup show three or four nav buttons... This task builds `MapNavRow` as the container plus the heat button only. **The row is laid out so the rest slot in without re-layout.**"* — naming Search/Profile/Places in the line just above as "the rest." That's a textual claim that Places will physically join `MapNavRow`, which is what T-036's D7 corrects (Places is bucket-2 chrome, never the nav row). T-036 §2.4/§8 D7 says as much ("This corrects T-032's D1's expectation... for T-036 it is not") but the correction lives only in T-036's document — T-032's own D1 text still asserts the stale expectation as written today. Distinct from `ios-developer`'s finding below: that one is a type-shape bug in a Swift contract; this one is a stale claim in prose that a future reader (an architect scoping T-038, a designer reading T-032 cold) would reasonably take at face value. **Recommend folding a one-line correction into T-032's D1** ("the rest" → "Search and Profile; Places is bucket-2 chrome, corrected by T-036's D7") **into the v3 pass already in flight** for T-032 (`BOARD.md` line 54) rather than opening a fourth pass later — same document, same trip. Non-blocking for T-036 itself, whose own text is internally consistent and correctly sourced.
- **Blocking — independently re-verified `ios-developer`'s `NavSurface` type-mismatch finding, concur.** T-036 §4.5 (`TRD.md:304`) specs `enum NavSurface: Equatable { case heat, places }` with the comment "whichever task lands first declares its own case." Read T-032's real, current §4.1 (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md:188-191`) directly: `enum NavSurface: String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable { case search, heat, places, profile }`, built **unconditionally** at T-032's C1, with its own commentary (`:205`) stating the four-member set exists specifically "to stop T-036/T-037/T-038 each inventing a private boolean" — i.e., T-032 already considers this file settled and non-negotiable, the opposite of "whoever lands first declares their own case." Different case count (2 vs 4), different conformances (`Equatable` alone vs `String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable`). T-036's own build step C6 (`:487`) — "build only if T-032's C1 has not landed" — would create the narrower 2-case stub in the live ordering risk the dispatch names (T-036 lands before T-032's v3 clears), and T-032's C1 is unconditional with no "if it exists, don't restructure" clause anywhere in its own text. That is exactly the restructuring T-036 §2.2 promises won't happen, promised unilaterally. **Fix, same as `ios-developer` recommended:** T-036 §4.5/§2.2/C6 should declare the actual four-case T-032 contract verbatim and make C6's conditional "if it doesn't exist, create it exactly per T-032 §4.1," not a narrower invented type.
- **Precedence-merge logic (`PlaceProvenance: Comparable`, §4.1/§4.3) — sound. No path to legitimate ambiguity found.** Precedence is `max` over an `Int`-backed `Comparable` enum (`visited=0, been=1, saved=2`); a place's provenance is never stored, only computed at read time from two independent sources (`saved: Set<Place.ID>`, `visits: [Place.ID: VisitKind]`). `VisitKind` is a **separate** type from `PlaceProvenance` with no `.saved` case, so the visit source cannot produce the top value by construction — there is no branch that could resolve it backwards. The one place ambiguity could theoretically enter — two visit records for the same `place_id` — is closed at the boundary: §4.2 requires "keep the higher kind, drop the other" on a duplicate id, enforced by a test (C4), so `[Place.ID: VisitKind]` can hold at most one value per key by the time `PlacesListComposition.entries` ever sees it. No third store, no persisted merged state, nothing to go stale. **One should-fix, non-blocking:** §3.4's "constraint the real detector inherits" list states the record shape (`{place_id, kind, first_observed_at}`) but not the **update** invariant — keep-the-higher-kind-on-revisit — that currently exists only as a Phase-1 fixture-authoring rule (§4.2, tested by C4). PRD req 3 bullet 4 ("revisiting a Been place... changes no label") depends on that same invariant surviving into the real detector's write path in Phase 2, and §3.4 is exactly where the TRD already writes down what the detector must inherit — recommend adding one line there so the invariant isn't re-discovered (or silently violated) when the fixture is swapped out.
- **Privacy / no-new-backend-surface claim — confirmed, checked three ways independently rather than accepted from §1.** (1) `VisitedPlacesStore` (§4.2) has no persistence and no `CoreLocation` import anywhere in its contract — read-only fixture in, in-memory dictionary out, matches §3.4's claim. (2) The one server-side field this feature reads, `places.permanently_closed`, is pre-existing scope already owned and approved by T-042's TRD (§3 schema, migration `004`, held for Build Phase 2) — this task adds no column, no table, no RLS, no endpoint; it only widens an existing `select=` string by one column. (3) `saved-places.json` is untouched (D4's own reasoning depends on this) and the Been/Visited fixture is bundle-read-only, never written. §9 row 5 makes the "no `CoreLocation` import, no authorization read" claim a grep-checkable review item rather than a promise — correct instinct, and it holds on inspection.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES**, net result unchanged by concurring rather than disagreeing — both signoffs required and the `NavSurface` mismatch is a real, independently-confirmed blocking bug regardless of which reviewer names it first. Two items for the fix pass: (1) blocking — T-036 §4.5/§2.2/C6 must adopt T-032's real four-case `NavSurface` contract verbatim; (2) should-fix, non-blocking — fold a one-line D1 correction into T-032's own v3 pass so its prose stops asserting Places will join `MapNavRow`. Everything else — D2, D3, D4–D6, D8, D9, the §9 table, the build breakdown — checked out on independent re-derivation and is not re-litigated here.
- **Evidence:** direct reads of `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (full), `places-been-saved.md` (full), `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1/§8 D1 (full sections), `design/ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1 (full sections, both citations verified verbatim), `agent-os/BOARD.md` T-032/T-036 rows.
- **Left behind:** the two items above, for the architect's next T-036 pass (and, for item 2, folded into T-032's already-scheduled v3 pass rather than a new dispatch). ### 2026-08-02 — ios-developer — T-036/PAS-27 `trd-review`: **REQUEST CHANGES** — the shared `MapChromeState` contract is not actually shared

- **Did:** read `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (v1) in full against the PRD (`places-been-saved.md`, Draft v1), `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row, and the relevant `PROGRESS.md` history (architect's 2026-08-02 T-036 TRD entry, `0051ef0`). - **D1 verified accurate.** `Place` today is exactly the 5-field shape the TRD says (`id, name, category, hoodID, coordinate`), no `permanentlyClosed`. All three of `PlaceCatalog`'s source paths currently and deliberately exclude the field: `SeedFile.Entry`'s own comment says so explicitly ("`permanently_closed`... deliberately undeclared"), `PlacesAPI.PlaceRow` + its `select=` query string carry no such column, and `PlacesCache.CachedPlace` carries no such field either. Threading it through all three, non-optional, is real work and not already partially done — the TRD's claim checks out.
- **D8 verified accurate.** `DetailRouter.swift`'s `closeHood()`/`closePlace()`/`openPlace(_:)`/`placeDepth` are exactly as described, and `MapScreen.swift:186` does carry `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` — the mechanism §4.6 builds its exclusivity rule on is real, not asserted.
- **Blocking finding — §2.2's "identical contract, either build order" claim is false, checked against T-032's actual TRD text, not assumed.** T-036 §4.5 specs:
  ```swift
  enum NavSurface: Equatable { case heat, places }   // §2.2: whichever task lands first declares its own case
  ```
  T-032's real, current §4.1 (v2, the version live right now — its pending v3 fix is scoped to an edge-gesture camera-immobility test, nowhere near this section) specs a **different type**:
  ```swift

      case search, heat, places, profile

  }
  ```
  Different case membership (2 vs. 4), different protocol conformances (`Equatable` only vs. `String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable`), and — the part that actually breaks the "either build order" claim — **no reciprocal coordination logic on T-032's side**. T-032's TRD text (§4.1's own commentary, plus §7's dependency-direction line: *"T-036/T-037/T-038 add their own `NavSurface` views and their own buttons to `MapNavRow`"*) states flatly that it ships the full four-member set up front because `ux-flows.md` §2.1 locks it, specifically to stop T-036/T-037/T-038 each inventing a private type. T-032's own build step (C1, unconditional, no "if it already exists" clause) just builds that file. There is no "whoever builds it second doesn't restructure it" language anywhere in T-032's TRD — that promise exists only in T-036's document, unilaterally, describing an agreement the other party never made.
  - **Concretely, in the order the dispatch flagged as live right now (T-036 lands before T-032's v3 clears review):** T-036's own build step C6 says "build `MapChromeState`+`NavSurface.places` only if T-032's C1 has not landed" and would create the 2-case `Equatable` stub per §4.5. When T-032 later builds its unconditional C1 against a file that already exists with the wrong shape, it has to change the case count (2→4), add three conformances, and rename the raw representation — that is restructuring, not "add one case," directly violating T-036's own §2.2 guarantee to whoever builds second.
  - **In the reverse order (T-032 lands first)** there's no collision, because T-032's real enum is a superset that already contains `.places` — T-036 would just read the shipped file and reuse it. But that's exactly why the fix is cheap: T-036 §4.5 should be corrected to declare the *actual* T-032 §4.1 contract verbatim (all four cases, real conformances) rather than a narrower invented one, and its build-step C6 conditional should read "if it doesn't exist yet, create it exactly per T-032 §4.1" — not a 2-case subset. That makes both build orders safe, including the live one.
- **Everything else checked out on this pass — D2–D7, D9, the §9 verification table, and the build breakdown are sound**, and the two "carried, not claimed" honesty notes (§9 rows 3/5 on the Been/Visited fixture-vs-detector gap, row 4 on T-044's ownerless freshness job) are accurate reads of what's actually built vs. what's asserted.
