### 2026-08-02 — ios-developer — T-034/PAS-25 `trd-review`: APPROVE WITH MINORS — one real mutual-exclusivity gap found in §4.7, everything else checked out against shipped code

- **Did:** read `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` against the PRD (Draft v2) and against the actual shipped Swift it claims to build on — `Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Map/PlaceLayer.swift`, `Places/PlaceHitTester.swift`, `Detail/DetailRouter.swift`, `Support/BuildPhase.swift` — and against `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2 for the backend-surface claim. No code written; this is a pre-build review pass.
- **D7 (tap precedence) verified concretely, not taken on the TRD's word.** `MapScreen.swift:113-122`'s `.simultaneousGesture(SpatialTapGesture()...)` is exactly as cited — greedy, fires on every tap alongside `PlaceLayer`'s own `Button`. Read `handleTap` (267-278): it's an if/else-if chain, place-then-hood, both branches gated identically to what they render (`showsNames`). The TRD's fix — `event (ungated) → place (gated) → hood`, `EventHitTester` reusing the same 22-screen-point `mapPointTolerance` — slots into that exact shape. For the actual race the PRD/TRD name (tap an event marker → `Button` fires `openEvent` directly, `SpatialTapGesture` fires `handleTap`), the event branch matches first and calls `openEvent` again — idempotent, same pattern `PlaceLayer`'s own doc comment states and `DetailRouter.openPlace` already proves out. **This specific race is correctly fixed as specified.**
- **The "idempotent two-path pattern" D7 says it mirrors does exist as described.** `PlaceLayer.swift`'s header comment states it verbatim ("both this button and the map's own `SpatialTapGesture` can fire... both call `router.openPlace`... a safe no-op the second time"), and `DetailRouter.openPlace` is in fact just `self.place = place` — idempotent by construction. Not asserted from the TRD's citation; read directly.
- **Real finding, not raised by the TRD: §4.7's mutual-exclusivity claim doesn't hold in both directions.** The TRD states the event destination is "always depth 1, and mutually exclusive with both other destinations," and its code sample makes `openEvent` clear `hood`/`place` — but the *existing*, unmodified `openHood` (`hood = hood; place = nil`) and `openPlace` (`place = place`) are never told to clear `event`, and the TRD's §4.7 diff doesn't add that either (only `closeHood` is named as needing the update, for the swipe-to-dismiss path). This is reachable, not theoretical: the event sheet ships with `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` — the same mechanism T-033's shipped sheets already rely on to let taps reach the map underneath a `.medium`-detent sheet. So: open an event sheet, then tap the exposed map (a Hood, or a place pin, still visible above the sheet) → `openHood`/`openPlace` fires and sets `hood`/`place`, but `event` stays non-nil. If the sheet's `Group` checks `event` first (the natural order, matching D7's event-first precedence), the sheet silently keeps showing the just-tapped-away-from event instead of switching — a stale sheet bug, not a crash, but a real one, and exactly the class of gate-mismatch this codebase has already shipped a REJECT for once (T-033's zoom/hit-test gate mismatch, cited in the TRD's own §4.5). Mechanical fix: `openHood` and `openPlace` each need `event = nil` alongside their existing clears, same as `openEvent` already clears both. §9 row 4 should gain a case (open an event, tap a Hood/place, confirm the sheet switches) alongside its existing D7 check.
- **§9 verification table:** every P0 row has a falsifiable observable and pass condition with an independent computation (not read back from the function under test) — confirmed by reading all six rows, not sampling. Row 2c (clustering) is the one deliberate exception, explicitly marked "no pass condition — not verifiable in this task" with the reasoning stated (L-018 discipline) rather than a "looks right" row faking coverage. Correct, not a gap.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-035/PAS-26 TRD delivered: Tourist-trap flag & Local QA — not iOS-only, two PRD requirements found unsatisfiable as written

- **Did:** wrote `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` (v1, ready for `trd-review`) from the approved PRD (Draft v1) plus the retired-gate design spec as reference. Eight decisions D1–D8, a §9 verification table with one row per P0 (plus a separate row for the leg Phase 1 cannot verify), and a 17-step build breakdown: **A1 `[Algo/Data]`, B1 `[Backend]` (HELD, Build Phase 2), C1–C14 `[iOS]`**. `trd-review` needs **three pairs** — `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer`, `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer` (A1), `developer` + `code-reviewer` (B1) — plus `product` on three flagged calls.
- **Surface determined, not assumed — and it is not iOS-only.** The design spec's own §8 item 3 says "the fixture needs 2–3 Hoods seeded with real values," which reads as an iOS edit and would have walked `ios-developer` straight into **L-024**: `Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` is a *generated* artifact — `database/scripts/build_hoods.py` emits it **and** `006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` from `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` in one run (read the generator, not the docs describing it). So Hood flag seeding is **A1, `[Algo/Data]`, in `passenger-brain`**: edit the source, regenerate, migration stays unapplied. `places-tel-aviv.json` is genuinely different and *is* hand-editable in Phase 1 — `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` C3a hand-authored it and T-042's `export_places.py` only becomes sole emitter at Phase 2 — so place flags are `[iOS]` C6. Two files that look identical, opposite rules; both checked against their own TRDs.
- **Finding 1 (D3): PRD reqs 2 and 5 contradict each other, and neither is checkable until it's resolved.** Req 2 bullet 3: *"Moving the time slider repaints heat and changes no stroke: the flag is not time-variant."* Req 5: a flagged Hood **above the busy threshold for the selected hour** gets a distinct stroke. Busy is per-hour, so a flagged Hood's stroke does change with the slider. Built the reconciled reading — for every hour 0…12 the **set** of Hoods rendering any flag stroke is identical; only an already-flagged Hood's stroke *style* varies — and wrote that as §9 row 2's falsifiable pass condition. **Flagged for `product`, not decided unilaterally:** if the intent was a fully hour-invariant stroke, req 5 is the requirement that changes.
- **Finding 2 (D5): req 4 bullet 2 has no surface anywhere in the app.** It says a not-yet-rated Hood "renders identically to not-flagged, **resolved on tap by the Hood sheet**." Read `HoodSheet.swift` directly: it has no flag surface of any kind; the PRD's req 6 covers the *place* modal only; the design spec's §0 explicitly scopes the Hood sheet out. As it stood, the tap resolves nothing and the bullet is prose no gate could fail — L-018's exact shape. Added a three-state line (flagged / not flagged / no local rating yet), flagged to `product`/`designer` as new UI no doc covers, with the "Not a tourist-heavy spot" phrasing named as an unapproved copy decision. One `@ViewBuilder` property; trivially reversible.
- **Finding 3: the local-QA loop's trigger does not exist and is not this task's to build.** No notification code, no region monitoring, no `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`, no background mode anywhere in the project; `LocationStore` deliberately holds authorization status and never a `CLLocation`. The PRD assigns that detector to `data-engineer` as *"one detector, three consumers"* (T-035/T-036/T-037) and T-036's design flagged the same absence independently — **it is named in three PRDs and owned in none.** Phase 1 therefore builds the consumer side for real (toast, gate, ask-once ledger, device-local queue, install identity) against a `VisitSource` seam driven by a launch argument, the same construction the shipped `-uiTestZoomedIn` uses. §1 and §9 state plainly that Phase-1 acceptance does **not** cover req 8's first bullet. Recommended as its own board row, with its unowned Always-authorization / background-mode / notification-permission surface (and no PRD-specified moment to ask, since the strategy forbids onboarding).
- **Finding 4 (D4): the design's toast construction doesn't work.** It specifies a root `ZStack` overlay rendering over an open sheet; SwiftUI presents `.sheet` in its own host above the presenting view's overlays, and the notification-tap path foregrounds the app to whatever it was last showing — which can be a sheet. Call: a passthrough `UIWindow` whose `hitTest` returns `nil` outside the toast, so req 8's non-blocking bullet is structural rather than a styling choice. Cost stated: one UIKit bridge in an otherwise pure-SwiftUI app, and `qa` must confirm the second window doesn't steal VoiceOver focus (`.announcement`, never `.screenChanged`).
- **Also corrected against source:** the design spec says the flag *stroke* can reuse the existing `showsNames` threshold. It can't — `nameLabelSpanThreshold` (0.06) is the **Close** boundary (it gates pins and tap resolution too), so a stroke gated on it would appear exactly when pins do and the Neighborhood tier of `map-rendering-spec.md` §2's table would not exist. Both stroke and label need the new coarser threshold; they differ in which side of the Close boundary they survive. `MapZoomTier` is one pure derivation and `showsNames` becomes `tier == .close`, so four consumers can't disagree.
- **Cross-task dependency, named not worked around:** req 5's busy+flagged state is **unobservable until T-032's density seed (its C10) lands** — `DensityStore` has no seed path, so `band` is `nil` for every Hood at every hour in Phase 1. A second density seed in this task was rejected (two seeds is two chances to disagree); A1's authoring rule instead requires its flagged Hood ids to be reconciled with T-032's seed by whichever lands second.
- **Privacy treated as a data-model constraint, per the role's own rule:** `local-qa.json` records that a person was *physically present* at a named place, which is a stronger artifact than `saved-places.json`'s record of intent. Slug only (never a coordinate), timestamp truncated to the UTC hour, excluded from device backup, `.completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication` file protection, and a per-install `UUID` that is **not** the IDFV, not the IDFA and deliberately **not** Keychain-backed (the Keychain survives reinstall, which would make "install id" a lie and create a more durable identifier than the feature needs). Flagged as a possible sibling finding that `saved-places.json` gets none of this — deliberately not changed here (surgical-changes rule).
- **Req 9 made structural rather than conventional:** there is exactly one direction of data flow (catalogs → render, toast → answer store) and **no type holds both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog**, so "one answer never changes what any viewer sees" is a fact about the reference graph, not a behaviour to test. `ios-code-reviewer` gets a blocking finding for any type that acquires both. Same posture for req 5: `FlagStroke` is a single-value enum, so "replaces, never stacks" is unrepresentable rather than merely tested.
- **Evidence:** every claim checked against source — `HoodLayer.swift`, `HoodCatalog.swift`, `Hood.swift`, `Place.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, `PlacesAPI.swift`, `PlacesCache.swift`, `PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `HoodSheet.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `SavedPlacesStore.swift`, `LocationStore.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `HeatBand.swift`, `project.pbxproj`'s Info.plist keys, the Assets catalog, `build_hoods.py`, `validate_dataset.py`, and both bundled fixtures (confirmed directly: **all 24 Hoods and all 9 places ship `null` today**). Migration number `007` assigned per the house rule that the number is passed in, never scanned (003/004/005/006 all claimed).
- **Left behind:** three `product` calls (D3's requirement contradiction, D5's new Hood-sheet surface, §1's Phase-1 boundary); the unowned dwell-detector row; `map-rendering-spec.md` §7's decision-#42-violating example ("not a tourist trap") still uncorrected — now flagged twice, by the design spec and by this TRD, and still owned by nobody. B1 must not be dispatched in Phase 1. `qa` builds `TEST-PLAN.md` from §9 and must treat the "8-trigger" row as explicitly out of Phase-1 scope rather than passing it by construction.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` **`a6c1c73`** — `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` (new), `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-035 rows ×2), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9. **[Hash corrected 2026-08-02 by the T-035 v2 `architect` pass.** This line originally read `a671373`, an object left orphaned by this entry's own self-reported `read-tree` mishap and `reset --soft` recovery — unreachable from `HEAD`, confirmed with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`. `a6c1c73` is the commit actually on the mainline carrying this content. Found by `ios-developer`'s `trd-review` pass, which asked for the correction whenever `architect` next touched the entry. Text otherwise untouched.**
- **Self-report — I clobbered another session's commit and then undid it; saying so rather than leaving the reflog to say it (rule 2 / L-023, a new variant worth recording).** Three sessions wrote `BOARD.md` and `PROGRESS.md` in this working tree during this run. Rule 2's `git diff --staged` check *worked* — it caught a T-034 row that wasn't mine — but the fix I reached for was wrong: `git diff` merged T-034's row and my T-035 row into **one hunk** (adjacent lines), so hunk-level filtering could not separate them, and I moved to a private `GIT_INDEX_FILE` seeded with `git read-tree HEAD`. Between that `read-tree` and the commit, the concurrent session committed T-034's TRD — so my tree, built from the older HEAD, **reverted it**: commit `edb5c81` deleted `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (503 lines) and reverted both shared files' T-034 content. Caught immediately in my own `git show --stat`, undone with `git reset --soft 7c4490f` (never pushed, nothing lost — `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md`, `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md`, T-034's PROGRESS entry and both its BOARD rows all verified present afterwards), and recommitted correctly on top of `7c4490f`. **The transferable lesson, for `retrospective`:** rule 2 protects against staging someone else's *content*; it says nothing about a stale *base*, and a private index seeded from a HEAD that then moves is a worse footgun than the shared index it was meant to avoid. When two changed rows are adjacent, reconstruct your own file content from the **current** HEAD immediately before committing and re-check the parent, or commit with a pathspec (`git commit -- <paths>`), which is what finally worked.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-034/PAS-25 TRD delivered: Live events overlay (client UI)

- **Did:** wrote `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (v1, ready for `trd-review`) from the approved PRD (Draft v2). No design spec exists and none was needed (pre-code design gate retired 2026-08-02; `BOARD.md`'s T-034 row confirms none had been drafted). Twelve decisions D1–D12, a §9 verification table with one row per P0, and a 12-step build breakdown C1–C12. **Surface: iOS-only — verified three ways, not assumed.** All 12 steps are `[iOS]`; `trd-review` routes to **`ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer`**.
- **How the iOS-only claim was checked** (not inferred from the PRD's "pure renderer" line): (1) every column the client reads is already pinned in `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2's `events_public` view (T-043, `build`-ready, 4/4 `trd-review`) — no new table, column, view, RPC, index or RLS policy falls out of anything in this TRD; (2) Build Phase 1 makes no request at all, the bundled fixture drives reqs 1–4 and 6; (3) the single contract edit (§4.2) is a query-string change with no server-side artifact.
- **Four findings from reading shipped code, none raised by any upstream doc:**
  - **D7, the one that would have shipped as a bug.** `MapScreen.swift:113-122`'s greedy `SpatialTapGesture` (the FB19394663 workaround) fires for *every* map tap, so a tap on an event marker would fire the marker's own `Button` **and** `handleTap`, and `handleTap` would resolve to a Hood and call `openHood` — clearing the event that was just set and opening the wrong sheet. `PlaceLayer` only survives this because both paths land on the same idempotent call. Fix: `EventHitTester` + event-first precedence (event → place → hood), mirroring the shipped pattern rather than inventing a third mechanism. C11 verifies it rather than assuming it.
  - **D1, hour binding.** PRD req 1 bullet 1 reads literally as start-hour-only, which hides a four-hour rooftop party from "now" for three of the four hours it is happening. Called as **overlap** (`start < bucketEnd && end > bucketStart && end > now`), which satisfies req 1 bullets 1 and 3 and req 5 bullet 2 with one predicate. **Carries the only contract edit in the document:** T-043 §4.2's client request filters `start_at=gte.{anchorHour}`, which deletes every already-in-progress event from the payload at every hour — dropped. Safe and free: `events_public` already enforces `end_at > now()`, so no SQL, view, RLS or build step changes. `data-engineer` to confirm at review.
  - **D8/D9, rollout.** Events go live in Build Phase **3**, places and density in Phase **2** — one shared `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` would fire the events fetch a phase early against a view that does not exist. Second constant, `eventSeedIsAuthoritative`. And once it is off the seed is **never** a fallback (`live → empty`, not `live → seed → empty`): a stale place is still a real place, a fabricated event is a lie about tonight, and req 5 already blesses an empty layer as shippable.
  - **D2, zoom.** Event markers render at **every** zoom, unlike place pins — a stated divergence from `map-rendering-spec.md` §2's pin row, whose density reasoning is about dozens of place pins per Hood, not a capped city-wide set. Inseparable from the cap (D3).
- **The PRD's named fixture gap, closed (D10, §3.4, step C2).** Folded into this task's own build scope, same pattern T-033 shipped and T-032's TRD adopted. Relative offsets, not absolute timestamps — an events file of absolute timestamps is not merely stale after a day, it is *empty*, since every row fails `end_at > now`. **Eight-clause authoring rule asserted by a test**, not left to the author's care (≥6 populated buckets, ≥1 empty bucket, one bucket with `cap+2` distinct-rank events, ≥1 event spanning ≥3 buckets, null `venue_name` and null `category` cases, ≥1 already-ended event, coordinates inside real Hood polygons, and plainly fictional names — a demo fixture must not assert that a real Tel Aviv business is hosting a real event tonight).
- **Evidence:** decisions traced to source, not to summaries — `DensityStore.swift:23` (`selectedHour` is the one hour, D12), `MapScreen.swift:79/95/113-122/157-187` (composition seam, pin zoom gate, the tap race, the single `.sheet`), `PlaceLayer.swift` (the marker idiom C5 copies), `PlaceCatalog.swift:90-107` (the seed-branch shape C1/C2 mirror, and the precedence D8 deliberately diverges from), `DetailRouter.swift` (the destination set C9 extends), `DirectionsService.swift` (Apple-Maps-only, inherited from T-033's D3 — PRD req 4 says "Maps/Waze" and this task neither widens nor re-litigates that), `BuildPhase.swift`, and `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2/§4.3 for the served-set and `rank` contract.
- **Left behind:**
  - **Req 2 bullet 2 (clustering) has no pass condition in §9, deliberately** — flagged, not waved through (L-018). Event markers are required to cluster by the same screen-distance rule as place pins; **that rule is unimplemented anywhere in the app** (`T-041`/`PAS-30`, unowned, Build Phase 2). Writing a check would mean either testing a private duplicate this TRD refuses to build, or a "looks right" row no gate can fail. `product` decides at review: accept the marker cap as Phase 1's bound and move the bullet to T-041 (**architect recommends this** — at ≤12 markers there is nothing to cluster), or give T-041 an owner ahead of T-034's build.
  - **Four calls flagged for `product`:** D1's widened hour binding, D4's clustering decision above, D10's fixture claim (the PRD names it unowned; this TRD claims it), D2's every-zoom rendering.
  - **One for `data-engineer`:** §4.2's query amendment. No build step, no SQL — but it edits a request shape an already-approved TRD documented, and that belongs acknowledged now rather than discovered in Phase 3.
  - **Two [ASSUMPTION]s with no design spec upstream:** the marker cap `= 12` (derivation stated — ≈1 marker per 2 Hoods at city-wide zoom; the PRD's own open question defers the real number to real event volume, which does not exist yet) and the marker glyph (`sparkles` in a rounded rect; the *shape* is what carries req 1 bullet 4 and survives greyscale, the glyph is one line for the post-ship `designer` redesign pass to overturn — same posture T-032's D6 held before `designer` pinned `flame.fill`).
  - **C12 (the heat-modal toggle) is blocked on T-032's C5** — `HeatModalCard` does not exist yet. It is the only step with an unshipped dependency; C1–C11 build against shipped code regardless of T-032's state. Also worth stating so nobody reads it as a missing build: the PRD says the toggle sits "with the other layer toggles" and **there are none** — T-032's D4 ships that modal with the slider only, so this row will be alone.
  - **`sourceName` is decoded and not rendered**, pending T-043 §8 F4's open per-source ToS question. Displaying it later is a view change, not a model change.
  - Salvage (`Models/LiveEvent.swift`, `EventsService.swift` et al., marked REUSE) is **unreachable** — `~/APE Studio/locali` is absent, the same access gap T-031 and T-032 hit — and largely moot: `prds/INDEX.md` already warns the Locali overlay shipped as an unflagged raw feed, so it has no notion of a ranked subset, a cap, or a served-set view. `ios-developer` should not block on it.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-036/PAS-27 TRD delivered: Places — Been & Saved

- **Did:** wrote `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (v1, ready for `trd-review`) from the approved PRD (Draft v1), with `design/phase-1/places-been-saved-design.md` as informational input, not a gate (pre-code design gate retired 2026-08-02). Resolved the three open TRD items `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row named, plus eight more found by reading the shipped code. **Surface: iOS-only — verified, not assumed.** All 14 build steps are `[iOS]`; `trd-review` routes to `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer` only.
- **How the iOS-only claim was checked** (three ways, not inferred from the PRD's "device-local" line): the PRD writes nothing server-side in V1; the one server field this feature reads, `places.permanently_closed`, **already has an owner and an approved TRD** — T-042's §3 schema (`not null default false` + `closed_checked_at` + the `places_closed_has_source` constraint) and migration `004_places.sql`, held for Build Phase 2; and Build-Phase-1 data for both halves is client-bundle content (`places-tel-aviv.json` already authors `permanently_closed` on all 9 rows).
- **The three board-named items, settled:**
  - **`Place.permanentlyClosed`** — added non-optional and threaded through **all three** of `PlaceCatalog`'s source paths (seed, `PlacesAPI` incl. its `select=` string, `PlacesCache`), not just the seed. Seed-only would compile, pass every Phase-1 test, and ship a silent `false` for every place the moment Phase 2 flips the constant (**D1**). No cache migration needed — Phase 1 never writes `places-cache.json`, so an older file failing to decode and falling through to the seed is the already-designed path; C1 proves it with a test rather than assuming it.
  - **Been/Visited provenance store** — bundled read-only fixture `place-visits-tel-aviv.json` behind a **new** `BuildPhase.visitsAreSeeded`, with **no persistence and no `CoreLocation` import anywhere in the feature** (**D2**). Second constant, not a reuse of `seedIsAuthoritative`: different axis (fixture-vs-sensor, not bundle-vs-server). The four fixture ids were verified present in the shipped seed, not copied from the design spec's suggestion.
  - **Read-time precedence merge** — **not a class or actor: a pure `enum PlacesListComposition`** over two independent stores (**D3**). A third `@Observable` store would hold state derived from two others and would need invalidation; a function cannot go stale. `PlaceProvenance: Int, Comparable` makes "Saved beats Been beats Visited" a `max`, and a separate `VisitKind { been, visited }` makes it structurally impossible for the visit source to claim `.saved`.
- **Five more found by reading the shipped code, not raised by any upstream doc:**
  - **D7 — the Places button is bucket-2 chrome, not a nav-row button.** `ux-flows.md` §2 says "4th icon, **separate from the 3 nav buttons**" and its stacking addendum lists the Places icon in bucket 2 by name. **This corrects T-032's D1**, which expected T-036/T-037/T-038 to all add nav-row buttons (true for T-037/T-038, not this one). Accepted cost, stated: the icon fades under its own surface, so re-tapping it can't dismiss the list.
  - **D8 — a `NavSurface` and a system sheet are never co-presented, in either direction.** The shipped depth-1 sheet carries `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))`, so bucket-2 chrome *is* tappable while a Hood sheet is up — both orderings are reachable states. Enforced by two calls (`closeHood()` on open, `closePlace()` on leave) and **needs no `DetailRouter` change**: under a presented `.places` the scrim blocks every other route to a depth-1 place modal, so `placeDepth == 1` already means "opened from a row."
  - **D4 — sort is name-ascending**, recency deliberately rejected: `SavedPlacesStore` persists a `Set<String>` with no timestamps, so recency means a versioned payload + migration on shipped, accepted state for an ordering no P0 names.
  - **D5 — the offline banner does not ship.** It could never render in Phase 1 (no network path in this feature) and would describe a sync V1 doesn't perform.
  - **D6 — closed state is read from the `places` column, never a client `MKMapItem` lookup** (a round trip per row inside a scroll, and it would hand a user's personal place list to a third party). Answers a PRD open technical question.
- **`SavedPlacesStore` is not rewritten.** One added read accessor (`savedPlaceIDs`), nothing else; its shipped tests must pass unmodified. Two independent stores with one write path each is what makes the PRD's "a manual save never destroys the record that a dwell happened" structural rather than disciplined.
- **§9 verification table, one row per P0 (8 rows), per L-018** — and it names what is **not** covered rather than passing it by construction: req 3's 20-minute threshold and known-place guard (no detector exists), req 4's three-element worst-case row (needs T-035's `isTouristTrap`) and the freshness of the closed column itself (T-044, still ownerless), and **req 5's Been/Visited bullets, which in Phase 1 are checked as the absence of any sensor code path, not as runtime behaviour** — the fixture populates regardless of permission, so read literally the requirement isn't observably met in Phase 1. Stated openly and flagged for `product` rather than papered over.
- **Evidence:** every claim derived from source, not from prose — `Place.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, `PlacesAPI.swift`, `PlacesCache.swift`, `SavedPlacesStore.swift`, `PlaceLayer.swift`, `PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `DetailRouter.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `PlaceCategory.swift`, `Resources/places-tel-aviv.json` (9 places, `permanently_closed` authored on every row and currently all `false`), plus T-042's TRD §3/§4 for the column and the widened select, T-032's TRD §2.3/§4.1/§7/D1 for the chrome layer, `ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1 and `map-rendering-spec.md` §6 (incl. its 2026-08-02 dashed-ring addendum)/§7.
- **Left behind:**
  - **`MapChromeState` is shared with T-032's C1** — the only file the two tasks share. §2.2 pins one contract and says who adds which case in **either** build order, so neither task blocks the other; whoever builds it second must not restructure it.
  - **Four items flagged for `product` at `trd-review`:** D4 (sort), D5 (no offline banner — a deliberate non-build of a specced element), D7 (button placement + the fade consequence), and §9 row 5's honest req-5 framing.
  - **One cross-check for T-042's reviewers:** this task widens `PlacesAPI`'s `select=` to add `permanently_closed` **and only that column** — confirm the split matches T-042's own amendment table (one field per reading task).
  - **T-037/Passport is unblocked** — its dependency was this TRD. §7 shapes the `VisitSourcing` seam so Passport reads `VisitedPlacesStore.visits` and needs no change when the real detector replaces the fixture.
  - **T-044 (closed-state refresh) stays ownerless.** The TRD satisfies req 4's *architecture* half (closed state is never copied into a personal record, so the badge follows the column whenever it changes) and explicitly does not claim the *freshness* half.
  - **`PROGRESS.md` has no "Current Snapshot" section**, contrary to `architect.md`'s board protocol, which instructs reading one. Read the recent worklog entries instead. Noting rather than adding a section unilaterally.

Read every file it cites and re-derived each load-bearing claim from source rather than from the TRD's prose.

Cross-checked against `database/migrations/001_hoods_and_density.sql:54`, `hour_bucket timestamptz not null check (hour_bucket = date_trunc('hour', hour_bucket))` — matches.
  - `Places/PlaceCatalog.swift:16-107,178-249` — the exact `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` / `Source.seed` / injectable-resource-name pattern D10 proposes to copy for `DensitySeed` already exists here, working, tested. D10 is not inventing a new mechanism.

  1. **§4.8's `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 4, coordinateSpace: .named("map"))` references a named coordinate space that is declared nowhere.** No view in §2.1's module layout or §4.8's own contract attaches `.coordinateSpace(.named("map"))` to any ancestor, and grep confirms no such modifier exists anywhere in the codebase today. `EdgeGeometry`'s `band`/`hour(atY:)` are documented as operating in "this view's own coordinates" (§3.3, `EdgeHourZone`'s own `band: ClosedRange<CGFloat>` doc comment) — which is what `DragGesture`'s **default** `coordinateSpace: .local` already gives for free. As written, `ios-developer` at build time either has to invent where to attach the named space (risk: attaching it to `Map` itself, which §2.4 goes out of its way to say the zone is *not* part of) or ships a gesture whose `value.location.y` is measured in an undefined frame relative to the `band` it's compared against — silently wrong, not a crash, exactly the kind of defect this task's own §9 exists to catch but this one row doesn't. Recommend correcting to `coordinateSpace: .local` before C13, or if a shared named space across both edges is genuinely wanted, naming which view declares it and why per-zone `.local` isn't sufficient.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-032/PAS-15 `trd-review`: REQUEST CHANGES — one verification gap in D7's central claim, one minor

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass over `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` v2 against `prds/time-slider/time-slider.md` Draft v7, per this task's own dispatch (this signoff runs alongside, not instead of, `ios-developer`'s). Read the TRD in full (§1–§11), the PRD in full, `BOARD.md`'s T-032 row, and the relevant `PROGRESS.md` history. Cross-checked the TRD's factual claims about shipped code directly against source rather than trusting its citations: `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift` (full file), `Detail/HoodSheet.swift`, `Detail/DetailRouter.swift`, `Density/DensityStore.swift`, `Density/DensityAPI.swift`.
- **TRD conformance, accessibility fallback, security surface — all checked and sound, not just accepted.** (1) The gesture-arbitration reframing in §2.4/§4.8/D7 (sibling overlay above `Map`, not a subtree gesture, so UIKit hit-testing resolves the touch before any MapKit recognizer sees it) is architecturally correct as reasoning and is distinct from T-031's `FB19394663` workaround for the right reason stated. (2) The VoiceOver/Switch Control fallback (§4.11: both `EdgeHint` and `EdgeHourTrack` are `.accessibilityHidden(true)`, the button/modal path carries the entire assistive-tech requirement via a native `Slider`'s discrete stepping) is the correct call and matches PRD req 6's simplified bullet exactly — a gesture-only 24pt edge strip with no visible chrome is not narrated, and does not need to be, because req 6 is satisfied entirely on the other path. (3) The "no touch coordinate is stored, logged, or sent anywhere" / "no new backend calls" claim (§3.1) is confirmed against source, not assumed: `DensityAPI.fetchDensity(from:)` (`DensityAPI.swift:41-69`) takes only `anchorHour`, no location, no device/user id, byte-identical request for every user; `EdgeGeometry`/`EdgeDragState` are pure/local-state only; `DensityStore.selectedHour` is a plain in-memory `Int` (`DensityStore.swift:14`), never written to `UserDefaults`/`AppStorage`. Security/privacy surface is genuinely nil, as claimed.
- **Blocking finding — §9 row 7 / §11 C13 never verify the one claim the whole edge-gesture architecture rests on.** `prds/time-slider/TRD.md:547` (§9 row 7) and `:594` (C13) check camera-immutability only for the *horizontal-dominant, inert* drag (row 7b: "selectedHour and camera both unchanged") and for a pan *starting outside* the 24pt band (row 7c: "map pans normally"). Neither exercises a normal, in-band, **vertical** edge slide — the one gesture this task exists to ship. D7 (`:521-522`) and §2.4 (`:109-120`) assert "there is nothing to win" / "MapKit's pan recognizer is never in that touch's recognizer chain" from UIKit hit-test-order reasoning alone, not from confirmed on-device MapKit behavior. That reasoning is asserted as settled fact, unlike the structurally similar and more honestly-hedged claim two sections later in the same document ("Should hold by construction... **confirm it, do not assume it**," §4.8 `:335`, about the tap-during-drag case) — the TRD applies epistemic caution to a secondary risk and skips it for the primary one. The TRD's own citation of `FB19394663` — a real, filed Apple Feedback report that SwiftUI `Map`'s gesture handling doesn't behave the way plain hit-test/recognizer-chain reasoning predicts — is itself evidence against taking D7's claim on reasoning alone. **Failure scenario:** if MapKit's pan recognizer does receive touches simultaneously under the sibling overlay (matching the flavor of bug the cited FB documents), the map will visibly jump or drift under the user's finger during every ordinary edge slide — a real regression against T-031's shipped camera behavior, on the app's single most-used new gesture. C13 lands, `qa` runs exactly the checks §9 row 7 lists, all pass, and the regression ships because no falsifiable row was ever written to catch it — the exact gap-shape L-018 exists to close, missed on the one row it mattered most for. **Fix required before build:** add one explicit sub-check to §9 row 7 and C13's verify-list — a vertical, in-band `EdgeHourZone` drag leaves `camera`/`MKCoordinateRegion` byte-identical throughout (same comparison method row 2c already uses), checked on-device or in a UI test, not just reasoned about.
- **Minor, non-blocking — §9 row 7d / C13's sheet-interaction check doesn't account for `presentationBackgroundInteraction`'s detent split.** `MapScreen.swift:186` sets `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` on the Hood/place sheet — the exposed map is background-interactive at `.medium` but not at `.large`. `EdgeAvailability.liveEdges` (§4.10) collapses this to one flat rule ("any sheet presented → `[]`"), which is the right call per Q6, but the resulting fallback behavior PRD req 7 describes ("an edge drag over a presented sheet moves the sheet, as it does today") is not actually one behavior — it differs by detent and by whether the touch lands on sheet content or the still-exposed map. Recommend the row 7d / C13 check exercise both detents explicitly rather than one undifferentiated "with `HoodStubSheet` up" pass, since "as it does today" is standing in for two different `.medium`/`.large` behaviors.
- **Everything else checked and found sound, no further findings:** §11's build breakdown gives falsifiable, non-"looks right" pass conditions for every other P0 row; the Q3/Q7/Q8 ratifications are built as structural facts rather than conventions (Q3 in particular — one storage location, not a convention); D9's landscape exclusion and D10's Build-Phase-1 seed are both correctly flagged for `product` rather than decided unilaterally; the mid-`await` guard on `refreshIfHourRolled()` (§4.5 item 3) correctly anticipates the exact race it names — a same-drag clobber from the new edge-touch-down call site — and is properly scoped (only reachable across an actual hour-roll, not on every touch-down).
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** One blocking finding (§9 row 7 / C13 verification gap on D7's central claim), one minor (sheet-detent granularity). Both are additions to existing table rows, not a redesign — cheap for `architect` to fix and resubmit. Everything else in the TRD is sound: correctly scoped, correctly cross-checked against shipped code, correctly flags its own open architect calls for `product`/`designer` rather than deciding them unilaterally.
- **Left behind:** for `ios-developer`'s own independent `trd-review` signoff (this task's gate requires both) — if `ios-developer` reaches APPROVE without touching the D7-verification gap, the two verdicts disagree and `chief-of-staff` should route back to `architect` on this review's finding regardless, since `trd-review` requires both signoffs and this one is REQUEST CHANGES.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). No other files changed; did not touch `BOARD.md`'s state per this task's own instruction (`chief-of-staff` owns that transition). Committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-032/PAS-15 TRD v2 delivered: edge-gesture architecture, gesture-construction and inset calls settled, Build-Phase-1 data gap found

- **Did:** rewrote `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` to **v2**. v1 (2026-07-30/31) designed one entry path — a heat button opening a modal card around a horizontal `Slider`. The PRD has since added a structurally different second path (req 7, edge drag) and Aviran has ratified Q3/Q7/Q8, so v2 keeps every v1 decision that survives (D1–D6 stand) and adds the edge architecture (§2.4, §4.8–§4.11, D7–D9), the Build-Phase-1 seed (§3.4, D10), and a §9 verification table with one row per P0 (per L-018). **iOS-only, re-verified not assumed** against the shipped `DensityAPI.fetchDensity(from:)` and the `hood_density` contract — no `[Backend]`, no `[Algo/Data]` step; C1–C14 all **[iOS]**.
- **Settled design §8 item 6 (gesture construction) — D7, and it changed the reasoning, not just the answer.** The design spec, the PRD's own open question, and v1's feasibility read all framed the 24pt zone as needing to "win gesture-initiation against MapKit's own pan recognizer," citing T-031's FB19394663 workaround. That framing doesn't apply: FB19394663 is about a gesture attached to `Map`'s *own subtree*, whereas the capture zone is a **sibling overlay drawn above the map**, and UIKit hit-testing resolves the touch to it before any recognizer arbitration happens — MapKit's pan recognizer is never in that touch's chain. So there is nothing to win, and the `UIGestureRecognizer` option loses its only real advantage (SwiftUI's `Map` exposes no `MKMapView` and no recognizers, so `require(toFail:)` against MapKit is unreachable in *either* construction). Call: plain SwiftUI `DragGesture` on a `Color.clear.contentShape(Rectangle())` overlay. **Q7 is explicitly not reopened** — the 24pt number and the acquisition argument Aviran ratified are untouched; only its stated mechanism is corrected. Two costs now stated rather than hidden: the strip is permanently undraggable/untappable map (~12% of an iPhone's width across both edges), and a horizontal-dominant drag starting inside it is consumed without panning — the vertical-dominance latch guarantees *no false hour change*, which is the checkable promise, but the touch cannot be handed back in any available construction.
- **Settled design §8 item 7 (insets) — D8. The design's numbers are right; the construction wasn't.** Checked against real geometry rather than accepted: 64 = 59pt (largest current top safe-area inset, Dynamic Island iPhone 14 Pro family onward) + 5pt clearance; 40 = 34pt (home-indicator inset, every Face ID iPhone) + 6pt. Both exactly correct for the worst current case — which is also why hardcoding them is silently wrong on a future device with a larger inset. `EdgeGeometry.band(in:safeArea:)` now computes `max(floor, safeAreaInset + clearance)`: identical output on every current device (the design's 708pt band and ~59pt-per-stop arithmetic on an 812pt reference device is preserved exactly), automatically correct beyond them. Smallest current portrait iPhone (SE, 667pt) still gives ~47pt per stop.
- **Found and closed a gap no upstream doc raised — D9, landscape.** The app ships all three orientations (`passenger-code` `project.pbxproj:367`); every design figure reasons from an 812pt *portrait* device. In landscape the sensor housing sits on a long screen edge (59pt horizontal safe-area inset on Dynamic Island devices), so the capture zone would sit under the housing — which voids the "an edge-anchored target can't be overshot past the bezel" argument that is the entire basis of Q7's ratification — and the band drops to ~24pt per stop. Call: **edge path portrait-only on iPhone**, button path unaffected (req 6 already guarantees all 13 hours through it). Made cheap to overturn on purpose: one row in one pure function (`EdgeAvailability.liveEdges`), checkable by `qa` with a rotation.
- **Found the real Build-Phase-1 blocker for this feature — D10.** Read `Density/DensityStore.swift:56-72` directly: `load()` has exactly three outcomes — live fetch → `DensityCache` → `.unavailable`. **There is no seed path**, unlike `PlaceCatalog`. In Build Phase 1 there's no `SupabaseConfig.plist`, so `DensityAPI` throws `.unconfigured`, the cache is empty on a fresh install, `band(for:hour:)` returns `nil` everywhere, and `HoodLayer.fillColor` is `.clear` at all 13 positions. Shipped as-is, **T-032 would be a control that visibly does nothing**, and three of PRD req 2's four bullets would have no observable to check — including the two `product` carried forward untested from T-031's own acceptance and assigned to this task. Folded a bundled seed into this task's build scope (C10) rather than reopening `done` T-031: same `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` constant, same `Source` enum shape, same injectable resource seam as `PlaceCatalog`. Stored as **relative** offsets (`bands: [13 entries]`, `null` = no row) synthesised against the live `anchorHour` at load, because absolute ISO timestamps in a bundled file are stale the day after authoring; it flows through the real `DensitySnapshot` epoch-hour keying, not a parallel path. Authoring rule is stated as a *requirement with its own test* (≥3 Hoods changing band across ≥4 adjacent hour pairs, ≥1 `null` hour) so C6's differs-across-hours test and `qa`'s perceptual check can't pass vacuously. Precedent is exact: `BOARD.md`'s own Phase-1 definition names the 12h time slider as a thing fake data exists to demo, T-034's PRD took the same call, T-033 shipped the pattern.
- **§9 verification table added — one row per P0, seven rows, each with observable + pass condition + layer + build step.** Written because L-018 exists and because two of these bullets have now survived a full feature cycle untested. No row's pass condition is "looks right"; the greyscale check for req 5's shape-not-colour rule and the full input-matrix test for `EdgeAvailability` are the two that would otherwise have been prose.
- **Ratified questions treated as settled requirements, not open ones.** Q3 made structural rather than conventional: there is exactly one storage location for the hour in the app (`DensityStore.selectedHour`), so "one shared value" is a fact about the type and `ios-code-reviewer` gets a blocking finding for any second stored hour. Q7's 24pt and Q8's persistent hint (5pt × 56pt, opacity 1.0, opaque `Surface`, non-hit-testing, `.accessibilityHidden(true)`) built as specced. Heat glyph `flame.fill` adopted from `designer`'s 2026-08-02 pin, closing the gap v1's **D6** flagged — D6's remaining `[ASSUMPTION]` (background/material/spacing follow `NearMeButton`'s floating-chrome idiom) is unchanged and still cheap to overturn.
- **Evidence:** all decisions checked against source, not against the docs describing it — `DensityStore.swift`, `DensityAPI.swift`, `AppConfig.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `HoodLayer.swift`, `PlaceLayer.swift` (confirmed `HoodLayer` is the only hour-bound layer today — `PlaceLayer` takes no band and no hour, so "every hour-bound layer" is a set of one), `NearMeButton.swift`, `ColdOpenSignpost.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, and `project.pbxproj`'s orientation key. Confirmed no `EdgeHour/`, `HeatModal/`, `MapChromeState` or `MapNavRow` code exists yet, so nothing in v1 has been built against and the rewrite costs no rework.
- **Left behind — three architect calls that touch scope, flagged for `product`/`designer` at `trd-review` rather than presented as settled:** (1) **D10's seed** adds a bundled data artifact to a PRD whose Technical design says "nothing to source or author" — a Build-Phase-1 sequencing consequence, not a scope change to the control, but `product` should confirm it rather than discover it, and confirm that Phase-2 acceptance re-runs req 2's data-dependent bullets against the live feed (§7 says so explicitly; Phase-1 acceptance covers the seed only and this TRD doesn't claim otherwise). (2) **D9's landscape exclusion.** (3) **D7's two accepted costs.** Also carried forward untouched: the non-whole-hour-timezone `[ASSUMPTION]` (§3.2, cosmetic, P1 surface, outside V1's city), req 2's "any open sheet is unchanged" being satisfied structurally rather than behaviourally (D4/Q6 — flagged again, not quietly passed; cross-check against T-033's TRD at review), and D6's per-icon visual `[ASSUMPTION]`. `qa` builds `TEST-PLAN.md` from §9 and must show a real case for both carried-forward req-2 bullets, not a "PASS by construction" note.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/time-slider/TRD.md`, `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-032 rows ×2), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit paths, `git diff --staged` re-read before committing per rule 2 (concurrent sessions have been writing to both shared files today). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9. **`prds/time-slider/TRD.html` is now stale** against this rewrite — not regenerated here (no generator is specified for it and it isn't mine to invent), flagged for whoever owns that artifact.

### 2026-08-02 — retrospective — nightly run, covering the missed nights of 07-31 and 08-01: 6 lessons (L-020…L-025), 4 applied + L-018 upgraded, 1 promoted to `verified`

- **Ran late and wide, deliberately.** 08-02 read as a zero-activity day at 19:04 (no commits since midnight, no Linear issue updated today) and the old step 0 would have short-circuited on it. It didn't, because `LESSONS.md`'s newest header was **2026-07-30** — the nightly retro was marked `lastRunAt 2026-08-01T20:58:59Z` and produced **nothing** on either 07-31 or 08-01, the two busiest days on record (106 `passenger-brain` + 11 `passenger-code` commits, T-031 accepted, T-033/T-040 through the full gate cascade twice). Three output surfaces silent: no `LESSONS.md` section, no `PROGRESS.md` retro entry, no Retro Log comment. That is **L-020**, and the fix is in `retrospective.md`'s step 0 — the window now starts at `LESSONS.md`'s newest header, not at last midnight, so a missed night self-heals on the next run.
- **Biggest applied fix — L-021, a class-wide sweep of stale environment facts in agent files.** Five agent files still routed pushes to `origin brain`, a branch that has never existed in this workspace (`code-reviewer`, `developer` ×2, `data-engineer`, `project-manager` — the last one teaching a workaround for it, `git push origin HEAD:brain`). `retrospective.md` carried the opposite stale claim ("no git remote, `git remote -v` is empty"), false since 07-31 and written by the retro itself. `project-manager.md` still gave the house PRD-link format as `github.com/AviranGrisaro/**locali**/blob/**brain**/…`. And every agent file's "commit + push … same turn" has contradicted `CLAUDE.md` rule 9 since 07-31 — `chief-of-staff` flagged that contradiction in its own 08-01 entry and it was still live tonight. **16 agent files touched**, all `brain`-branch references removed, every push instruction replaced with "commit … never push — Aviran-gated, rule 9 — report the hash". `project-manager`'s check F was rewritten (as written it would have flagged every commit in the repo as a defect) and now reports the unpushed backlog as a dated number and **treats any file asserting a remote/branch that git disagrees with as itself a hit, reported for `retrospective` to sweep** — so this drift is self-reporting from now on rather than waiting for a retro to `grep` for it.
- **L-018 upgraded `logged` → `applied`** — its watch item triggered. `prds/hood-place-detail/TEST-PLAN.md` was created in `671d6c2` (08-01 00:33, `qa`'s first behavioral pass) while the TRD landed `2455ff4` (07-31 00:00) and passed six `trd-review` verdicts with no plan in existence — second feature in a row. Cost: `product`'s acceptance REJECT, "two findings … neither of which any earlier gate had a criterion to fail," one extra fix round and a third QA round. The existing `qa.md` rule to write the plan at TRD time could never have fired — **`qa` isn't running at `trd`**. So the obligation moved to an agent that is: `architect.md` gains TRD **§9 Verification**, one row per P0 requirement naming the falsifiable check (observable, pass condition, layer), with an instruction to bounce to `product` where no pass condition can be stated. `qa.md` now builds `TEST-PLAN.md` from that table and raises a missing §9 as a hit. Build breakdown renumbered 9→10; no other file referenced the old numbering (checked).
- **Two rules amended in place in `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`** rather than added, to hold the size budget: rule **7** (now "Name the repo, always") — a commit hash is never written alone, after a `qa` dispatch pinned `a5351ed` as a `passenger-code` commit when it was a `passenger-brain` one (**L-022**, caught only because `qa` verified the checkout itself); rule **2** — an explicit path isn't enough for a shared file, re-read `git diff --staged` and confirm every hunk is yours, after `0dd3d21` staged `BOARD.md` by explicit path and carried another session's in-flight T-032 row verbatim (**L-023**, self-reported by `product`).
- **Logged, not applied:** **L-024** — the `florentin` blurb hand-edited into the *generated* `hoods-tel-aviv.json` survived code-review and two QA rounds because every gate read the artifact and the artifact was correct; only `product` re-running `build_hoods.py` to a scratch path found it. **L-025** — today's relayed "Aviran accepts T-033/T-040" refusal, which escalated with an authority claim and urgency when refused and still held. Both are behaving-correctly cases where a rule would codify what's already universal; both carry explicit watch items.
- **Verification (step 4):** **L-009 promoted `applied` → `verified`** (8 days, cited by name at 08-01's acceptance). L-017 holding with hard evidence — `diff -qr` across all three agent copies was clean before tonight's edits, against a baseline of 13 drifted copies on 07-30, and the orphaned mirrors were cleared via T-045 as the rule's routing half intended. L-015's primary fix visibly working ("Committed, not pushed (`PAS-21`)" in nearly every entry); its doc-cleanup half was what failed, which is L-021. L-007 exercised three times on 07-31 and detected every time. L-010 had no occasion to fire and was **not** promoted on that basis.
- **Left behind / for other owners:** (1) **No `PM Nightly Log` issue exists** in the Passenger team — `project-manager.md` and `retrospective.md` both name it as where the nightly digest goes. `retrospective.md` now has a fallback; creating the issue is `project-manager`'s call, not mine. (2) `agent-os/agents-mirror/chief.md` is an **extra file with no live counterpart** — it's the `/chief` slash command (`description` + `argument-hint` front-matter), mis-filed into the agents mirror by `7657529`. Not deleted (never `rm`); it belongs to `project-manager`'s check G. (3) `chief-of-staff` has a founder-direct **pipeline pivot in flight this session** (pre-code design gate removed) that will edit agent files — re-run the three-way `diff -qr` after it lands, since I synced all 16 mirrors at 19:30. Tonight's `architect.md` §9 change sits at `trd` and does not touch the design gate, so the two don't conflict.
- **Needs Aviran (2, flagged not applied):** a liveness alert on scheduled tasks that notices "enabled job, `lastRunAt` advanced, no artifact produced" — tonight's fix makes the *next* retro self-heal but cannot catch a run that dies before step 0; and dispatching `qa` at `trd` so the test plan exists before `build` (gate-order change, his call — §9 gets the content there without moving anyone's stage).
- **`chief-of-staff.md` deliberately excluded from this commit — rule 2 / L-023, observed live for the second time in three days.** I swept and mirrored all 16 agent files at 19:30; between then and commit, the concurrent `chief-of-staff` session edited the live file with its pipeline-pivot changes (design gate retired). Live now holds **both** its pivot and my push-instruction fix (verified: `chief-of-staff.md:34` and `:80` still carry the rule-9 wording) — but my *mirror* copies held only my half. Copying live→mirror would have swept its in-flight work into a retro commit, which is exactly the `0dd3d21` failure L-023 was written about tonight; leaving the half-state uncommitted would have orphaned it, which is L-017. So both mirror copies were restored to `HEAD` and the file is untouched by this commit. **Owner: the in-flight `chief-of-staff` session** — its own mirror-sync will carry both changes, since live already has both. Re-run `diff -qr` across all three copies once its pivot lands.
- **Git:** committed `CLAUDE.md`, `agent-os/LESSONS.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/` (15 explicit files, `chief-of-staff.md` excluded per above) in `passenger-brain`; `.claude/agents/` (same 15 explicit files) in `passenger-code`. Explicit paths only; `git diff --staged` re-read before committing per tonight's own rule-2 amendment, since `chief-of-staff` was writing to `PROGRESS.md` in the same window. **Committed, not pushed** — pushing is Aviran-gated (`PAS-21`).

### 2026-08-02 — designer — T-035/PAS-26 design spec + mockup: ready for `design-approval`

- **Did:** wrote `design/phase-1/tourist-trap-flag-design.md` covering exactly the four surfaces the PRD hands off — the Hood outline flag stroke (flagged/not-flagged/busy+flagged, by zoom tier), the Hood centroid label wording, the place-detail-modal flag line (fills `PlaceDetailModal.swift`'s `touristTrapSlot`, currently a bare `EmptyView()` reserved for this task), and the local-QA toast (binary Yes/No, top-anchored, non-blocking). Built directly against the real shipped code, not an idealized version of it: `Hood.swift` already carries `isTouristTrap: Bool?` (T-040's Phase-1 carve-out), `HoodLayer.swift`'s stroke is currently one constant value for every Hood regardless of flag — this spec is what makes it vary — and all 24 bundled Hoods currently ship `isTouristTrap: null` (checked directly against `hoods-tel-aviv.json`), the real cold-start state, not a bug.
- **Mockup:** interactive HTML/CSS/JS, published as a Claude Artifact — https://claude.ai/code/artifact/40b9ec94-b831-4d20-a334-fb95bf5f4cbd. Three tabs (Map/Hood stroke, Place modal, Toast), each demonstrating the real component logic (a zoom-tier control driving PRD req 3's exact table; a flagged/not-flagged toggle on the place modal; six toast scenarios including offline, notifications-denied, and already-answered) rather than static screenshots. Mobbin research unavailable (connector needs authorization, same standing block T-031/T-033 hit) — proceeded from the PRD and the two locked reference docs per the standing "don't block" rule. Figma not attempted — task didn't request it, HTML artifact is the default per the 2026-07-22 founder ruling.
- **A real finding, caught and fixed in the same pass, not deferred:** `map-rendering-spec.md` §7's own quoted VoiceOver example — *"Florentin, busy, not a tourist trap"* — contains the literal phrase decision #42 and the PRD's own req 1 bullet 3 ban from all user-facing copy. VoiceOver output is spoken to the user, so it's user-facing, not internal vocabulary. That doc predates both the decision and the requirement and wasn't swept when they landed. Didn't edit that already-locked shared doc directly (not this task's file to rewrite outright) — instead defined the actual compliant strings this spec uses ("Florentin, busy, not a tourist-heavy spot") and flagged the stale example for whoever next has write reason to touch `map-rendering-spec.md` (§8 item 6 of the new spec). Second real finding: the first pass of this spec gated the flag's centroid label on the same zoom threshold as the Hood name/pins (`showsNames`), which can't satisfy PRD req 3's literal "close... drops the label" — caught by checking the requirement's exact text against the component table, fixed by naming the actual gap (a second, coarser threshold needed, flagged for `ios-developer` rather than invented here).
- **`ui-design-review` pass run and documented (§2.3 of the spec), not just cited** — checked contrast numerically (new `--flag` accent: 4.93:1/5.19:1 light, 9.97:1/9.16:1 dark, against both bg and surface tokens, computed via a WCAG relative-luminance script, not asserted), checked the flagged-vs-busy+flagged stroke distinction against the mockup's actual CSS (one `if/else`, never both stroke rules firing at once), and weighed two quick wins explicitly rejected (a toast countdown affordance, a second warning hue) with reasoning rather than silently omitting them.
- **Artifact conformance checked both directions (§2.4)** before submission: grepped the published mockup's actual source for the banned phrase (zero hits outside this spec's own prose), confirmed every rendered DOM element inside the phone frame has a matching component/state row, and confirmed the toast's countdown bar is labelled reviewer-only instrumentation rather than left ambiguous as shipped UI.
- **Every [ASSUMPTION] and open PRD question carried forward untouched, not resolved here** (§9 of the spec) — cold-start risk, notification-denied coverage gap, reward-for-answering gap, the not-flagged-place-modal-line call, the single-answer-never-flips-the-flag call. None of the PRD's existing open questions were resolved by this design pass, per the dispatch brief.
- **Left behind:** ready for `design-approval` — product and chief-of-staff sign off next, per the lifecycle. Real open items for the TRD (§8 of the spec): the new zoom threshold for the flag label, adding `Place.isTouristTrap` to the `Place` struct (mirroring `Hood`'s existing field), seeding 2–3 demo Hoods/places with real true/false values so the Phase-1 demo can actually show the flagged states (not just the current all-null fixture), the toast's ~5s auto-dismiss timing (this spec's working default, not a PRD number), and the toast's VoiceOver announcement mechanism (new ground — no comparable passively-arriving surface exists yet in T-031/T-033 to follow as precedent).
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `design/phase-1/tourist-trap-flag-design.md` (new), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-035 row), explicit paths. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, per rule 9.

### 2026-08-01 — product — T-033/PAS-13 + T-040/PAS-17 `acceptance` verdict, 2nd attempt: **ACCEPT** — both REJECT findings independently re-verified against source; req 4 carried unmet

- **Method:** re-derived both findings from source at `passenger-code` `165fd7f`, not from `qa`'s round-3 PASS or `ios-code-reviewer`'s APPROVE. Read `MapScreen.swift` in full, `HoodHitTester.swift`, `PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `DetailSheetInteractionTests.swift`, all three data artifacts, and the full `291c010..165fd7f` source diff. Every claim below is something I ran or read myself.
- **Finding 1 (T-033) — CLOSED. Req 1 bullet 4: PASS. Req 3 bullet 4: PASS.** `MapScreen.swift:273` now reads `if showsNames, let place = placeHitTester.place(at: tapPoint, tolerance: tolerance)`. Three things make this genuinely satisfy the bullets rather than merely look right: (a) `showsNames` is one `@State` flag set in one place (`onMapCameraChange`, line 110) and read by both the pin `ForEach` (line ~95) and now the hit test — there is no second threshold that can drift; (b) Swift's comma-conjunction short-circuits, so an ungated camera skips the `PlaceHitTester` call entirely and falls into the `else if` Hood branch, unchanged; (c) `HoodHitTester.hood(at:tolerance:)` runs **containment before tolerance** (pass 1 ray-casts, pass 2 only runs on a containment miss), so a tap inside Florentin resolves to Florentin and not to a nearer-edged neighbour — which is what req 1 bullet 4's "opens **that Hood's** sheet" actually asks, beyond just "some Hood".
- **The tolerance concern I raised in the REJECT is structurally answered, not just gated.** `mapPointTolerance(forScreenPoints: 22, …)` converts 22 *screen* points through the live `MapProxy` per tap, so the hit radius is visually constant at every zoom — the ~700m figure from my first pass was 22 points expressed in metres at the city-wide camera, not a 700m finger. With the gate closed below `nameLabelSpanThreshold`, the only reachable place taps are within a half-finger of a drawn pin. That is req 3 bullet 4's "visible on screen at the moment of the tap" met in substance, not by coincidence.
- **The test rework is real coverage, not a green checkmark.** `testTappingPopulatedHoodAtColdOpenOpensHoodSheet` taps the *same* coordinate the old buggy test used (`kerem-suzana-yemenite-kitchen`, 32.068407/34.76525) and asserts both that `hoodSheetTitle == "Kerem HaTeimanim"` **and** that `placeDetailTitle` does not exist — an assertion that could not have passed under the old code. The prior file's own header documented the defect as expected behaviour; that header is gone and replaced with one explaining the fixed contract. **Test backdoor checked myself, not taken on the reviewer's word:** `grep` across all `.swift`/`.xcscheme`/`.plist`/`.pbxproj` finds `-uiTestZoomedIn` in exactly two files — `MapScreen.initialCameraRegion`'s guard and the UI test's `launchArguments`. No scheme sets any `Argument` at all. A shipped launch always gets `telAvivCityWide`.
- **Finding 2 (T-040) — CLOSED. Req 6 bullets 3 and 4: PASS.** Verified by **re-running the generator**, which is the only check that actually proves "never hand-editing either artifact" — a surviving hand-edit anywhere shows up as a diff. `build_hoods.py --migration-number 006` to a scratch path: `006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` comes back **byte-identical** (`diff` exit 0); the iOS bundle comes back byte-identical apart from the `generatedAt` run-stamp. Also ran my own 3-way parity script across the authored source, the migration SQL and the bundle: 24/24 ids match, every polygon matches, and `blurb`/`isTouristTrap`/`designatedForProgression` match on every row. `florentin`'s `[PROVISIONAL]` string is present and identical in all three (1 occurrence in the SQL); the stale `_note` hand-edit disclosure is gone from the bundle. Generator run itself clean — 0 errors, 0 warnings, 24 hoods.
- **Req 5 bullet 3 (route hand-off round trip) — was a carried gap at the first pass, now PASS.** Confirmed at source that `PlaceDetailModal.routeButton` calls `router.closeHood()` *before* `directionsService.open(...)`, clearing both depths regardless of where the modal was opened from; `qa` round 3 exercised the full Apple-Maps-and-back trip and observed the map with no sheet open on return. Source and behaviour agree.
- **All other requirements: PASS, unchanged from the first pass and structurally unable to have regressed.** The entire source delta since my REJECT is `handleTap`'s one added condition, the `initialCameraRegion` test override, the reworked UI test file, and the regenerated bundle — nothing else in the app was touched. Reqs 2/4/6/7 of `hood-place-detail` and reqs 1/2/3/5/7 of `hood-dataset` stand as verified at `291c010`.
- **`hood-dataset` req 4 remains unmet and carried, not waived** — relocating the `[PROVISIONAL]` blurb fixed a *pipeline* violation, not the sourcing gap. 23 of 24 Hoods still ship `blurb: null` and `florentin` still ships a placeholder. Written into the PRD's Status line so it can't be lost, with the hard condition intact: no build seen outside the team ships a `[PROVISIONAL]` blurb. This is the item for Aviran's attention at `aviran-review`.
- **Two non-blocking observations, recorded not chased:** (1) the `generatedAt` run-stamp means the iOS bundle is never byte-reproducible across runs — my own req 6 bullet 4 said "must not change any shipped artifact," which was not mechanically checkable as written, so I tightened it in the same commit to name the stamp as the sole permitted diff and to state the `diff`-against-scratch pass condition (L-009 discipline applied to a bullet I wrote myself, not only to build defects). (2) A test-only camera override now lives in shipping code; verified inert, and the guard is the standard XCUITest pattern, but it is worth someone noticing if a second one ever appears.
- **Why ACCEPT and not CONDITIONAL:** every bullet my own REJECT added is satisfied by a mechanism I re-derived, not by a verdict I trusted. The one unmet P0 (`hood-dataset` req 4) is a content-sourcing deliverable no rejection loop can close — the same shape as T-031's accepted-with-gaps precedent, and it is now recorded in the PRD's Status line rather than buried in a bullet.
- **Files:** `prds/hood-place-detail/hood-place-detail.md` (Status → Accepted, Decisions row), `prds/hood-dataset/hood-dataset.md` (Status → Accepted with req 4 carried, req 6 bullet 4 clarified, Decisions row), this entry. Status flips ship in the same commit as the verdict per L-006.
- **Left behind:** both tasks route to `aviran-review`, not `done`. Carried gaps for that gate: `hood-dataset` req 4's placeholder blurb; Hood fills still render `.clear` because `DensityStore` is live-fetch-only, so T-040's non-overlap and coverage bullets stay visually unobserved until Build Phase 2; save-state across relaunch structurally sound but unexercised; req 7's permanently-closed-save satisfied by construction only, with no closed place in the fixture to falsify it against.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md`, Linear, or `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`, per the dispatch brief. Did not stage other sessions' in-flight files (`.claude/agents/marketing.md` is modified in `passenger-code` and belongs to another session — left untouched). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated.

Swift short-circuits the `&&`-style conjunction left to right, so `showsNames == false` skips the `PlaceHitTester` call entirely (not just discards its result) and falls straight into the `else if` Hood branch, unchanged from before this fix. `showsNames` is set at `MapScreen.swift:110` (`context.region.span.latitudeDelta < nameLabelSpanThreshold`), the identical predicate gating the pin `ForEach` at line ~95 — one flag, two consumers, no drift possible between what's drawn and what's tappable. Confirmed correct.
- **Check 2 — `-uiTestZoomedIn` backdoor, verified inert on a real launch, not assumed:** `initialCameraRegion` (`MapScreen.swift:29-39`) guards on `ProcessInfo.processInfo.arguments.contains("-uiTestZoomedIn")`, returning `telAvivCityWide` unless present. Grepped every `.xcscheme` in the project for `LaunchArguments`/`Argument` — none set this (or any) argument at the scheme level, so it activates only via `XCUIApplication.launchArguments`, which only `DetailSheetInteractionTests`' zoomed-in test sets. No URL scheme, environment variable, or build-config path can set a process launch argument on a real (App Store or TestFlight) launch — this is the standard, safe pattern for XCUITest-only camera overrides. Confirmed inert.
- **Check 3 — cold-open tap coordinate, re-derived independently rather than trusted (this exact claim was wrong once already in this saga):** hand-derived lat/lon math off `telAvivCityWide`'s region and the test's normalized offset (`dx:0.376, dy:0.560`) placed the tap point ~940m from `kerem-suzana-yemenite-kitchen`'s coordinate and **outside** `kerem-hateimanim`'s polygon in a naive equal-aspect projection — which would have been a REQUEST CHANGES finding. But hand math can't account for MapKit's region-to-aspect-ratio fitting (a non-square device viewport stretches a square-delta `MKCoordinateRegion` on-screen), so rather than trust either the developer's claim or my own arithmetic, ran it for real: cleared `DerivedData`, ran `xcodebuild test` against booted `iPhone 17 Pro` (iOS 26.5), one unscoped invocation. `testTappingPopulatedHoodAtColdOpenOpensHoodSheet` passed — log shows the synthesized tap at `[0.38, 0.56]` on the real `Map` element, followed by `hoodSheetTitle` appearing and `placeDetailTitle` confirmed absent. This is empirical, not re-derived-and-trusted math; the tapped coordinate is genuinely inside `kerem-hateimanim` at the real cold-open camera.
- **Check 4 — blurb regeneration, diffed myself:** `git show 37c402f` touches exactly one file, 3 lines (1 insertion, 2 deletions) — `generatedAt` timestamp updated, the stale `_note` hand-edit-disclosure field removed. `florentin.blurb` is byte-identical before/after and matches `passenger-brain/database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json`'s `florentin.blurb` exactly (diffed both with a script, not eyeballed). All 24 hood ids/geometry untouched per the diff stat alone (only 3 lines changed in a multi-hundred-line file). Confirmed clean.
- **Check 5 — full suite, real clean build:** `rm -rf DerivedData`, `xcodebuild test` unscoped. **83/83 green**: Swift Testing run reports "Test run with 80 tests in 15 suites passed"; XCUITest run reports 3/3 (`ColdOpenPerformanceTests` + both reworked `DetailSheetInteractionTests` cases), 0 failures. Matches the ios-developer's claimed count exactly, verified independently rather than taken on the commit message's word.
- **No findings.** Both diffs are exactly what their commit messages describe, no more and no less. Code comments on both the gate and the launch-argument mechanism explain the reasoning in place, matching this agent's readability bar.
- **Verdict: APPROVE.** Routing to `qa` for the third round — worth `qa` specifically re-confirming case 5.4 (the route-hand-off round trip, `product`'s non-blocking recommendation from the REJECT) since neither fix here touches it and it's still open per the prior QA PASS.
- **Left behind:** nothing blocking. `chief-of-staff` owns dispatching `qa` next per the lifecycle.
- **Git:** committed `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (explicit path) in `passenger-brain`. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated.

### 2026-08-01 — ios-developer — T-033/PAS-13 acceptance fix: `handleTap`'s place branch gated on `showsNames`; `DetailSheetInteractionTests` reworked to test the fixed contract instead of the bug

- **Ask:** exactly `product`'s Finding 1 from the `acceptance` REJECT (`7e388e7`) — no broader rework. `MapScreen.swift`'s `handleTap` (~line 237) called `PlaceHitTester.place(at:tolerance:)` unconditionally, with no `showsNames` guard, while the pin-rendering `ForEach` (line 73) was correctly gated. At the cold-open `telAvivCityWide` span (0.14) the live-computed tap tolerance is ~700m — every point inside Florentin's or Kerem HaTeimanim's real Hood polygon falls within tolerance of one of their own bundled places, so a tap anywhere in a populated Hood at cold open silently opened a place modal for a pin nobody could see.
- **Fix, confirmed to match the finding exactly, not a broader rework:** added one `showsNames` guard to `handleTap`'s place branch (`if showsNames, let place = placeHitTester.place(...)`), reusing the exact gate `HoodLayer`'s name label and the pin `ForEach` already use — no new threshold invented. A miss on the now-gated place branch falls through to the existing Hood branch unchanged, which is what makes PRD req 1's new bullet ("a tap inside a Hood opens that Hood's sheet") hold automatically rather than needing separate logic. Diffed my own change against `git show 291c010`'s `handleTap` before committing: the only delta is the added `showsNames` condition on one `if` — nothing else in `handleTap`, `mapPointTolerance`, or the Hood branch touched.
- **`DetailSheetInteractionTests.swift` reworked, not patched to keep passing:** the prior version's own header documented the defect as expected behavior (test 1 tapped a place coordinate at cold-open and asserted the modal opened; test 2 picked `old-north` "away from any bundled place" specifically to dodge the bug). Both tests now exercise the fixed contract:
  - `testTappingPopulatedHoodAtColdOpenOpensHoodSheet` — taps `kerem-suzana-yemenite-kitchen`'s exact bundled coordinate (32.068407, 34.76525) at the default cold-open camera and asserts `kerem-hateimanim`'s Hood sheet opens (`hoodSheetTitle` == "Kerem HaTeimanim") **and** that no place modal renders (`placeDetailTitle` does not exist) — this is the new coverage the bug prevented from existing, and it's the same coordinate the old buggy test used, not a coordinate picked to avoid the bug.
  - `testTappingPlacePinOpensPopulatedPlaceDetailModalWhenZoomedIn` — the pin-tap path still needs coverage once the zoom gate is actually open, and nothing in the existing harness could move the camera mid-test (the prior fix pass already ruled out synthesized pinch and `doubleTap()` — both conflict with the `SpatialTapGesture` FB19394663 workaround). Added a `-uiTestZoomedIn` launch argument (`MapScreen.initialCameraRegion`) that sets the *initial* camera, not a mid-test one — starts already zoomed in past `nameLabelSpanThreshold`, centered on the same place — gated so a real launch never carries it. Taps center-screen and asserts the place modal opens with the correct content.
- **Verified before commit, clean build:** deleted `DerivedData`, `xcodebuild test` on `iPhone 17 Pro` (iOS 26.5), one invocation, not scoped. **83 tests green** — 80 unit tests / 15 suites (unchanged — `PassengerTests` untouched by this fix) + 3 UI tests (`ColdOpenPerformanceTests`, unchanged, avg 0.489s within budget, plus the 2 reworked `DetailSheetInteractionTests`). Both new assertions confirmed for the reason stated, read directly from the test log rather than assumed from a green checkmark: the Hood test's `hoodSheetTitle` label is exactly "Kerem HaTeimanim" and `placeDetailTitle` does not exist at that point; the zoomed-in test's `placeDetailTitle` label is exactly "Suzana Yemenite Kitchen".
- **Built on `passenger-code` HEAD `37c402f`** (data-engineer's T-040/PAS-17 blurb-relocation fix, already landed when this session started) — the two fixes are independent (one touches `MapScreen.swift`/a UI test, the other touches only the bundled `hoods-tel-aviv.json`), so no merge conflict and no interaction to verify beyond the full green suite already covering both.

Did not touch T-040/Finding 2 (data-engineer's fix, already landed) or `BOARD.md`/Linear, per the dispatch brief. Did not stage `.claude/agents/marketing.md` (another session's in-flight edit, left untouched per safety rule 2).
